## IV Fort Phil Kearney / 
===Original===

Long since the column, pushing north again

With Carrington, had left the little post

On Laramie; unwitting how the ghost

Of many a trooper, lusty yet and gay,

Disconsolately drifting back that way,

Should fill unseen the gaps of shattered ranks.
Scarce moved to know what shadows dogged their flanks,

Till all the winds that blew were talking spies

And draws had ears and every hilltop, eyes,

And silence, tongues, the seven hundred went.

How brazenly their insolent intent

Was flaunted! Even wolves might understand

These men were going forth to wed the land

And spawn their breed therein. Behold their squaws!

Could such defend the Great White Father’s laws?

So weak they were their warriors hewed the wood,

Nor did they tend the pots, as women should,

Nor fill them.
Powder River caught the word

Of how they swam their long-horned cattle herd

At Bridger’s Ferry. Big Horn and the Tongue

Beheld through nearer eyes the long line flung

Up Sage Creek valley; heard through distant ears

The cracking lashes of the muleteers

The day the sandy trail grew steep and bleak.

The Rosebud saw them crossing Lightning Creek,

Whence, southward, cone outsoaring dizzy cone,

Until the last gleamed splendidly alone,

They viewed the peak of Laramie. When, high

Between the head of North Fork and the Dry

They lifted Cloud Peak scintillant with snows,

The Cheyenne hunters and Arapahoes,

Far-flung as where the Wind becomes the Horn,

Discussed their progress. Spirits of the morn,

That watched them break the nightly camp and leave,

Outwinged the crane to gossip with the eve

In distant camps. Beyond the Lodge Pole’s mouth

Relentless Red Cloud, poring on the south,

Could see them where the upper Powder ran

Past Reno Post, and counted to a man

The soldiers left there. Tattlings of the noon

Were bruited by the glimmer of the moon

In lands remote; till, pushing northward yet

Past Crazy Woman’s Fork and Lake DeSmedt,

They reached the Big and Little Piney Creeks.
Some such a land the famished hunter seeks

In fever-dreams of coolness. All day long

The snow-born waters hummed a little song

To virgin meadows, till the sun went under;

Then tardy freshets in a swoon of thunder,

That deepened with the dark, went rushing by,

As ’twere the Night herself sang lullaby

Till morning. Cottonwoods and evergreens

Made music out of what the silence means

===Chinese===

许久以前，随卡林顿再度北上的
纵队已经离开拉勒米
那座小小的哨站；他们不知道，
多少骑兵的鬼魂——此刻还健壮欢快——
将凄凄惶惶地飘回那条路上，
无声无息地填满残破军列的空缺。
他们几乎不去想有什么影子跟在侧翼，
直到所有的风都成了搬弄口舌的暗探，
每条沟壑都长了耳朵、每座山顶都长了眼睛、
连寂静也长了舌头，七百人一路走去。
他们那骄横的意图炫耀得多露骨！
连狼都看得懂：这些人是去
跟这片土地成婚、在它里面繁衍子孙的。
看他们那些娘们！
这样的货色也配维护伟大的白人父亲的法律？
他们弱到要战士们自己去砍柴，
也不像女人该做的那样照看锅灶，
更不往里添柴。
波德河听说了
他们怎样在布里杰渡口泅过长角的牛群。
大角河与汤河借更近的眼睛
看见长长的队列甩上鼠尾草溪的谷地；
借远处的耳朵听见骡夫的鞭子
噼啪炸响的那一天——沙土的路正陡峭荒凉。
玫瑰苞河看见他们渡过闪电溪，
从那里向南，一座锥峰压着一座锥峰地晕眩高耸，
直到最后一座独自灿烂地闪光，
他们望见了拉勒米峰。当他们
在北汊与干河源头之间的高处
举起积雪闪烁的云峰时，
夏延猎人和阿拉帕霍人——
散布到风河变作角河那么远的地方——
已在议论他们的行程。
清晨的精灵
看着他们拔营上路，
振翅赶过鹤群，把闲话带给远营
的黄昏。在帐篷杆溪口之外，
毫不留情的红云凝神南望，
看得见他们沿上波德河行进，
过了雷诺哨站，还把留守的士兵
数得一个不差。正午的私语
由闪烁的月光传到
遥远的土地；直到他们继续北行，
过了疯女汊和德斯梅湖，
抵达大小派尼溪。
饥渴的猎人在清凉的
热病之梦里追寻的，正是这样一片土地。
整整一天，雪生的水
对着处女草甸哼一支小小的歌，直到太阳落下；
随后迟来的山洪在雷声的晕厥里——
雷声随夜色加深——奔涌而过，
仿佛是黑夜亲自唱着摇篮曲
直到天明。三角叶杨和常绿树
把寂静的意味谱成了音乐

===Original===

In timeless solitudes. And over all,

White towers dizzy on a floating wall

Of stainless white, the Big Horn Mountains rose.

Absoraka, the Country of the Crows,

A land men well might fight for!
Here they camped,

Rejoicing, man and beast. The work-mule champed

The forage of the elk, and rolled to sate

His lust for greenness. Like a voice of fate,

Foretelling ruthless years, his blatant bray

With horns of woe and trumpets of dismay

Crowded the hills. The milk cow and the steer

In pastures of the bison and the deer

Lowed softly. And the trail-worn troopers went

About their duties, whistling, well content

To share this earthly paradise of game.
But scarcely were the tents up, when there came﻿—

Was it a sign? One moment it was noon,

A golden peace hypnotic with the tune

Of bugs among the grasses; and the next,

The spacious splendor of the world was vexed

With twilight that estranged familiar things.

A moaning sound, as of enormous wings

Flung wide to bear some swooping bat of death,

Awakened. Hills and valleys held their breath

To hear that sound. A nervous troop-horse neighed

Shrill in the calm. Instinctively afraid,

The cattle bellowed and forgot to graze;

And raucous mules deplored the idle day’s

Untimely end. Then presently there fell

What seemed a burlesque blizzard out of hell﻿—

A snow of locusts﻿—tawny flakes at strife,

That, driven by a gust of rabid life,

Smothered the windless noon! The lush grass bent,

Devoured in bending. Wagon-top and tent

Sagged with the drift of brown corrosive snow.

Innumerable hungers shrilled below;

A humming fog of hungers hid the sky,

Until a cool breath, falling from the high

White ramparts, came to cleanse the stricken world.

Then suddenly the loud rack lifted, swirled

To eastward; and the golden light returned.
Now day by day the prairie people learned

What wonders happened where the Pineys flowed;

How many wagons rutted out a road

To where the pines stood tallest to be slain;

What medicine the White Man’s hand and brain

Had conjured; how they harnessed up a fog

That sent a round knife screaming through a log

From end to end; how many adzes hewed;

And how the desecrated solitude

Beheld upon a level creek-side knoll

The rise of fitted bole on shaven bole,

Until a great fort brazened out the sun.

And while that builded insolence was done,

===Chinese===

——在那无始无终的荒寂里。而在一切之上，
大角山脉拔起，白色的塔群晕眩地立在
一堵漂移的、无瑕的白墙上。
阿布萨罗卡，克劳人的国度，
一片人们完全值得为之战斗的土地！
他们在这里扎营，
人欢畜跃。役骡咀嚼着
廉鹿的牧草，打着滚，餍足
它对绿色的欲望。像一道命运之声，
预言着无情的岁月，它刺耳的嘶叫
带着哀伤的号角和惊惶的喇叭
挤满群山。奶牛和阉牛
在野牛和鹿的牧场上
柔声低哞。而风尘仆仆的骑兵们
到处执行勤务，吹着口哨，心满意足地
分享这座猎物的地上乐园。
可帐篷刚扎好，就来了——
那是征兆吗？前一刻还是正午，
一片金色的安宁，被草间
虫豸的曲调催眠；下一刻，
世界敞朗的壮丽就被
疏远了一切熟悉之物的暮色搅扰。
一阵呻吟般的声音，像巨大的双翼
豁然张开、驮着某只俯冲的死亡蝙蝠，
醒了过来。山峦和谷地屏住呼吸
去听那声音。一匹紧张的战马
在寂静里发出刺耳的嘶鸣。牛群
凭着本能害怕起来，哞叫着忘了吃草；
粗嗓的骡子哀叹这无所事事的一天
竟这样不合时宜地到了头。
随后不一会儿，落下了
一场仿佛出自地狱的滑稽暴风雪——
一场蝗虫之雪——黄褐的雪片彼此倾轧，
被一阵癫狂的生命之风吹赶，
闷死了无风的正午！丰茂的青草弯下腰，
在弯腰中被啃光。篷车顶和帐篷
被棕色的、蚀骨的积雪压得下坠。
无数饥饿在下面尖声嘶叫；
一片嗡嗡的饥饿之雾遮蔽了天空，
直到一股凉气从高高的白色
壁垒上落下，来洗净这受难的世界。
随后那喧闹的云阵忽然抬起，卷向
东方；金色的光回来了。
从此日复一日，草原上的人打听着
派尼溪流淌之处出了什么奇迹；
多少辆篷车碾出一条路，
通到长得最高的松树受死的地方；
白人的手和脑变出了
什么魔法；他们怎样给一团雾套上轭，
叫一把圆刀尖啸着从头到尾
剖开一根圆木；多少锛子刨削；
被亵渎的荒寂怎样
在一座临溪的平整小丘上
眼看光洁的圆木层层垒起，
直到一座大堡赫然顶着太阳。
而就在那垒起来的骄横慢慢成形的时候，

===Original===

Far prairies saw the boasting banner flung

Above it, like a hissing adder’s tongue,

To menace every ancientry of good.
Long since and oft the workers in the wood

Had felt the presence of a foe concealed.

The drone of mowers in the haying-field

Was silenced often by the rifle’s crack,

The arrow’s whirr; and often, forging back

With lash and oath along the logging road,

The scared mule-whacker fought behind his load,

His team a kicking tangle. Oft by night

Some hill top wagged a sudden beard of light,

Immediately shorn; and dark hills saw

To glimmer sentient. Hours of drowsy awe

Near dawn had heard the raided cattle bawl,

Afraid of alien herdsmen; bugles call

To horse; the roaring sally; fleeing cries.

And oft by day upon a distant rise

Some naked rider loomed against the glare

With hand at brow to shade a searching stare,

Then like a dream dissolved in empty sky.
So men and fate had labored through July

To make a story. August browned the plain;

And ever Fort Phil Kearney grew amain

With sweat of toil and blood of petty fights.

September brought the tingling silver nights

And men worked faster, thinking of the snows.

Aye, more than storm they dreaded. Friendly Crows

Had told wild tales. Had they not ridden through

The Powder River gathering of Sioux?

And lo, at one far end the day was young;

Noon saw the other! Up along the Tongue

Big villages were dancing! Everywhere

The buzzing wasp of war was in the air.
October smouldered goldenly, and gray

November sulked and threatened. Day by day,

While yet the greater evils held aloof,

The soldiers wrought on wall, stockade and roof

Against the coming wrath of God and Man.

And often where the lonely home-trail ran

They gazed with longing eyes; nor did they see

The dust cloud of the prayed-for cavalry

And ammunition train long overdue.

By now they saw their forces cut in two,

First Reno Post upon the Powder, then

Fort Smith upon the Big Horn needing men;

And here the center of the brewing storm

Would rage.
Official suavities kept warm

The wire to Laramie﻿—assurance bland

Of peace now reigning in the prairie land;

Attest the treaty signed! So said the mail;

But those who brought it up the Bozeman Trail

Two hundred miles, could tell of running fights,

Of playing tag with Terror in the nights

To hide by day. If peace was anywhere,

===Chinese===

远方的草原看见那面吹嘘的旗帜
在堡上抛卷，像一条嘶嘶响的蝰蛇之舌，
威胁着一切古老的善。
林中的工匠们早已屡屡
感到暗藏敌人的存在。
割草人在干草地里
嗡嗡的作业常常被一声枪响、
一阵箭啸打断；常常，沿着伐木的路
骂骂咧咧、鞭子乱挥地往回赶，
吓坏了的骡夫躲在他那车木料后面作战，
他的一队牲口踢成了一团乱麻。常常在夜里，
哪座山顶忽然抖出一把光的胡子，
随即被剃光；黑黢黢的山丘
亮了一下，仿佛有了知觉。拂晓前
昏昏欲睡的惊惧时辰里，人们听见过
遭劫的牛群哞叫——
它们怕那些外来的牧人；听见过号角
催人上马；吼叫的出击；逃遁的哀号。
白天也常有：远远的山脊上
一个赤裸的骑手顶着强光显现，
手搭在眉上遮出一道搜索的凝视，
随后像一场梦，消散在空空的天空里。
人和命运就这样劳碌过整个七月，
做成一个故事。八月晒黄了平原；
菲尔·卡尼堡顶着劳作的汗
和小规模战斗的血，一个劲儿地长。
九月带来了刺痛的银夜，
人们想起雪，干活更快了。
是啊，他们怕的比风暴更多。友善的克劳人
讲过骇人的故事。他们难道没有
骑马穿过波德河畔苏人的大集会？
看哪，这一头天才破晓，
那一头已是正午！沿着汤河
大村子都在跳舞！到处
空中都嗡嗡飞着战争的黄蜂。
十月金黄金黄地闷烧，灰色的
十一月阴沉着脸威吓。一天又一天，
趁着更大的灾祸还没有临头，
士兵们赶修围墙、栅栏和屋顶，
防备将临的神与人的震怒。
他们常常在孤独的回家路上
投去渴望的目光；却总也望不见
盼了许久的骑兵和弹药车队
该到而未到的烟尘。
到这时他们眼见兵力被切成两半，
先是波德河上的雷诺哨站，然后是
大角河上的史密斯堡都缺人；
而酝酿中的风暴，
中心就要在这里发作。
官腔的温言暖语焐着
通拉勒米的电报线——圆滑地保证
草原上如今太平无事；
条约已经签署，有据可查！信件上这么说；
可是沿着博兹曼小道
走两百里把信送来的人，却能讲出一路的追击战，
讲夜里同“恐怖”捉迷藏、
白天躲藏的日子。要说哪里有和平——

===Original===

It favored most the growing graveyard there

Across the Piney under Pilot Hill.
December opened ominously still,

And scarce the noon could dull the eager fang

That now the long night whetted. Shod hoofs rang

On frozen sod. The tenuated whine

And sudden shriek of buzz-saws biting pine

Were heard far off unnaturally loud.

The six-mule log-teams labored in a cloud;

The drivers beat their breasts with aching hands.

As yet the snow held off; but prowling bands

Grew bolder. Weary night-guards on the walls

Were startled broad awake by wolf-like calls

From spots of gloom uncomfortably near;

And out across the crystal hemisphere

Weird yammerings arose and died away

To dreadful silence. Every sunny day

The looking-glasses glimmered all about.

So, clinging to the darker side of doubt,

Men took their boots to bed, nor slumbered soon.
It happened on the sixth December noon

That from a hill commanding many a mile

The lookout, gazing off to Piney Isle,

Beheld the log-train crawling up a draw

Still half way out. With naked eye he saw

A lazy serpent reeking in the glare

Of wintry sunlight. Nothing else was there

But empty country under empty skies.

Then suddenly it seemed a blur of flies

Arose from each adjacent gulch and break

And, swarming inward, swirled about the snake

That strove to coil amid the stinging mass.

One moment through the ill-adjusted glass

Vague shadows flitted; then the whirling specks

Were ponies with their riders at their necks,

Swung low. The lurching wagons spurted smoke;

The teams were plunging.
Frantic signals woke

The bugles at the fort, the brawl of men

Obeying “boots and saddles.”
Once again

The sentry lifts his glass. ’Tis like a dream.

So very near the silent figures seem

A hand might almost touch them. Here they come

Hell-bent for blood﻿—distorted mouths made dumb

With distance! One can see the muffled shout,

The twang of bow-thongs! Leaping fog blots out

The agitated picture﻿—flattens, spreads.

Dull rumblings wake and perish. Tossing heads

Emerge, and ramrods prickle in the rack.

A wheel-mule, sprouting feathers at his back,

Rears like a clumsy bird essaying flight

And falls to vicious kicking. Left and right

Deflected hundreds wheel about and swing

To charge anew﻿—tempestuous galloping

On cotton! Empty ponies bolt away

To turn and stare high-headed on the fray

===Chinese===

它最眷顾的，倒是派尼河对岸、
领航山下面那片不断长大的墓园。
十二月不祥地静着开场，
漫长的夜磨快了利齿，
正午也几乎磨不利它的钝。
钉了掌的马蹄敲响
冻硬的草地。绷紧的呜咽
和蜂锯咬进松木的骤然尖啸，
远远听着响得不自然。
六骡拉的原木队在烟尘里挣扎；
车夫用酸痛的手捶着胸口。
雪迟迟不下；可游荡的股匪
胆子越来越大。墙上疲惫的夜哨
被狼嚎似的呼唤惊得彻底醒来，
那声音来自近得难受的暗处；
而在水晶般的半球天穹上，
怪异的嚥叫此起彼伏又归于
可怕的寂静。每一个晴天，
四面八方都有镜子在闪光。
于是人们抓着疑心的阴暗面，
穿着靴子上床，久久不能入睡。
十二月六日的正午，
事情发生了：在一座俯瞰数里的山头，
瞭望兵眺望着派尼岛，
看见运木队正爬上一道冲沟，
才走了一半。他用肉眼看见
一条懒洋洋的大蛇，在冬日
炫目的阳光里冒着热气。除此之外
只有空空天空底下的空空旷野。
随后忽然间，仿佛一团团蝇群
从附近每条冲沟和裂谷里升起，
向内聚拢，绕着那条
想在蜇刺的群阵中盘起身的蛇打旋。
一时间，透过没调好的望远镜，
模糊的影子掠动；随后那些打旋的斑点
是压低身子、伏在马颈上的骑手
胯下的小马。摇晃的篷车喷出硝烟；
骡队狂跳起来。
疯狂的信号唤醒了
堡里的号角，唤醒了应着
“备鞍上马”喧闹起来的人。
哨兵又一次举起望远镜。这像一场梦。
那些无声的身影显得那么近，
一只手几乎能碰到他们。他们来了——
拼着命来索血——距离把歪扭的嘴
弄成了哑巴！人能看见那闷住的呐喊，
弓弦的脆响！跃动的雾一团团抹掉
这激荡的画面——摊平，铺开。
沉闷的轰响醒来又消逝。扬起的马头
冒出来，通条在枪架上一根根竖起。
一匹挽骡，背上插着羽箭，
像一只笨拙学飞的鸟那样人立而起，
跌落下来便狠命地踢。左右
成百的骑手斜里一转、一摆，
重新冲锋——一场暴风般的疾驰
冲进一片棉花似的虚空！空了背的小马四散奔逃，
又转回身，昂着头呆望这场厮杀——

===Original===

With muted snorting at the deeds men do.

But listen how at last a sound breaks through

The deathly silence of the scene! Hurrah

For forty troopers roaring down the draw

With Fetterman! A cloud of beaten dust

Sent skurrying before a thunder-gust,

They round the hogback yonder. With a rush

They pierce the limpid curtain of the hush,

Quiescing in the picture. Hurry, men!

The rabid dogs are rushing in again!

Look! Hurry! No, they break midway! They see

The squadron dashing up. They turn, they flee

Before that pack of terriers﻿—like rats!

Yell, yell, you lucky loggers﻿—wave your hats

And thank the Captain that you’ve kept your hair!

Look how they scatter to the northward there,

Dissolving into nothing! Ply the spurs,

You fire-eaters! Catch that pack of curs

This side the Peno, or they’ll disappear!

Look out! They’re swooping in upon your rear!

Wherever did they come from? Look! Good God!

The breaks ahead belch ponies, and the sod

On every side sprouts warriors!
Holy Spoons!

The raw recruits have funked it! Turn, you loons,

You cowards! Can’t you see the Captain’s game

To face them with a handful? Shame! O shame!

They’ll rub him out﻿—turn back﻿—that’s not the way

We did it to the Johnnies many a day

In Dixie! Every mother’s baby rides

As though it mattered if they saved their hides!

Their empty faces gulp the miles ahead.

Ride on and live to wish that you were dead

Back yonder where the huddled muskets spit

Against a sea!
Now﻿—now you’re in for it!

Here comes the Colonel galloping like sin

Around the hill! Hurrah﻿—they’re falling in﻿—

Good boys! It’s little wonder that you ran.

I’m not ashamed to say to any man

I might have run.
Ah, what a pretty sight!

Go on, go on and show ’em that you’re white!

They’re breaking now﻿—you’ve got ’em on the run﻿—

They’re scattering! Hurrah!
The fight was done;

No victory to boast about, indeed﻿—

Just labor. Sweat today, tomorrow, bleed﻿—

An incidental difference. And when

The jaded troopers trotted home again

There wasn’t any cheering. Six of those

Clung dizzily to bloody saddle-bows;

And Bingham was the seventh and was dead;

And Bowers, with less hair upon his head

Than arrows in his vitals, prayed to die.

He did that night.
Now thirteen days went by

With neither snow nor foe; and all the while

The log-trains kept the road to Piney Isle.

===Chinese===

闷声打着响鼻，怪人干的这些事。
可听啊，终于有一阵声音冲破了
这死一样的寂静！好哇，
四十名骑兵跟着费特曼
吼着冲下冲沟！一团被击碎的尘土
被雷暴前的疾风卷着跑，
他们绕过那边猪背岭。他们一阵猛冲，
穿透寂静的透明帷幕，
嵌进画面里。快啊，弟兄们！
那群疯狗又扑上来了！
看！快！不，他们半路折返了！他们看见
骑兵队冲上来了。他们转身，逃窜，
在那群小猎犬面前——像一群耗子！
吼啊，吼啊，你们走运的伐木工——挥舞帽子，
谢谢上校保住了你们的头发！
看他们往北边那里散开，
散成乌有！夹马，夹马，
你们这帮玩命的！赶在佩诺河
这边抓住那群野狗，不然他们就没影了！
小心！他们朝你们后背扑上来了！
他们到底是从哪儿冒出来的？看！天哪！
前面的断崖往外喷小马，四面八方
草地里都冒出战士！我的老天！
新兵蛋子们吓瘫了！回头，你们这群蠢货，
你们这群懦夫！难道看不出上校的招数——
拿一小撮人迎战他们？丢人！丢人！
他们要把他抹掉了——回头呀——当年在南边
我们打南军可不是这么打的，多少天哪！
这些娘养的娃娃一个个骑着马，
倒像保住皮囊要紧！
他们空洞的脸吞着前面一里里路。
骑下去吧，活着去后悔没死在
后面那片挤作一团的枪刺
对着人海喷吐的地方！
现在——现在你们跑不掉了！
上校像催命似的绕过
山头奔驰而来！好哇——他们归队了——
好小子！难怪你们要跑。
当着谁我都敢说，
换了我没准也跑。
啊，多好看的一景！
上啊，上啊，让他们看看你们是条汉子！
他们垮了——你们把他们赶跑了——
他们散了！好哇！
仗打完了；
确实没什么可夸耀的胜利——
只是干活。今天流汗，明天流血——
无非一点附带的差别。等
人困马乏的骑兵们小跑着回营，
没有任何欢呼。他们中间六个
晕晕地伏在血污的鞍头上；
宾厄姆是第七个，已经死了；
鲍尔斯头上剩的头发还不及
扎进他内脏的箭多，他求一死。
当夜他死了。随后十三天过去，
没有雪也没有敌人；运木队
一路照旧通往派尼岛。

===Original===

Soon all the needed timber would be hauled,

The work be done. Then, snugly roofed and walled,

What need for men to fear? Some came to deem

The former mood of dread a foolish dream,

Grew mellow, thinking of the holidays

With time for laughter and a merry blaze

On every hearth and nothing much to do.

As for the bruited power of the Sioux,

Who doubted it was overdrawn a mite?

At any rate, they wouldn’t stand and fight

Unless the odds were heavy on their side.

It seemed the Colonel hadn’t any pride﻿—

Too cautious. Look at Fetterman and Brown,

Who said they’d ride the whole Sioux nation down

With eighty men; and maybe could, by jing!

Both scrappers﻿—not afraid of anything﻿—

A pair of eagles hungering for wrens!
And what about a flock of butchered hens

In Peno valley not so long ago

But for the Colonel? Bowers ought to know;

Go ask him! Thus the less heroic jeered.

These Redskins didn’t run because they feared;

’Twas strategy; they didn’t fight our way.
Again it happened on the nineteenth day

The lookout saw the logging-train in grief;

And Captain Powell, leading the relief,

Returned without a single scratch to show.

The twentieth brought neither snow nor foe.

The morrow came﻿—a peaceful, scarlet morn.

It seemed the homesick sun in Capricorn

Had found new courage for the homeward track

And, yearning out across the zodiac

To Cancer, brightened with the conjured scene

Of grateful hills and valleys flowing green,

Sweet incense rising from the rain-soaked sward,

And color-shouts of welcome to the Lord

And Savior.
Ninety took the logging-road

That morning, happy that the final load

Would trundle back that day, and all be well.

But hardly two miles out the foemen fell

Upon them, swarming three to one. And so

Once more the hilltop lookout signalled woe

And made the fort a wasp-nest buzzing ire.
The rip and drawl of running musket fire,

The muffled, rhythmic uproar of the Sioux

Made plain to all that what there was to do

Out yonder gave but little time to waste.

A band of horse and infantry soon faced

The Colonel’s quarters, waiting for the word.

Above the distant tumult many heard

His charge to Powell, leader of the band;

And twice ’twas said that all might understand

The need for caution: “Drive away the foe

And free the wagon-train; but do not go

Past Lodge Trail Ridge.”
A moment’s silence fell;

===Chinese===

用不了多久，需要的木料都将运齐，
工程就完了。那时屋顶墙身严丝合缝，
人还有什么可怕的？有些人已开始把
先前那种恐惧的心绪当作愚蠢的梦，
心肠软和下来，想着节日快到了，
有工夫欢笑，家家炉膛里
烧起快活的旺火，没什么要紧事可做。
至于传说中苏人的厉害，
谁不信那是添了油加了醋？
再说，他们也不敢站着对阵，
除非优势全在他们那一边。
上校看来是一点傲气也没有——
太谨慎了。瞧费特曼和布朗，
人家放话，八十个人就能把整个苏族
踏平；说不定还真行，真是的！
两个都是斗士——天不怕地不怕——
一对饿了想吃鹪鹩的鹰！
再说不久前佩诺谷里那一窝
差点被宰光的老母鸡，要不是上校，
现在怎样了？鲍尔斯最清楚；
去问他吧！英雄气短的人们就这样讥笑。
这些红种人跑不是因为怕；
那是战略；他们不按我们的路数打。
第十九天，事情又来了：
瞭望兵看见运木队又遭了难；
鲍威尔上尉带兵去接应，
回来连一点擦伤都没有。
第二十天，还是没有雪，没有敌人。
次日来了——一个太平的、猩红的早晨。
仿佛摩羯宫里那思乡的太阳
重新鼓起了回家的勇气，
隔着黄道带朝巨蟹宫眺望，
因心间幻出的景象而明亮——
感恩的山峦、流绿的山谷，
雨浸的草皮升起甜香，
漫山遍野的色彩欢呼着迎接主
和救主。
那天早晨，九十个人走上运木路，
高高兴兴，想着最后一车木料
当天就能运回，从此万事大吉。
可出营不到两里，敌人
蜂拥扑来，三打一。于是
山头的瞭望兵又一次挂出噩讯，
把堡变成一个怒气嗡嗡的马蜂窝。
滑膛枪一路噼啪拖沓地响，
苏人那边闷闷的、有节奏的喧嚣，
让所有人都明白：外头那件事
容不得半点耽搁。一队骑步兵
很快在上校营房前列队，等候号令。
在远处的喧嚣之上，许多人听见
他授命给队长鲍威尔；
那句话说了两遍，好让人人记住
务须谨慎：“把敌人赶走，
救出车队；但不得越过
柴道岭。”片刻的寂静落下；

===Original===

And many in the after-time would dwell

Upon that moment, little heeded then﻿—

The ghostly horses and the ghostly men,

The white-faced wives, the gaping children’s eyes

Grown big with wonder and a dread surmise

To see their fathers waiting giant-tall;

That mumbling voice of doom beyond the wall;

The ghastly golden pleasance of the air;

And Fetterman, a spectre, striding there

Before the Colonel, while the portals yawn.

As vivid as a picture lightning-drawn

Upon the night, that memory would flash,

More vivid for the swooping backward crash

Of gloom. ’Twas but the hinges of the gates

That shrieked that moment, while the eager Fates

Told off the waiting band and gloated: *Done!*

*He asked for eighty﻿—give him eighty-one!*

Then Fetterman, unwitting how the rim

Of endless outer silence pressed on him

And all his comrades, spoke: “With deference due

To Captain Powell, Colonel, and to you,

I claim command as senior captain here.”
So ever is the gipsy Danger dear

To Courage; so the lusty woo and wed

Their dooms, to father in a narrow bed

A song against the prosing after-years.
And now the restive horses prick their ears

And nicker to the bugle. Fours about,

They rear and wheel to line. The hillsides shout

Back to the party. Forward! Now it swings

High-hearted through the gate of common things

To where bright hazard, like a stormy moon,

Still gleams round Hector, Roland, Sigurd, Fionn;

And all the lost, horizon-hungry prows,

Eternal in contemporary nows,

Heave seaward yet.
The Colonel mounts the wall,

And once again is heard his warning call:

“Relieve the wagon-train, but do not go

Past Lodge Trail Ridge.” And Fetterman, below,

Turns back a shining face on him, and smiles

Across the gap that neither years nor miles

May compass now.
A little farther still

They watched him skirt a westward-lying hill

That hid him from the train, to disappear.

“He’ll swing about and strike them in the rear,”

The watchers said, “and have the logging crew

For anvil.”
Now a solitary Sioux

Was galloping in circles on a height

That looked on both the squadron and the fight﻿—

The prairie sign for “many bison seen.”

A lucky case-shot swept the summit clean,

And presently the distant firing ceased;

Nor was there sound or sight of man or beast

Outside for age-long minutes after that.

At length a logger, spurring up the flat,

===Chinese===

后来，多少人会在那一时刻上久久停留——彼时无人在意——
鬼魅似的马，鬼魅似的人，
脸色惨白的妻子们，孩子们张大的眼睛
因惊奇和不祥的揣测而睁圆，
看着他们的父亲巨汉一般伫立；
墙外那含混的厄运之声；
空气里惨金色的宜人；
还有费特曼，一个幽灵，在堡门
大张的时候，大步走在上校身前。
像闪电画在夜幕上的一幅画，那段记忆会一次次闪亮，
更亮，因为随后黑暗劈头盖下。
那一刻嘶叫的不过是大门的铰链，
而急切的命运三女神正清点
待命的队伍，暗自得意：*成了！*
他要八十个——给他八十一个！
随后费特曼开口了——他不知道，无尽的外界寂静
怎样把它的边缘压上他
和他所有的弟兄：“出于对鲍威尔上尉、
对上校您应尽的敬意，
我以资深上尉的身份，请领此行的指挥权。”
危险这吉卜赛人，永远这样被“勇气”珍爱；
健壮的人们就这样向他们的劫数求爱、成婚，
在一架狭窄的床榻上生下一支歌，
来对抗往后岁月的乏味平铺。
此刻躁动的战马竖起耳朵，
应着号角轻嘶。四人一排，
它们人立、旋绕、排成横列。山坡
朝队伍喊出回声。前进！此刻队伍
意气扬扬，穿过寻常事物的大门，
走向那明亮的风险——它像一轮风雨的月亮，
至今仍照着赫克托耳、罗兰、西格德、芬恩；
而所有失落的、渴望地平线的船首，
在一代代“现在”里永生，
仍在向大海起伏。
上校登上围墙，
他那声警告又一次传来：
“救援车队，但不得越过
柴道岭。”下方的费特曼
回过头，把一张发亮的脸对着他，微笑着，
隔着如今岁月与里程
都无法再丈量的一道鸿沟。
他们又目送了他一小程，
看他绕过一座朝西的山丘——
那山把他挡在车队视线之外，随后他不见了。
“他会兜过去，从背后打击他们，”
瞭望的人说，“拿伐木队
当铁砧。”
此刻一个孤单的苏人
在一座高地上绕着圈子疾驰——
那高地俯瞰着骑兵队和战场——
这是草原上的信号：“看见很多野牛。”
一发走运的榴霰弹扫清了山顶，
远处的枪声随即停了；
此后漫长漫长的几分钟里，
外面再无人声兽影。
终于，一个伐木工策马奔上平地，

===Original===

Arrived with words of doubtful cheer to say.

The Indians had vanished Peno way;

The train was moving on to Piney Isle.

He had no news of Fetterman.

===Chinese===

带来几句没把握的好消息。
印第安人已朝佩诺方向消失；
车队正继续开往派尼岛。
他没有费特曼的消息。

## V Rubbed Out / 五 被抹掉

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out /

## V Rubbed Out / 
===Original===

Meanwhile

Where ran the Bozeman Road along the bleak

North slope of Lodge Trail Ridge to Peno Creek,

Big hopes were burning. Silence waited there.

The brown land, even as the high blue air,

Seemed empty. Yet the troubled crows that flew

Keen-eyed above the sunning valley knew

What made the windless slough-grass ripple so,

And how a multitude of eyes below

Were peering southward to the road-scarred rise

Where every covert was alive with eyes

That scanned the bare horizon to the south.
The white of dawn had seen the Peno’s mouth

A-swarm with men﻿—Cheyennes, Arapahoes,

Dakotas. When the pale-faced sun arose﻿—

A spectre fleeing from a bath of blood﻿—

It saw them like a thunder-fathered flood

Surge upward through the sounding sloughs and draws﻿—

Afoot and mounted, veterans and squaws,

Youths new to war, the lowly and the great﻿—

A thousand-footed, single-hearted hate

Flung fortward. Now their chanted battle-songs

Dismayed the hills. Now silent with their wrongs

They strode, the sullen hum of hoofs and feet,

Through valleys where aforetime life was sweet,

More terrible than songs or battle cries.
The sun had traversed half the morning skies

When, entering the open flat, they poured

To where the roadway crossed the Peno ford

Below the Ridge. Above them wheeled and pried

The puzzled crows, to learn what thing had died,

What carcass, haply hidden from the ken

Of birds, had lured so large a flock of men

Thus chattering with lust. There, brooding doom,

They paused and made the brown December bloom

With mockeries of August﻿—demon flowers

And lethal, thirsting for the sanguine showers

That soon should soak the unbegetting fields﻿—

The trailing bonnets and the pictured shields,

The lances nodding in the warwind’s breath,

And faces brave with paint to outstare Death

In some swift hush of battle!
Briefly so

They parleyed. Then the spears began to flow

On either side the Ridge﻿—a double stream

Of horsemen, winking out as in a dream

High up among the breaks that flanked the trail.

Amid the tall dry grasses of the vale

The footmen disappeared; and all the place

Was still and empty as a dead man’s face

That sees unmoved the wheeling birds of prey.
The anxious moments crawled. Then far away

Across the hills a muffled tumult grew,

As of a blanket being ripped in two

And many people shouting underground.

===Chinese===

与此同时，
在博兹曼大道沿着柴道岭荒凉的
北坡伸向佩诺溪的地方，
巨大的希望在燃烧。寂静在那里守候。
棕色的大地，和高处的蓝天一样，
看似空无一人。可那些盘旋的乌鸦
目光锐利，在晒着太阳的谷地上空，它们知道
是什么让无风的沼草这样泛起涟漪，
知道下面多少双眼睛
正向南窥望那道被道路划伤的坡——
那里的每一处掩蔽都活满了眼睛，
扫视着南面光秃的地平线。
黎明的鱼肚白曾看见佩诺河口
黑压压全是人——夏延人、阿拉帕霍人、
达科他人。当那张苍白的脸的太阳升起——
一个逃避血浴的幽灵——
它看见他们像雷声催生的大水，
轰轰隆隆涌上沼泽与冲沟——
步行的、骑马的，老兵和妇人，
初上战场的小伙子，卑者与俊杰——
一条千足的、同心的仇恨，
直扑向堡的方向。
此刻他们吟唱的战歌
使群山惊惶。此刻他们又怀着冤苦默然
大步走着，蹄声脚步声沉郁地嗡鸣，
穿过那些往日生活甜美的谷地，
比歌声与呐喊更可怖。
太阳走过半个上午的天空，
这时他们涌进开阔的平地，
漫向大道越过佩诺渡口
在岭下的地方。头顶上，困惑的乌鸦
盘旋窥探，想知道是什么东西死了，
什么样的尸首——也许藏着躲开了
鸟类的眼力——竟引来了这么大的一群人，
这样欲火熊熊地聒噪。就在那里，孵着厄运，
他们停了下来，让棕色的十二月
开出八月的嘲弄——一朵朵恶鬼之花，
致命之花，渴饮着即将浸润
这片不孕田亩的血雨——
拖尾的羽冠和彩绘的盾，
长矛在战风的呼吸里点头，
一张张面孔涂彩逞勇，要在战斗
某个迅疾的间歇里瞪视过死亡！
他们就这样简短地
商议。随后矛群开始在岭的
两侧流动——两股骑手的
溪流，像梦里那样在大道两侧
高处的断崖间眨眼隐没。
步兵消失在谷中高高的枯草里；
整个地方静止而空旷，
像一张死人的脸，
无动于衷地看着盘旋的猛禽。
焦灼的时光爬行。随后远处
群山之外，一阵闷闷的骚动涨起来，
像一条毯子被撕成两半，
又像许多人在地下呼喊。

===Original===

The valley grasses rippled to the sound

As though it were a gusty wind that passed.
Far off a bugle’s singing braved the vast

And perished in a wail.
The tall grass stirred.
The rumor of the distant fight was heard

A little longer. Suddenly it stopped;

And silence, like a sky-wide blanket, dropped

Upon the landscape empty as the moon.
The sun, now scarce a lance-length from the noon,

Seemed waiting for whatever might occur.

Across the far Northwest a purplish blur

Had gathered and was crawling up the sky.
Now presently a nearer bugle cry

Defied the hush﻿—a scarlet flower of sound

That sowed the sterile silences around

With futile seed of music.
Once again

The sound of firing and the cries of men

Arose; but now ’twas just beyond the place

Where, climbing to the azure rim of space,

The roadway topped the Ridge and disappeared.

The tongueless coverts listened, thousand-eared,

And heard hoof-thunders rumbling over there.
Then suddenly the high blue strip of air

Was belching warriors in a wind of cries.

In breakneck rout they tumbled from the skies,

Wheeled round to fling more arrows at a foe,

And fled to where the breast-deep grass below

Swayed wildly.
Now a crow-black stallion ’rose,

And looming huge against the blue noon doze,

Raced back and forth across the Ridge’s rim,

While, shooting from beneath the neck of him,

The Cheyenne Big Nose held the roaring rear;

Nor did the snarling musket-balls come near,

So mighty was his medicine, they say.

Now presently the high blue wall of day

Spewed cavalry along the Ridge; and then

A marvel for the tongues and ears of men

Amazed the hidden watchers of the height.

For like a thunder-stridden wind of night

That rages through a touselled poplar grove,

The rider of the stallion charged, and drove

Straight through the middle of the mounted crowd.

Men saw his bonnet tossing in a cloud

Of manes and tails; and sabre lightnings played

About it. Then, emerging undismayed,

He charged back through and galloped down the hill

With bullets that were impotent to kill

*Spat-pinging* all around.
The firing ceased.

The fugitives were half a mile at least

Beyond the Peno ford. There, circling wide

With bows and lances brandished, they defied

The foe to come and fight with them. By now

The infantry had crossed the Ridge’s brow.

It joined the troop a little way below;

===Chinese===

谷中的草应声泛起涟漪，
仿佛刮过了一阵急风。
远处一支号角迎着洪荒歌唱，
随即在一声哀鸣里殒灭。
高草颤动。
远处战斗的传闻又响了
一小会儿。忽然它停了；
寂静像一条天那么宽的毯子，落下来，
罩住月亮一样空空的风景。
太阳离正午已不到一矛之长，
仿佛在等着无论发生什么。
遥远的西北方，一团发紫的模糊
已经聚起，正爬上天空。
此刻，一声更近的号角
向寂静挑战——一朵猩红的声音之花，
向四周贫瘠的寂静撒下
音乐的、徒劳的种子。
枪声与人喊
又一次响起；可此刻是在
大道爬上碧空边缘、越过岭脊
消失不见之处的再前方。
无言的掩蔽处竖起千耳聆听，
听见那边蹄声的雷在滚动。
随后忽然，高处那一条蓝色空气
在一片呐喊之风里喷出战士。
他们不顾死活地溃奔着从天上滚落，
回身又朝敌人掷出一蓬箭，
再逃向下面齐胸深的
狂野摇摆的草丛。
此刻一匹鸦黑的种马人立而起，
衬着正午蓝色的倦意显得巨大，
沿岭脊来回疾驰，
马颈下探身射击的，
是夏延人大鼻子，他独拒咆哮的追兵；
咆哮的枪弹也没能挨着他，
人们说，他的法力就有这样大。
此刻白日高高的蓝色墙壁
沿着岭脊喷出骑兵；随后，
一桩让人们的口舌和耳朵
惊愕不已的奇迹，震住了高地上潜伏的观者。
那匹黑马的骑手像一阵挟着雷霆的夜风，
怒扫过一片凌乱的白杨林，
径直冲进骑兵群的正中。
人们看见他的羽冠在一团
马鬃马尾的云里翻腾；军刀的闪电
在四下起舞。随后他毫无惧色地穿阵而出，
再冲回来，又驰下山坡，
杀不死他的子弹在他四周
*噼啪*弹跳。
枪声停了。
诱敌的溃骑离佩诺渡口
至少还有半里。在那里，他们兜着大圈，
挥舞着弓和长矛，挑衅
敌人过来交战。这时
步兵已经翻过岭脊，
在坡下不远与骑兵会合；

===Original===

Then all together, cautiously and slow,

Came down the hated road. And silence lay

On summit, slope and valley, deep as day

And doomful, as they came. The flat could hear

The murmur of the straining saddle-gear,

The shuffling feet, the clinking of the bits;

And when a nervous troop-horse neighed by fits,

The ponies, lurking in the broken lands

That flanked the Ridge, kept silence for the hands

That gripped their nostrils.
Now the eighty-one

Were half way down the hill. The nooning sun

Slipped fearfully behind a flying veil,

And from the gray northwest a raw-cold gale

Came booming up. The fugitive decoys,

Off yonder in the flat, like playing boys

Divided now and waged a mimic fight.

Immediately half way up the height

Among the breaks appeared a warrior’s torse.

A thousand hidden eyes knew Little Horse,

The Cheyenne chieftain; saw him wave a spear

Left-handed; pass it round him in the rear

To seize it with the right.
The whole flat swarmed

With footmen. Mounted warriors thunder-stormed

By hundreds from the breaks above; and one

Came dashing down the ridge-road at a run

And plunged among the soldiery to die

Beneath the frantic sabres. With a cry

That set the horses wild, the swarm closed in.
The cavalry, as hoping yet to win

The summit of the Ridge, wheeled round and hewed

A slow way upward through the solitude

Of lances, howling in the arrow-storm.

The rest, already circled by the swarm,

Took cover in a patch of tumbled rock

Where, huddled like a blizzard-beaten flock,

They faced the swirling death they could not stem.

A little while before it smothered them

The dwindling few toiled mightily, men say,

With gun-butts swinging in the dim melee

Of battle-clubs and lances; then were still.
The wave broke over, surging up the hill;

For yonder yet the battle smoked and roared

Where, midway ’twixt the summit and the ford,

The little band of troopers held the height﻿—

Green manhood withering in a locust flight

Of arrows! Aye, a gloaming of despair

The shuttling arrows wove above them there,

So many were the bows. Cheyenne and Sioux

Went down beneath the shafts their brothers drew;

Arapahoes struck down Arapahoes

Unwittingly. And many a red gout froze

Along the slopes, so keen had grown the gale.
A little while those makers of a tale

Gave battle like a badger in a hole;

Nor could the ponies charge the narrow knoll,

===Chinese===

随后他们合在一处，小心而缓慢地
走下那条可恨的大路。他们走来时，
寂静铺在岭脊、山坡和谷地上，
深得像白昼，也满是厄运。平地听得见
鞍具绷紧的低吟、
杂沓的脚步、衔铁的叮当；
一匹紧张的战马断续嘶鸣时，
埋伏在岭侧乱石地里的小马
被捂住鼻孔的手按着，一声不出。
此刻那八十一个
已到半山。正午的太阳
惊惶地躲进一片飞驰的薄纱后面，
灰色的西北方，一股刺骨的狂风
隆隆刮起。远处平地上那些诱敌的
溃骑，像戏耍的男孩一样
分成两拨，打起一场假仗。
转瞬间，半山腰的
乱石间现出一个战士的上身。
一千双潜伏的眼睛认出了小马——
那位夏延酋长；看见他左手
挥矛；又把矛绕到身后，
换右手握定。整片平地
涌满了步兵。
骑手的雷暴
以百计从上面的乱石间炸下；
有一个顺着岭上的大路狂奔而下，
一头扎进敌军里去死在
疯狂的军刀之下。一声呼喊
惊得战马发狂，人潮合拢了。
骑兵们还存着夺回
岭脊的指望，拨转马头，在长矛的
荒寂中、在箭雨的呼啸中，
一路砍出一条缓慢的上山路。
其余的人早被人潮围住，
退进一片乱石堆里，
像被暴风雪捶打的一群羊，挤作一团，
面对他们无力遏止的旋卷的死亡。
人们说，在被吞没之前
那一小撮渐稀的人还在苦斗，
抡着枪托，在战棍与长矛
昏暗的混战里搏杀；随后静了。
浪头漫过去，涌着爬上山坡；
因为那边战斗还在冒烟咆哮——
在岭脊与渡口之间的半途，
那一小队骑兵守着高处——
青涩的青春在蝗群一样的
箭雨里凋零！
是啊，绝望的暮色——
穿梭的箭在他们头顶织出一片昏暗，
弓实在太多了。夏延人和苏人
倒在自家弟兄开的弓下；
阿拉帕霍人不知不觉
射倒了阿拉帕霍人。山坡上
一摊摊血珠冻结，风已刮得那样刺骨。
这些故事的缔造者像洞里的獾，
又顽斗了一阵；
小马也冲不上那狭窄的丘头，

===Original===

For either slope was steep and gully-scrawled.

Still up and up the cautious bowmen crawled,

And still the troopers overawed the field.
Then presently, men say, a white chief reeled;

Rolled from his saddle; like a man gone daft

Got up and doddered, tugging at a shaft

That sprouted from his belly. Then a yell

Of many bowmen mocked him as he fell,

His writhing body feathered like a goose.
The troops began to turn their horses loose,

Retreating up the ridge, a hopeless crowd.

A lull of battle thinned the arrow-cloud

Above them; for the mounted warriors knew

The soldiers doomed whatever they might do,

And fell to rounding up the runaways.

Meanwhile the broken troopers in a daze

Of desperation scrambled up the slope.

Strewn boulders yonder woke a lying hope,

And there they waited, living, in their grave.
The horse-chase ended. Once again the wave

Began to mount the steep on either side,

While warriors hailed their fellows and replied:

*Be ready!﻿—We are ready, brothers!*
Then

The hillsides bellowed with a surf of men

Flung crowding on the boulders. ’Twas the end.
Some trooper’s wolfhound, mourning for his friend,

Loped fortward, pausing now and then to cry

His urgent question to the hostile sky

That spat a stinging frost. And someone said:

“Let yonder dog bear tidings of the dead

To make the white men tremble over there.”

“No, teach them that we do not even spare

Their dogs!” another said. An arrow sang

Shrill to the mark. The wolfhound yelped and sprang,

Snapped at the feather, wilted, and was still.
And so they perished on that barren hill

Beside the Peno. And the Winter strode

Numb-footed down that bloody stretch of road

At twilight, when a squadron came to read

The corpse-writ rune of battle, deed by deed,

Between the Ridge’s summit and the ford.
The blizzard broke at dusk. All night it roared

Round Fort Phil Kearney mourning for the slain.

===Chinese===

因为两面山坡都陡，沟壑纵横。
谨慎的弓手仍一步步往上爬，
而那些骑兵仍震慑着战场。
随后，人们说，一个白人酋长晃了晃；
从鞍上滚落；像个疯子一样
爬起来，摇摇晃晃，去拔
从肚子里长出的那支箭杆。
他倒下时，许多弓手一声呐喊嘲弄他，
他扭动的身子插满羽毛，像一只鹅。
士兵们开始放走战马，
向岭上退去，一群绝望的人。
战斗的间歇稀薄了他们头顶的
箭云；因为骑手们知道
这些士兵横竖是死，
便转去围捕那些跑散的马。
与此同时，溃散的骑兵们
在绝望的迷乱里手脚并用地爬上陡坡。
那边散乱的巨石唤起一个骗人的希望，
他们便在那里等着，活在自己的坟墓里。
追马的战事结束了。人浪
又一次开始从两侧涌上陡坡，
战士们呼唤同伴，同伴应答：
*预备好！——我们预备好了，兄弟们！*
随后
山坡上轰响着人的浪涛，
层层叠叠扑向巨石。结束了。
某个骑兵的猎狼犬，哀悼着它的主人，
朝堡的方向奔去，不时停下，
把它急切的疑问哭向
那喷吐刺骨寒霜的敌意的天空。
有人说：“让那条狗
给死人捎信去，叫那边的白人发抖。”
“不，让他们知道我们连
他们的狗也不放过！”另一个说。
一支箭尖啸着中了目标。
猎狼犬吠了一声，跳起来，
咬了一口箭羽，蔫下去，不动了。
他们就这样死在佩诺河边
那座荒山上。当暮色里一支骑兵队
前来逐桩逐桩辨认
岭脊与渡口之间那具具尸体写成的
战斗符文时，冬天
拖着冻僵的双脚走上那段血路。
黄昏时暴风雪爆发了。整夜，
它绕着菲尔·卡尼堡咆哮，
为阵亡者哀悼。

## VI The Wagon Boxes / 六 货车箱

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes /

## VI The Wagon Boxes / 
===Original===

Besieging January made the plain

One vast white camp to reinforce the foe

That watched the fort. Mad cavalries of snow

Assaulted; stubborn infantries of cold

Sat round the walls and waited. Wolves grew bold

To peer by night across the high stockade

Where, builded for the Winter’s escalade,

The hard drifts leaned. And often in the deep

Of night men started from a troubled sleep

To think the guards were fighting on the wall

And, roaring over like a waterfall,

The wild hordes pouring in upon the lost.

But ’twas the timber popping in the frost,

The mourning wolves. Nor did the dawn bring cheer.

Becandled like a corpse upon a bier

The lifeless sun, from gloom to early gloom,

Stole past﻿—a white procession to a tomb

Illumining the general despair.
Meanwhile Omniscience in a swivel chair,

Unmenaced half a continent away,

Amid more pressing matters of the day

Had edited the saga of the dead.

Compare the treaty where it plainly said

There was no war! All duly signed and sealed!

Undoubtedly the evidence revealed

The need of an official reprimand.

Wherefore stern orders ticked across the land

From Washington to Laramie. Perhaps

No blizzard swept the neat official maps

To nip a tracing finger. Howsoe’er,

Four companies of horse and foot must bear

To Fort Phil Kearney tidings of its shame.

Through ten score miles of frozen hell they came﻿—

Frost-bitten, wolfish﻿—with the iron word

Of Carrington dishonored and transferred

To Reno Post. The morning that he went,

The sun was like a sick man in a tent,

Crouched shivering between two feeble fires.

Far off men heard his griding wagon tires

Shriek fife-like in the unofficial snow,

His floundering three-span mule-teams blaring woe

Across the blue-cold waste; and he was gone.
Without a thaw the bitter spell wore on

To raging February. Days on days

Men could not see beyond the whirling haze

That made the fort’s the world’s wall fronting sheer

On chaos. When at times the sky would clear

And like a frozen bubble were the nights,

Pale rainbows jigged across the polar heights

And leafy rustlings mocked the solitude.

Men sickened with the stale and salty food,

For squadrons hunt at best with ill success;

And quiet days revealed the wilderness

Alert with fires, so doggedly the foe

Guarded the deer and elk and buffalo

That roamed the foothills where the grass was good.

===Chinese===

围城的一月把平原变成
一座广大的白色营盘，给监视着
堡垒的敌军增兵。疯狂的雪的骑兵
冲锋；顽固的寒冷的步兵
围着墙坐下来等候。狼胆壮了，
夜里敢隔着高高的栅栏窥探，
那里，为着冬天的云梯，
坚硬的雪堆斜倚着。深夜里
人们常从烦乱的睡眠中惊起，
以为卫兵正在墙上厮杀，
而野性的部落像瀑布一样
轰然漫过头顶，涌向失守的人。

其实是霜里爆裂的木头、
哀号的狼。黎明也不带来欢欣。
插满蜡烛，像灵台上的一具尸首，
死气沉沉的太阳，从昏暝走向早来的昏暝，
悄悄溜过——一支出殡的白色行列，
照亮了普遍的绝望。
与此同时，坐在转椅里的全知者，
远隔半个大陆、毫发无损，
在当天更要紧的事务中间，
把死者们的萨迦编辑了一过。

去比一比条约吧，那里清清楚楚
写着并无战争！全都正式签署、钤印！
毫无疑问，证据表明
需要一纸官方的申斥。

于是严厉的命令嗒嗒地传过大地，
从华盛顿到拉勒米。也许
没有暴风雪扫过整洁的官方地图，
冻僵那描图的手指。不管怎样，
四个连的骑兵和步兵必须
把耻辱的消息带进菲尔·卡尼堡。

他们穿过两百英里冰冻的地狱走来——
冻伤的、饿狼似的——带着那铁铸的字：
卡林顿蒙羞，调任
里诺堡。他动身的那天早晨，
太阳像帐篷里的一个病人，
缩在两堆微弱的火间打着寒战。
人们远远听见他碾磨似的车轮
在非官方的雪地里像横笛般尖啸，
他挣扎的三驾骡队把悲怆
吼过青冷的荒原；他走了。

苦咒不解冻地继续，
狂怒到二月。一天又一天，
人们望不透旋转的雪雾，
雪雾把堡垒的墙砌成世界的墙，
笔直地面对混沌。偶尔天空放晴，
夜晚像冻结的气泡，
苍白的彩虹在极地般的高空蹦跳，
叶子的沙沙声嘲弄着荒寂。

人们吃陈腐的咸肉吃出了病，
因为部队打猎充饥至多难得手；
而安静的日子显出荒野
处处有火光警醒，敌人那样死死
守卫着山麓草好地带里
游荡的鹿、麋和野牛。

===Original===

A battle often bought a load of wood;

And arrows swept the opening water-gate

From where the wily bowmen lurked in wait

Along the brush-clad Piney.
March went past,

A lion, crouched or raging, to the last;

And it was April﻿—in the almanac.

No maiden with the southwind at her back

Ran crocus-footed up the Bozeman Road.

A loveless vixen swept her drear abode

With brooms of whimsic wrath, and scolded shrill.

Men pined to think of how the whippoorwill

Broidered the moony silences at home.

There now a mist-like green began to roam

The naked forest hillward from the draws;

The dogwood’s bloom was vying with the haw’s;

The redbud made a bonfire of its boughs.

And there, perchance, one lying in a drowse

At midnight heard the friendly thunder crash,

The violet-begetting downpour lash

The flaring panes; and possibly one heard

The sudden rapture of a mocking bird

Defy the lightning in a pitch-black lull.
Here dull days wore the teeth of Winter dull.

Drifts withered slowly. Of an afternoon

The gulches grumbled hoarsely, ceasing soon

When sunset faded out. The pasque flower broke

The softened sod, and in a furry cloak

And airy bonnet brazened out the chill.

The long grave yonder under Pilot Hill,

Where eighty lay, was like a wound unwrapped.

The cottonwoods, awaking sluggish-sapped,

Prepared for spring with wavering belief.

May stole along the Piney like a thief.
And yet, another sun made summer now

In wild hearts given glebe-like to the plow

Of triumph. So miraculously fed

With slaughter, richly seeded with the dead,

The many-fielded harvest throve as one.
And Red Cloud was the summer and the sun.
In many a camp, in three great tribal tongues,

That magic name was thunder in the lungs

Of warriors. Swift, apocalyptic light,

It smote the zenith of the Red Man’s night

With dazzling vision. Forts dissolved in smoke,

The hated roadway lifted, drifted, broke

And was a dust; the white men were a tale;

The green, clean prairie bellowed, hill and vale,

With fatted bison; and the good old days

Came rushing back in one resistless blaze

Of morning!
It was good to be a youth

That season when all dreaming was the truth

And miracle familiar! Waning May

Could hear the young men singing on the way

To Red Cloud. Pious sons and rakehell scamps,

Unbroken colts, the scandals of their camps,

And big-eyed dreamers never tried by strife,

===Chinese===

常常要打一仗才换回一车柴；
箭扫过开敞的水门，
射自灌木丛生的派尼河边
狡诈的弓手埋伏之处。三月过去了，
自始至终是一头狮子，或蹲伏或狂怒；
然后就到四月——在历书上。

没有少女背着南风
迈着番红花的脚步跑上博兹曼小道。
一个无爱的泼妇用无常怒气的扫帚
打扫她阴郁的窝，尖声咒骂。

人们苦念家乡：夜鹰此刻
正在月光的静寂里绣花。
那里，一片雾似的绿正从洼地
向裸露的山林漫游；
山茱萸的花在跟山楂花争艳；
紫荆把枝条烧成一蓬篝火。

而那里，也许有个半睡的人
在午夜听见友好的雷声炸响，
孕育紫罗兰的骤雨抽打
灼亮的窗格；也许还有人听见
一只反舌鸟突然的欢歌
在漆黑的风间歇里向闪电挑战。

这里，阴沉的日子把冬天的牙齿磨钝。
雪堆慢慢地枯萎。某个午后
冲沟沙哑地轰鸣，日落
一褪尽就住了声。白头银莲花顶破
松软的草皮，裹着毛茸茸的斗篷、
戴着轻灵的小帽傲对寒气。

领航山下那座长长的坟，
八十人躺着的地方，像一道解开的伤口。
三角叶杨，汁液迟钝地醒来，
怀着游移的信心准备入春。
五月沿着派尼河潜行，像个窃贼。

然而，另一个太阳此刻在狂野的心里
造出了夏天，那些心如田地、
交给了胜利之犁的心。就这样，奇迹般地
以杀戮为肥、以死者慷慨下种，
多田的庄稼长成了一片。
而红云就是那夏天，就是那太阳。

在多少营地里，用三种伟大的部落语言，
那有魔力的名字是战士们
肺中的雷。迅疾的、启示录般的光，
它带着炫目的幻象
击中红种人黑夜的天顶。堡垒在烟里消融，
可恨的大道被掀起、飘散、崩断，
化成一撮尘土；白人成了一段传说；
绿色、洁净的草原，山峦和溪谷，
轰响着养肥的野牛；美好的旧时光
在清晨一场不可阻挡的
烈焰里冲了回来！

在那个一切梦想都真实、
奇迹司空见惯的季节，
做青年真好！将尽的五月
听得见年轻人们一路唱着歌
投奔红云。虔诚的儿子和放荡的浪子，
未驯的小马驹、营地里招摇的丑闻，
还有从未被忧患磨砺的大眼梦想家，

===Original===

One-hearted with the same wild surge of life,

Sang merrily of dying as they came.

Aloof amid his solitude of fame,

The battle-brooding chieftain heard, to dream

Of great hordes raging like a flooded stream

From Powder River to the Greasy Grass,

That never after might a wagon pass

Along that hated highway of deceit.
The meadows of Absoraka grew sweet

With nursing June. War-ponies, winter-thin,

Nuzzled the dugs of ancient might therein

Against the day of victory. July

Poured virile ardor from a ruthless sky

To make stern forage﻿—that the hardened herds

Might speed as arrows, wheel and veer as birds,

Have smashing force and never lack for breath,

Be fit for bearing heroes to their death

In that great day now drawing near.
Meanwhile

Once more the solitude of Piney Isle

Was startled with a brawl of mules and men.

The Long Knives’ wagons clattered there again;

The axes bit and rang, saws whined and gnawed;

And mountain valleys wakened to applaud

The mighty in their downfall, meanly slain.
Now close to Piney Isle there lay a plain

Some three long bow-shots wide. Good grazing land

It was, and empty as a beggar’s hand.

Low foothills squatted round with bended knees,

And standing mountains waited back of these

To witness what the hunkered hills might view.

They saw a broad arena roofed with blue

That first of August. Where the mid-plain raised

A little knoll, the yellow swelter blazed

On fourteen wagon-beds set oval-wise﻿—

A small corral to hold the camp supplies,

Flour, salt, beans, ammunition, grain in sacks.

Therein, forestalling sudden night attacks,

The mules were tethered when the gloaming starred

The laggard evening. Soldiers, sent to guard

The logging crew, had pitched their tents around.

And all of this was like a feeble sound

Lost in the golden fanfare of the day.

Across the Piney Fork, a mile away,

Unseen among the pines, the work-camp stood;

And trundling thence with loads of winter wood,

Stript wagon-trucks creaked fortward.
Twilight awe

Among the pines now silenced axe and saw.

With jingling traces, eager for their grain,

Across the creek and up the gloaming plain

The work mules came, hee-hawing at the glow

Of fires among the tents. The day burned low

To moonless dusk. The squat hills seemed to lift,

Expectant. Peaks on shadow-seas adrift,

Went voyaging where lonely wraiths of cloud

===Chinese===

同一条生命的野浪把他们连成一心，
一路欢唱着死亡而来。
超然于名声的孤寂之中，
临战沉思的酋长听着，梦想
大队人马像泛滥的洪流
从波德河奔腾直到油腻草滩，
从此再没有一辆大车
能通过那条可恨的欺骗的大道。
阿布萨罗卡的草场在哺乳的
六月里转为甘美。战马，
冬天里消瘦的，在那里
吮着古老力量的乳头，为胜利之日
积攒。七月从无情的天空
倾下雄烈的炎威，催出严酷的草料——
好让练硬了的马群
疾如飞箭、如鸟一般回旋闪转，
有雷霆之力而气永不竭，
配得上驮着英雄们去赴死，
在那正临近的伟大一日。

与此同时，
派尼岛的荒寂又一次
被骡子和人的喧闹惊起。
长刀们的货车又在那里哐当作响；
斧子咬进、铿鸣，锯子哀叫着啃噬；
而山间的谷地醒来，为强梁者
卑贱地被杀、坠落的下场喝彩。
派尼岛近旁有一片平原，
约三箭之地宽。那本是好牧场，
却空得像乞丐的手掌。
低矮的山丘屈膝蹲踞在四周，
直立的高山在它们背后等候，
要见证蹲伏的丘峦所看见的一切。
它们看见一座蓝穹为顶的广阔竞技场，
在八月初一。平原中央
隆起一座小丘，黄色的酷热
灼照着十四节摆成椭圆的货车厢——
一座小小的围栏，圈着营地的给养：
面粉、盐、豆子、弹药、成袋的谷子。
为了防备突如其来的夜袭，
当暮光给迟缓的傍晚缀满星子，
骡子都拴在里面。奉命
护卫伐木队的士兵，在四周扎下了帐篷。
而这一切只像一声微弱的音响，
湮没在白昼金色的号角里。

隔着派尼河汊一英里远，
松林中隐着无人看见的工营；
装满冬柴的脱篷货车
从那里嘎吱着滚向堡垒。
黄昏的敬畏在松林里
静了斧子和锯子。挽具叮当，
惦着各自的谷料，工骡们
趟过小溪、爬上暮色中的平原，
朝着帐篷间的火光嘶鸣。
白日烧低成无月的薄暮。
蹲踞的丘峦似乎挺起身来，
期待着。群峰漂在影海之上，
航行到孤独的云魂出没
星空静谧的地方。

===Original===

Haunted the starry hushes. Bugs grew loud

Among the grasses; cynic owls laughed shrill;

Men slept. But all night long the wolves were still,

Aware of watchers in the outer dark.

And now and then a sentry’s dog would bark,

Rush snarling where it seemed that nothing stirred.

But those who listened for a war-cry, heard

The skirling bugs, the jeering owls, the deep

Discordant snoring of the men asleep

Upon their guns, mules blowing in the hay.
At last the blanching summits saw the day.

A drowsy drummer spread the news of morn.

The mules began to nicker for their corn

And wrangle with a laying back of ears.

Among them went the surly muleteers,

Dispensing feed and sulphurous remarks.

The harness rattled, and the meadow larks

Set dawn to melody. A sergeant cried

The names of heroes. Common men replied,

Sing-songing down the line. The squat hills heard

To seize and gossip with the running word﻿—

*Here! Here! Here!* Coffee steaming in the pot,

Woodsmoke and slabs of bacon, sizzling hot,

Were very good to smell. The cook cried “chuck!”

And when the yellow flood of sunrise struck

The little prairie camp, it fell on men

Who ate as though they might not eat again.

Some wouldn’t, for the day of wrath arose.

And yet, but for a cruising flock of crows,

The basking world seemed empty.
Now the sun

Was two hours high. The axes had begun

Across the Piney yonder. Drowsy draws

Snored with the lagging echoes of the saws.

The day swooned windless, indolently meek.

It happened that the pickets by the creek

Were shaken from a doze by rhythmic cries

And drumming hoofs. Against the western skies,

Already well within a half a mile,

Came seven Indians riding single file,

Their wiry ponies flattened to the quirt.

A sentry’s Springfield roared, and hills, alert

With echoes, fired a ghostly enfillade.

The ball fell short, bit dust and ricocheted.

The foremost pony, smitten in the breast,

Went down amid the rearing of the rest

And floundered to a dusty somersault.

Unhurt, the tumbled brave emerged to vault

Behind a comrade; and the seven veered

To southward, circling round the spot they feared

Where three far-stinging human hornets stood.

Now one of these went running to the wood

To see what made the logging camp so still.

Short breath sufficed to tell the tale of ill

He brought﻿—the whole crew making off in stealth

===Chinese===

虫子在草丛里越叫越响；
愤世的鸮鸟尖声发笑；
人们睡了。但狼整夜不作声，
知道外头的黑暗里有守望的人。
哨兵的狗不时吠起来，
冲着似乎毫无动静的地方低吼。
而那些侧耳等着战吼的人，只听见
虫子的尖鸣、鸮鸟的讪笑、
伏在枪上睡熟的人们
深沉而不和谐的鼾声、
干草里喷着响鼻的骡子。

终于，泛白的峰顶望见了白昼。
一个睡眼惺忪的鼓手把清晨的消息
敲播开去。骡子们开始
嘶着讨玉米，放平了耳朵相互怄气。
脾气乖戾的赶骡人在它们中间穿行，
分发着草料和火气冲天的话。
挽具哗啦啦响，草地上的百灵
给黎明配上了曲调。一个中士
点着英雄们的名字。寻常士兵
顺着队列唱名似的应答。蹲踞的丘峦
听着，抓住那奔跑的口令去传话——
*到！到！到！*壶里的咖啡冒着热气，
柴烟和滋滋作响的培根厚片，
闻起来好极了。厨子喊了声“开饭！”
当日出黄色的洪流击中
这座小小的草原营地，它正落在
一些吃得仿佛再没有下一顿的人身上。
有些人吃不下，因为震怒之日已经升起。

然而，若不是一群盘旋的乌鸦，
这晒着太阳的世界仿佛空无一人。

此刻太阳
已升起两个小时。那边的派尼河对岸
斧子已经开工。困倦的洼地
伴着锯子迟滞的回声打鼾。
这日子无风，慵懒温顺得发晕。

恰在这时，小溪边的哨兵
被有节奏的呼喊和
擂鼓般的蹄声从瞌睡里震醒。衬着西天，
已在半英里之内，来了
七个印第安人，成一列纵队奔驰，
坚韧的小马被马鞭压平了身子。
哨兵的斯普林菲尔德枪轰然一响，丘峦
警觉地用回声打出一排幽灵般的纵射。
子弹落近了，啃了口尘土又弹起来。
最前面那匹小马胸口中弹，
在其余马匹的人立中倒下，
翻滚成一身尘土的筋斗。
摔下马的勇士没有受伤，翻身
跳上同伴的马臀；那七骑
转向南，兜着圈子避开他们害怕的
三只远远蜇人的黄蜂立脚处。
其中一人跑向树林，
去看伐木营为什么这样安静。
一口气就够他讲完他带回的
凶讯——全队人正偷偷溜走

===Original===

And going to the mountains for their health,

The mules stampeded!
Things were looking blue.

With shaking knees, uncertain what to do,

The pickets waited. Whisperings of death

Woke round them, and they felt the gusty breath

Of shafts that plunked and quivered in the sod.

As though men sprouted where the ponies trod,

The circling band now jeered them, ten to one.

They scanned the main camp swinking in the sun.

No signal to return! But all the men

Were rushing round there, staring now and then

To where the foothills, northward, broke the flat.
A pointing sentry shouted: “Look at that!

Good God! There must be thousands over there!”

Massed black against the dazzle of the air,

They made the hilltops crawlingly alive﻿—

The viscid boiling over of a hive

That feels the pale green burning of the spring.

Slow-moving, with a phasic murmuring

As of a giant swarm gone honey-wild,

They took the slope; and still the black rear piled

The wriggling ridges. What could bar the way?

Dwarfed in the panorama of the day,

The camp was but a speck upon the plain.

And three remembered eighty lying slain

Beside a ford, and how the Winter strode

Numb-footed down a bloody stretch of road

Across strange faces lately known and dear.
“I guess we’d better hustle out of here,”

The sergeant said. To left, to right, in front,

Like starving kiotes singing to the hunt,

Yet overcautious for a close attack,

Scores pressed the fighting trio, falling back

Across the Piney campward. One would pause

To hold the rear against the arrow-flaws,

The pelting terror, while the two ran past;

Then once again the first would be the last,

The second, first. And still the shuttling hoofs

Wove closelier with gaudy warps and woofs

The net of death; for still from brush and break

The Piney, like a pregnant water snake,

Spewed venomous broods.
So fleeing up the slope

The pickets battled for the bitter hope

Of dying with their friends. And there was one

Who left the wagon boxes at a run

And, dashing past the now exhausted three,

Knelt down to rest his rifle on his knee

And coolly started perforating hides.

Bare ponies, dragging warriors at their sides

And kicking at the unfamiliar weight,

Approved his aim. The weaving net of hate

Went loose, swung wide to southward.
So at last

They reached the camp where, silent and aghast,

===Chinese===

说是进山去养病——
骡子全炸了群！
局面看来不妙。
哨兵们膝盖发颤，不知如何是好，
只好等着。死亡的窃窃私语
在四周醒来，他们感到箭杆
扎进草皮、颤动时扇起的风。
仿佛小马蹄印所过之处就有人冒出来，
兜着圈的队伍这时嘲弄着他们，十对一。
他们张望在烈日下挥汗的主力营。
没有召回的信号！可那边所有的人
都跑来跑去，不时望向
北面山丘截断平原之处。

一个哨兵指过去，喊：“看那边！
天哪！那边准有好几千人！”
黑压压地衬在炫目的天光里，
他们叫山顶蠕蠕地活了过来——
像一窝尝到春天淡绿灼烧的
蜂群，黏稠地沸腾四溢。
缓缓地移动，带着一阵一阵的嗡鸣，
像一大群为蜜发狂的蜂，
他们漫下坡地；黑色的后队仍在
给蠕动的山脊层层加码。谁能挡路？

在白昼的全景里显得渺小，
营地不过是平原上的一个斑点。
那三个人想起倒在渡口边的
八十具尸体，想起冬天怎样
拖着冻僵的双脚走过一段血路，
跨过那些新近才熟识、才亲近的陌生脸孔。
“我看咱们最好赶紧离开这儿，”
中士说。左边、右边、前头，
像饿狼唱着围猎的歌，
却又过分谨慎不敢近逼，
几十个人压迫着这战斗的三人，
他们且战且退，过派尼河奔营地。
一个人停下
顶着箭的横风、顶着密集的恐怖
断后，另两人跑过去；
随后第一个又变成末一个，
第二个又变成头一个。穿梭的蹄子
仍用花哨的经纬把
死亡的网织得更密；因为灌木和断崖间，
派尼河仍像一条怀胎的水蛇，
吐出一窝窝毒种。

就这样逃上坡地，
哨兵们为一个苦涩的希望而战：
同自己的伙伴死在一处。而有一个人
从货车箱那里拔腿奔出，
冲过已经力竭的三人，
单膝跪下，把步枪架在膝上，
冷静地开始给皮肉钻窟窿。
光背的小马，拖着挂在侧面的战士，
踢蹬着这不习惯的重量，
都称许他的枪法。仇恨织成的网
松了，甩向南面。

终于
他们到了营地，那里人们

===Original===

The men stood round and stared with haunted eyes.

’Tis said a man sees much before he dies.

Were these not dying? O the eighty-one

Bestrewn down Lodge Trail Ridge to Peno Run

That blizzard evening! Here were thirty-two!

And no one broached what everybody knew﻿—

The tale there’d be and maybe none to tell

But glutted crows and kiotes. Such a spell

As fastens on a sick room gripped the crowd﻿—

When tick by tick the doctor’s watch is loud,

With hours between. And like the sound of leaves

Through which a night-wind ominously grieves,

The murmur of that moving mass of men

To northward rose and fell and rose again,

More drowsing music than a waking noise.
And Captain Powell spoke: “Get ready, boys;

Take places; see their eyes, then shoot to kill.”
Some crouched behind the boxes, staring still

Like men enchanted. Others, seeming fain

To feel more keenly all that might remain

Of ebbing life, paced nervously about.

One fortified the better side of doubt

With yokes of oxen. That was Tommy Doyle.

(Alas, the total profit of his toil

Would be a hot slug crunching through his skull!)

And Littman yonder, grunting in the lull,

Arranged a keg of salt to fight behind;

While Condon, having other things in mind

Than dying, wrestled with a barrel of beans.

And others planned escape by grimmer means.

Old Robertson, with nothing in his face,

Unlaced a boot and noosed the leather lace

To reach between a trigger and a toe.

He did not tell, and no one asked to know

The meaning of it. Everybody knew.

John Grady and McQuarie did it too,

And Haggirty and Gibson did the same,

And many others. When the finish came,

At least there’d be no torturing for them.
Now as a hail-cloud, fraying at the hem,

Hurls ragged feelers to the windless void,

The nearing mass broke vanward and deployed

To left and right﻿—a dizzy, flying blear,

Reek of a hell-pot boiling in the rear.

And now, as when the menaced world goes strange

And cyclone sling-shots, feeling out the range,

Spatter the waiting land agape with drouth,

The few first arrows fell. Once more the south

Was humming with a wind of mounted men

That wove the broken net of death again

Along the creek and up the campward rise.
Then suddenly, with wolfish battle-cries

And death-songs like the onset of a gale

And arrows pelting like a burst of hail,

The living tempest broke. There was no plain;

===Chinese===

人们围站着，瞪着中了邪似的眼睛。
据说人死之前会看见许多东西。
这些人不正在死吗？哦那八十一个人，
横陈在柴道岭到佩诺溪之间，
在那个暴风雪的傍晚！这里是三十二个人！
而谁也不去捅破人人知道的事——
将会有怎样的故事，也许再没有人讲，
只有吃撑的乌鸦和郊狼。像粘住
病房的那种符咒攫住了人群——
那时医生的表滴答作响，
两点之间隔着几个钟头。而像夜风
悲泣着穿过树叶的声响，
北面那大片移动的人马发出的
低沉喧响起了又落、落了又起，
更像催眠的音乐，不像醒着的噪音。

这时鲍威尔上尉说话了：“准备，小伙子们；
各就各位；看清他们的眼睛再往死里打。”
有些人蹲到车厢后面，仍旧发怔，
像中了妖法的人。另一些人，仿佛
急着把残生将尽的一切感觉得更深，
不安地踱来踱去。
有个人用几对牛轭加固了
疑心的稳妥一面。那是汤米·多伊尔。
（唉，他这番辛苦的全部收益，
将是一颗滚烫的弹头嘎扎一声钻进他的头颅！）
而那边利特曼，在间歇里咕哝着，
搬来一桶盐挡在身前打仗；
康登呢，心里想着死以外的
别的事，同一桶豆子搏斗。
还有人谋划着用更冷酷的办法脱身。

老罗伯逊，脸上什么也看不出来，
解开一只靴子，把皮靴带
结成活套，一头够到扳机和脚趾。
他不说，也没有人问
这是什么意思。人人都懂。
约翰·格雷迪和麦夸里也这么办了，
哈格蒂和吉布森照办不误，
还有许多别的人。末日来临时，
他们至少不会被拷打了。

这时，像下摆磨损的冰雹云
把破烂的触须抛向无风的虚空，
逼近的大队在先锋处散开，
向左右铺展——一阵晕眩的、飞掠的迷乱，
后排是地狱锅里沸腾的秽气。
而现在，像受威胁的世界变得异样、
龙卷风的弹弓试探着射程，
把干渴大张着等待的土地打得斑斑点点，
最初几支箭落了下来。南面
又一次哼响起来，带着骑手的风，
沿着小溪、顺着营地前的坡地，
把破碎的死亡之网重新织起。
随后突然间，伴着狼嚎般的战吼、
像狂风袭来一样的死亡之歌、
像冰雹骤降一样密集的箭，
活的风暴炸开了。平原不见了；

===Original===

Just headgear bobbing in a toss of mane,

And horses, horses, horses plunging under.

Paunch-deep in dust and thousand-footed thunder,

That vertigo of terror swarmed and swirled

About the one still spot in all the world﻿—

The hushed cyclonic heart. Then that was loud!

The boxes bellowed, and a spurting cloud

Made twilight where the flimsy fortress stood;

And flying splinters from the smitten wood

And criss-cross arrows pricked the drifting haze.

Not now, as in the recent musket days,

The foe might brave two volleys for a rush

Upon the soldiers, helpless in a hush

Of loading. Lo, like rifles in a dream

The breech-fed Springfields poured a steady stream

That withered men and horses roaring in!

And gut-shot ponies screamed above the din;

And many a wounded warrior, under-trod

But silent, wallowed on the bloody sod﻿—

Man piled on man and horses on the men!
They broke and scattered. Would they come again?

Abruptly so the muted hail-storm leaves

Astonished silence, when the dripping eaves

Count seconds for the havoc yet to come.

Weird in the hush, a melancholy hum,

From where the watching women of the Sioux

Thronged black along the circling summits, grew

And fell and grew﻿—the mourning for the dead.
One whispered hoarsely from a wagon-bed,

“Is anybody hit?” But none replied.

Awe-struck at what they did and hollow-eyed,

All watched and waited for the end of things.
Then even as the fleeing hail-cloud swings

Before some freakish veering of the gale,

Returning down its desolated trail

With doubled wrath, the howling horsemen came.

Right down upon the ring of spurting flame

The quirted ponies thundered; reared, afraid

Of that bad medicine the white men made,

And, screaming, bolted off with flattened ears.

So close the bolder pressed, that clubs and spears

Were hurled against the ring.
Again they broke,

To come again. Now flashing through the smoke,

Like lightning to the battle’s thunder-shocks,

Ignited arrows, streaming to the nocks,

Fell hissing where the fighting soldiers lay;

And flame went leaping through the scattered hay

To set the dry mule-litter smouldering.
Half suffocated, coughing with the sting

Of acrid air, like scythemen in a field

The soldiers mowed. And gaudy man-flower reeled

To wriggling swaths. And still the mad Sioux fought

To break this magic that the white men wrought﻿—

===Chinese===

只剩下浮沉的鬃毛里颠动的帽缨，
和马、马、马在底下翻腾。
没腹于尘土和千足的雷霆，
那晕眩的恐怖围着全世界
唯一静止的一点打旋——
飓风寂静的风眼。随后那一点也响了起来！
货车箱怒吼，喷涌的硝烟
把那座单薄的堡垒罩成黄昏；
被击碎的木片横飞，
纵横的箭在浮动的烟霭里穿扎。

再不像不久前的滑膛枪时代，
敌人可以硬挨两排枪弹，
扑向装弹间隙里束手的士兵。
看哪，后膛供弹的斯普林菲尔德
像梦里的连珠枪，倾出稳定的弹流，
把吼叫着冲进来的人和马成片扫倒！
腹破的小马在喧声之上嘶喊；
许多负伤的勇士被踩在底下
却不作声，在血染的草皮上翻滚——
人压着人，马压着人！
他们溃散了。还会再来吗？

喑哑的冰雹就是这样骤然收场，
留下错愕的寂静，滴水的檐口
为着还没来的大祸数着秒。
静默中有一种怪异的、哀伤的嗡鸣，
从苏族观战的女人们
黑压压挤满四周山顶的地方
升起、落下、又升起——那是哀悼死者。
一节车厢里有人沙哑地低声问：
“有人挂彩没有？”没人回答。
众人被自己的作为震住，眼窝深陷，
全都望着、等着事情的结局。

随后，恰像逃逸的雹云
被一阵邪门的狂风兜转，
顺着它自己蹂躏过的路
加倍狂怒地折返，呼啸的骑手们来了。
鞭笞的小马径直朝着那圈喷火的
阵地雷鸣而至；又惊得人立，
怕白人弄出的那种坏巫术，
尖叫着放平耳朵炸开。胆大的逼得那样近，
短棍和长矛都掷向了车阵。

他们又溃散，
好再来。此刻穿透硝烟，
像战斗雷鸣中的道道闪电，
燃着的箭曳着火尾嗡嗡而下，
落在伏地射击的士兵中间；
火焰跳过散乱的干草，
把干燥的骡粪堆焖燃。
士兵们呛得半窒息，咳着
刺鼻的空气，像田里的割草人
挥割。绚丽的人之花成片
倒伏扭滚。而疯狂的苏族仍在攻打，
要破掉白人施展的这妖法——

===Original===

Heroic flesh at grapple with a god.
Then noon was glaring on the bloody sod;

And broken clouds of horsemen down the plain

Went scudding; hundreds, heavy with the slain

And wounded, lagging in the panic rout.
Again the ridges murmured round about

Where wailed the wives and mothers of the Sioux.

Some soldier whispered, asking for a chew,

As though he feared dread sleepers might arise.

Young Tommy Doyle with blood upon his eyes

Gaped noonward and his fighting jaw sagged loose.

Hank Haggirty would never need a noose

To reach between a trigger and a toe.

Jenness would never hear a bugle blow

Again, so well he slept. Around the ring

Men passed the grisly gossip, whispering﻿—

As though doomed flesh were putting on the ghost.
A sound grew up as of a moving host.

It seemed to issue from a deep ravine

To westward. There no enemy was seen.

A freak gust, gotten of a sultry hush,

May mumble thus among the distant brush

Some moments ere a dampened finger cools.

But still the smudgy litter of the mules

Plumed straight against the dazzle of the day.

Upon a hilltop half a mile away

To eastward. Red Cloud presently appeared

Among his chieftains, gazing where the weird

Susurrus swelled and deepened in the west;

And to and from him dashed along the crest

Fleet heralds of some new-begotten hope.
Once more the Piney spread along the slope

A dizzy ruck of charging horse. They broke

Before those stingers in a nest of smoke,

Fled back across the creek, and waited there.

For what?
The voice of it was everywhere﻿—

A bruit of waters fretting at a weir.

The woman-peopled summits hushed to hear

That marching sound.
Then suddenly a roar,

As from the bursting open of a door,

Swept out across the plain; and hundreds, pressed

By hundreds crowding yonder from the west,

Afoot and naked, issued like a wedge,

With Red Cloud’s nephew for the splitting edge,

A tribe’s hot heart behind him for a maul.
Slow, ponderously slow, the V-shaped wall

Bore down upon the camp. The whirlwind pace

Of horsemen seemed less terrible to face

Than such a leisure. Brave men held their breath

Before that garish masquerade of Death

Aflaunt with scarlets, yellows, blues and greens.
Then Condon there behind his barrel of beans,

Foreseeing doom, afraid to be afraid,

Sprang up and waved his rifle and essayed

Homeric speech according to his lights.

===Chinese===

英雄的血肉在与一位神角力。
随后正午灼照着血染的草皮；
溃云般的骑队顺着平原
疾掠而去；成百的人驮着
死者和伤者，在慌乱的大溃退中掉队。
四周的山脊又嗡嗡响了起来，
苏族的妻子和母亲在那里哭嚎。
有个士兵悄声讨一口嚼烟，
仿佛怕惊醒可怖的沉睡者。
年轻的汤米·多伊尔，血糊了双眼，
朝着正午的方向张望，咬合的下巴松垂了。
汉克·哈格蒂再不需要一根绳套
去连结扳机和脚趾了。
詹尼斯再也听不见军号吹响，
他睡得太沉。沿着车阵，
人们低声传递着可怕的闲话——
仿佛注定的血肉正在披上鬼魂。
一阵声音起来了，像大军在移动。

它似乎发自西边一道深深的冲沟。
那边看不见一个敌人。
闷热寂静里生出的一阵怪风，
会在远处的灌木里这样咕哝，
在蘸湿的手指凉下去之前响那么几刻。
可骡粪的浊烟仍旧笔直地
在白昼的炫光里竖着羽冠。
东边半英里外的一座山顶上，
红云随即出现在众酋长中间，
凝望那怪异的沙沙声在西边
涨大、变深；轻快的传令兵
载着某种新生的希望，在他身侧
沿山顶来回疾驰。派尼河边，
坡地上又一次铺开晕眩的冲锋马潮。
他们在那烟巢里的蜇刺者面前溃散，
逃回小溪对岸，在那里等候。

等什么？
那声音到处都是——
像水在堰口焦躁的哗响。
挤满妇人的山顶静下来听
那行军的声音。随后一声吼，
像一扇门猛然撞开，
扫过平原；成百的人，
被从西边涌来的成百的人推挤着，
徒步、赤裸，楔形涌出，
楔刃是红云的侄子，
楔背是一整族滚烫的心当大锤。
缓慢，沉得可怕的缓慢，V 字的人墙
向营地压来。骑手们旋风般的速度
似乎还没有这样的从容可怖。
勇士们在死神那炫丽的化装舞会前
屏住了呼吸——
猩红、明黄、宝蓝、翠绿招展。
这时康登，在他那桶豆子后面，
预见了末日，又怕自己害怕，
一跃而起，挥着步枪，按他自己的
见识，尝试了一篇荷马式的演说。

===Original===

“Come on!” he yelled, “ye dairty blatherskites,

Ye blitherin’ ijuts! We kin lick yez all,

Ye low-down naygurs!” Shafts began to fall

About him raging. Scattered muskets roared

Along the fraying fringes of the horde.

“Get down there, Jim!” men shouted. “Down!” But Jim

Told Death, the blackguard, what he thought of him

For once and all.
Again the Springfields crashed;

And where the heavy bullets raked and smashed

The solid front and bored the jostling mass,

Men withered down like flame-struck prairie grass;

But still the raging hundreds forged ahead

Pell mell across their wounded and their dead,

Like tumblebugs. The splitting edge went blunt.

A momentary eddy at the front

Sucked down the stricken chief. The heavy rear,

With rage more mighty than the vanward fear,

Thrust forward. Twenty paces more, and then﻿—

’Twould be like drowning in a flood of men.

Already through the rifts one saw their eyes,

Teeth flashing in the yawn of battle-cries,

The sweat-sleek muscles straining at the bows.
Forgotten were the nooses for the toes.

Tomorrows died and yesterdays were naught.

Sleep-walkers in a foggy nowhere fought

With shadows. So forever from the first,

Forever so until this dream should burst

Its thin-blown bubble of a world. And then,

The shadows were a howling mass of men

Hurled, heavy with their losses, down the plain

Before that thunder-spew of death and pain

That followed till the last had disappeared.

The hush appalled; and when the smoke had cleared,

Men eyed each other with a sense of shock

At being still alive.
’Twas one o’clock!
One spoke of water. Impishly the word

Went round the oval, mocking those who heard.

The riddled barrel had bled from every stave;

And what the sun-stewed coffee-kettles gave

Seemed scarcely wet.
 Off yonder on the hill

Among his chieftains Red Cloud waited still﻿—

A tomcat lusting for a nest of mice.

How often could these twenty-nine suffice

To check his thousands? Someone raised a sight

And cursed, and fell to potting at the height;

Then others. Red Cloud faded into air.
What fatal mischief was he brewing there?

What ailed the Fort? It seemed beyond belief

That Wessels yonder wouldn’t send relief!

The hush bred morbid fancies. Battle-cries

Were better than this buzzing of the flies

About Jenness and Haggirty and Doyle.

Wounds ached and smarted. Shaken films of oil

===Chinese===

“来啊！”他吼道，“你们这些脏嘴的废物，
穷嚷嚷的蠢货！爷们能把你们全舔趴下，
你们这些下三滥的黑货！”箭雨
开始狂怒地落在他四周。零星的滑膛枪
沿着人群磨破的边缘轰响。
“趴下，吉姆！”人们喊。“趴下！”可吉姆
把他对死神——那个恶棍——的看法
一口气骂了个痛快。

斯普林菲尔德又连声炸响；
重型子弹耙过、砸烂
坚实的正面，钻透推挤的人群，
人像遭火的草原草一样成片枯倒；
可狂怒的人群仍旧向前涌，
越过他们自己的死者和伤者乱冲，
像屎壳郎滚粪球。楔刃钝了。
正面一阵短暂的漩涡
把中箭的酋长吸了下去。沉重的后队，
怒气比前锋的恐惧更猛，
往前硬顶。再进二十步，那么——
就会像溺死在人的洪峰里。
透过缝隙已经看得见他们的眼睛、
战吼大张的嘴里闪亮的牙齿、
汗光发亮的拉弓的肌肉。
脚趾上的绳套都被忘掉了。
明天死了，昨天也不算数。
梦游者在雾茫茫的乌有之乡
同影子作战。

从太初起就这样，直到这个梦
吹破它薄薄的世界的皂泡，
永远这样。随后，
影子变成一片嚎叫的人的洪流，
载着重重的死伤，被那
死亡和痛苦的雷霆喷沫
赶下平原，一直追到最后一个人
消失。寂静骇人；烟散之后，
人们面面相觑，震惊于
自己还活着。
一点钟了！
有人说起水。这词儿像淘气鬼
沿着椭圆车阵传了一圈，嘲弄着听见的人。
千疮百孔的水桶从每一块桶板渗血；
而烈日焖煮的咖啡壶里
倒出的东西湿都湿不够。

===Original===

Troubled the yellow dazzle of the grass.

The bended heavens were a burning glass

Malevolently focused. Minutes crawled.

Men gnawed their hearts in silence where they sprawled,

Each in the puddle of his own blue shade.
But hear! Was that a howitzer that bayed?

Look! Yonder from behind the eastward steep

Excited warriors, like a flock of sheep

That hear the wolves, throng down the creekward slope

And flee along the Piney!
Slow to hope,

Men searched each other’s faces, silent still.
A case-shot, bursting yonder on the hill,

Sent dogging echoes up the foe-choked draws.

And far hills heard the leather-lunged hurrahs

And answered, when the long blue skirmish line

Swept down the hill to join the twenty-nine

Knee-deep in standing arrows.

===Chinese===

搅浑了草色明黄的炫光。
弯垂的天穹是一块烧镜，
恶毒地聚着焦。时间爬行。
人们趴着，各自淹在自己
蓝阴阴的影子水洼里，无声地咬着心。
可听！那是一声榴弹炮在吠吗？
看！东边陡坡的背后，
激动的战士们像听见恶狼的
羊群，涌下临溪的坡地，
沿着派尼河溃逃！

迟迟不敢希望的人们
仍旧沉默地彼此察看脸色。
一发开花弹在那边山上炸开，
回声追着爬上被敌人塞满的冲沟。
远山听见了扯开嗓子的欢呼，
应和着，这时长长的蓝色散兵线
扫下山来，同那二十九个人会合——
他们齐膝立在插立的箭丛中。

## VII Beecher’s Island / 七 比彻岛

## VII Beecher’s Island /

## VII Beecher’s Island / 
===Original===

Summer turned.

Where blackbirds chattered and the scrub oaks burned

In meadows of the Milk and Musselshell,

The fatted bison sniffed the winter-smell

Beneath the whetted stars, and drifted south.

Across the Yellowstone, lean-ribbed with drouth,

The living rivers bellowed, morn to morn.

The Powder and the Rosebud and the Horn

Flowed backward freshets, roaring to their heads.

Now up across the Cheyenne watersheds

The manless cattle wrangled day and night.

Along the Niobrara and the White

Uncounted thirsts were slaked. The peace that broods

Aloof among the sandhill solitudes

Fled from the bawling bulls and lowing cows.

Along the triple Loup they paused to browse

And left the lush sloughs bare. Along the Platte

The troubled myriads pawed the sandy flat

And snorted at the evil men had done.

For there, from morning sun to evening sun,

A strange trail cleft the ancient bison world,

And many-footed monsters whirred and whirled

Upon it; many-eyed they blinked, and screamed;

Tempestuous with speed, the long mane streamed

Behind them; and the breath of them was loud﻿—

A rainless cloud with lightning in the cloud

And alien thunder.
 Thus the driving breed,

The bold earth-takers, toiled to make the deed

Audacious as the dream. One season saw

The steel trail crawl away from Omaha

As far as ox-rigs waddled in a day﻿—

An inchworm bound for San Francisco Bay!

The next beheld a brawling, sweating host

Of men and mules build on to Kearney Post

While spring greens mellowed into winter browns,

And prairie dogs were giving up their towns

To roaring cities. Where the Platte divides,

The metal serpent sped, with league-long strides,

Between two winters. North Platte City sprang

From sage brush where the prairie sirens sang

Of magic bargains in the marts of lust;

A younger Julesburg sprouted from the dust

To howl a season at the panting trains;

Cheyenne, begotten of the ravished plains,

All-hailed the planet as the steel clanged by.

And now in frosty vacancies of sky

The rail-head waited spring on Sherman Hill,

And, brooding further prodigies of will,

Blinked off at China.
 So the man-stream flowed

Full flood beyond the Powder River road﻿—

A cow path, hardly worth the fighting for.

Then let grass grow upon the trails of war,

Bad hearts be good and all suspicion cease!

Beside the Laramie the pipe of peace

===Chinese===

夏天转了向。
在黑鹂啁啾、矮栎如焚的
牛奶河和马斯尔谢尔河流域，
养肥的野牛在磨尖的星光下
嗅出了冬天的气味，向南游去。
黄石河对岸，干渴得肋骨嶙峋，
活的河流从早到晚咆哮。
波德河、玫瑰蕾河和大角河
倒流成一股股洪汛，吼向自己的源头。

这时，在夏延河流域的分水岭上，
无人的牛群昼夜奔逐。
沿着尼奥布拉勒河和白河，
数不清的渴得到了消解。高栖在
沙丘荒寂中的那种安宁，
从吼叫的公牛和低鸣的母牛身边逃走。
沿着三叉的卢普河，它们停下吃草，
把丰茂的洼沼啃得精光。沿着普拉特河，
不安的万千兽群刨着沙质的河滩，
对着人闯下的祸喷着响鼻。

因为那里，从日出到日落，
一条奇怪的路劈开了古老的野牛世界，
多足的怪物在路上嗡嗡疾转；
它们眨着许多眼睛，尖声喊叫；
速度掀成狂风，长长的鬃毛
飘在身后；它们的呼吸震耳——
一朵无雨的云，云里裹着闪电
和异样的雷声。
就这样，那个进取的族类，
大胆的夺地者，苦干着要让事迹
配得上梦想的大胆。一个季节里，
钢轨的路从奥马哈爬出
牛车一天摇晃的那么远——
一条奔向旧金山湾的尺蠖！

下一个季节看见吵嚷流汗的
人和骡子的大军续建卡尼堡，
春绿酿成冬褐，
草原犬鼠把自己的城镇
让给了轰鸣的城市。在普拉特河分汊处，
金属的巨蟒迈着里格长的大步，
在两个冬天之间疾驰。北普拉特城
从鼠尾草丛里蹦出，草原的海妖
曾在那里歌唱情欲市场上的魔幻交易；
一个年轻的朱尔斯堡从尘土里发芽，
朝着喘息的列车嚎了一个季节；
夏延城，被蹂躏的平原所生，
在钢轨铿然驶过时向星球欢呼。

而现在，在霜冻的天空空处，
轨头在谢尔曼山上等待春天，
一面盘算着意志的进一步奇迹，
一面向中国眨眼作别。
人潮就这样漫过
波德河大道，涌成满潮——
一条牛径，简直不值得争。
那就让草长上战争的旧路，
坏心变好、猜疑止息吧！
在拉勒米河边，和平的烟斗

===Original===

Awaited; let the chieftains come and smoke!
’Twas summer when the Great White Father spoke.

A thousand miles of dying summer heard;

And nights were frosty when the crane-winged word

Found Red Cloud on the Powder loath to yield.

The crop from that rich seeding of the field

Along the Piney flourished greenly still.

The wail of many women on a hill

Was louder than the word. And once again

He saw that blizzard of his fighting men

Avail as snow against the August heat.

“Go tell them I am making winter meat;

No time for talk,” he said; and that was all.
The Northwind snuffed the torches of the fall,

And drearily the frozen moons dragged past.

Then when the pasque-flower dared to bloom at last

And resurrected waters hailed the geese,

It happened that the flying word of peace

Came north again. The music that it made

Was sweet to Spotted Tail, and Man Afraid

Gave ear, bewitched. One Horn and Little Chief

Believed; and Two Bears ventured on belief,

And others who were powers in the land.

For here was something plain to understand:

As long as grass should grow and water flow,

Between Missouri River and the snow

That never melts upon the Big Horn heights,

The country would be closed to all the Whites.

So ran the song that lured the mighty south.

It left a bitter taste in Red Cloud’s mouth,

No music in his ears. “Go back and say

That they can take their soldier-towns away

From Piney Fork and Crazy Woman’s Creek

And Greasy Grass. Then maybe I will speak.

Great Spirit gave me all this country here.

They have no land to give.”
The hills went sere

Along the Powder; and the summer grew.

June knew not what the white men meant to do;

Nor did July. The end of August came.

Bullberries quickened into jets of flame

Where smoky bushes smouldered by the creeks.

Grapes purpled and the plums got rosy cheeks.

The nights were like a watching mother, yet

A chill as of incipient regret

Foretold the winter when the twilight fell.

’Twas then a story wonderful to tell

Went forth at last. In every wind it blew

Till all the far-flung bison hunters knew;

And Red Cloud’s name and glory filled the tale.

The soldier-towns along the hated trail

Were smoke, and all the wagons and the men

Were dust blown south! Old times had come again.

Unscared, the fatted elk and deer would roam

Their pastures now, the bison know their home

===Chinese===

正等着；让酋长们来抽吧！
大白父说话时正是夏天。
垂死的夏天在千里之外听见了；
鹤翼般的话语到达波德河上、
找到不肯让步的红云时，
夜里已经上了霜。派尼河边
那块沃土里播下的庄稼
依旧长得青青。
山冈上众多妇人的哭声
比话语更响。而他又一次
看见他那些战斗勇士的暴风雪
落进八月的热里，像雪一样无济于事。
“去告诉他们，我正备冬肉；
没工夫空谈，”他说；就这一句。
北风掐灭了秋天的火把，
沉闷地，冻结的月亮一个个拖过去。

后来白头银莲花终于敢开了，
复活的水声迎着雁阵，
恰在这时，飞驰的和平话语
又一次北来。它奏出的音乐
斑尾听着受用，惧父
中了魔似的侧耳。独角和小酋长
信了；双熊也大胆信了，
还有别的一方能人。
因为这里有一件明明白白的事：
只要草还长、水还流，
在密苏里河与常年不化地压在
大角山巅的积雪之间，
这片土地将对所有白人关闭。

引诱南方豪强的歌就这么唱。
这话在红云嘴里留着一股苦味，
耳朵里没有音乐。“回去说，
他们把军镇从派尼河汊、疯女溪
和油腻草滩撤走，
那时我或许开口。
大神灵把这整片土地给了我。
他们没有土地可以给。”

波德河沿岸的山峦转枯；
夏天往深处长。六月不知道
白人打算做什么；七月也不知道。
八月将尽。牛莓在溪边
烟蒙蒙的灌木里焖烧的地方
催成一簇簇火苗。
葡萄转紫，李子透出红晕。
夜像守望着的一位母亲，可是
一种初生悔意的寒意，
在暮色降临时预告着冬天。

就在那时，一个奇妙的故事
终于传了出去。它随风四起，
直到所有散处远方的猎牛人都知道；
红云的名字和光荣充满了这故事。
可恨的路沿线的军镇
成了烟，所有的大车和人
都成了吹向南方的尘土！旧日又回来了。
养肥的麋和鹿不再惊惶，
从此在牧场上游荡，野牛认得自己的家，

===Original===

And flourish there forever unafraid.

So when the victor’s winter-meat was made

And all his lodges ready for the cold,

He listened to the word, now twelve moons old,

Rode south and made his sign and had his will.
Meanwhile the road along the Smoky Hill

Was troubled. Hunters, drifting with the herd

The fall before, had scattered wide the word

Of Red Cloud’s victory. “Look north,” they said;

“The white men made a road there. It is red

With their own blood, and now they whine for peace!”

The brave tale travelled southward with the geese,

Nor dwindled on the way, nor lacked applause.

Comanches, South Cheyennes and Kiowas,

Apaches and the South Arapahoes

Were glad to hear. Satanta, Roman Nose,

Black Kettle, Little Raven heard﻿—and thought.

Around their winter fires the warriors fought

Those far-famed battles of the North again.

Their hearts grew strong. “We, too,” they said, “are men;

And what men did up yonder, we can do.

Make red the road along the Smoky too,

And grass shall cover it!”
So when the spring

Was fetlock-deep, wild news ran shuddering

Through Kansas: women captured, homes ablaze,

Men slaughtered in the country north of Hays

And Harker! Terror stalking Denver way!

Trains burned along the road to Santa Fe,

The drivers scalped and given to the flames!

All summer Panic babbled demon names.

No gloom but harbored Roman Nose, the Bat.

Satanta, like an omnipresent cat,

Moused every heart. Out yonder, over there,

Black Kettle, Turkey Leg were everywhere.

And Little Raven was the night owl’s croon,

The watch-dog’s bark. The setting of the moon

Was Little Rock; the dew before the dawn

A sweat of horror!
All that summer, drawn

By vague reports and captive women’s wails,

The cavalry pursued dissolving trails﻿—

And found the hotwind. Loath to risk a fight,

Fleas in the day and tigers in the night,

The wild bands struck and fled to strike anew

And drop the curtain of the empty blue

Behind them, passing like the wrath of God.
The failing year had lit the goldenrod

Against the tingling nights, now well begun;

The sunflowers strove to hoard the paling sun

For winter cheer; and leagues of prairie glowed

With summer’s dying flare, when fifty rode

From Wallace northward, trailing Roman Nose,

The mad Cheyenne. A motley band were those﻿—

Scouts, hunters, captains, colonels, brigadiers;

===Chinese===

从此在那儿永远无惧地繁盛。
于是，当胜者的冬肉备好、
所有帐篷都备妥御寒，
他听了那已经过了十二个月的话，
南下画押，如愿以偿。

与此同时，烟山大道
出了乱子。头年秋天随兽群
漂泊的猎手们，早已把红云
得胜的消息四处撒开。“往北看，”他们说；
“白人在那边修了条路。那路
让他们自己的血染红了，如今他们哭着求和！”
这勇敢的故事随雁阵南传，
一路不减，也不缺喝彩。
科曼切、南方夏延和基奥瓦、
阿帕奇和南方阿拉帕霍
都乐闻其事。萨坦塔、罗马鼻、
黑壶、小鸦听了——都动了心思。
在冬夜的火边，战士们把北方
那些赫赫有名的战役又打了一遍。
他们的心壮了。“我们也是人，”他们说；
“上头的人做得到的，我们也做得到。
把烟山路也染红，
让草把它盖掉！”

于是春天
刚没过距毛，狂野的消息就战栗着
跑遍堪萨斯：妇女被掳、房舍起火、
海斯和哈克以北的乡野
男人遭屠！恐怖在丹佛一带逡巡！
去圣菲的路上列车被焚，
车夫被剥了头皮扔进火里！
一夏天，恐慌念叨着群魔的名字。
没有一处阴暗不窝藏着罗马鼻那头蝙蝠。
萨坦塔像一只无处不在的猫，
把每一颗心当鼠玩。那边那边，
黑壶、火鸡腿无处不在。
小鸦是夜枭的咕哝、
看门狗的吠声。月亮的沉落
是小石；黎明前的露水
是一层恐怖的冷汗！

一夏天，被含糊的传闻
和被掳妇女的哀哭牵引着，
骑兵追逐着不断消散的踪迹——
只追上了热风。野性的部落不愿冒险一战，
白天是跳蚤、夜里是猛虎，
打一下就跑，好再打一下，
随手在身后放下空洞的蓝天
这道幕，像神的震怒一样掠过。
衰竭的一年点起了秋麒麟，
迎着刚开始刺骨的夜晚；
向日葵竭力攒着转白的太阳
以备冬寒；几十里草原辉耀着
夏天垂死的火焰，这时五十骑
从华莱士北上，追踪罗马鼻，
那个疯狂的夏延人。这一伙人真杂——
侦察兵、猎手、上尉、上校、准将；

===Original===

Wild lads who found adventure in arrears,

And men of beard whom Danger’s lure made young﻿—

The drift and wreckage of the great war, flung

Along the brawling border. Two and two,

The victor and the vanquished, gray and blue,

Rode out across the Kansas plains together,

Hearts singing to the croon of saddle leather

And jingling spurs. The buffalo, at graze

Like dairy cattle, hardly deigned to raise

Their shaggy heads and watch the horsemen pass.

Like bursting case-shot, clumps of bluejoint grass

Exploded round them, hurtling grouse and quail

And plover. Wild hens drummed along the trail

At twilight; and the antelope and deer,

Moved more by curiosity than fear,

Went trotting off to pause and gaze their fill.

Past Short Nose and the Beaver, jogging still,

They followed hot upon a trail that shrank

At every tangent draw. Their horses drank

The autumn-lean Republican and crossed;

And there at last the dwindled trail was lost

Where sandhills smoked against a windy sky.
Perplexed and grumbling, disinclined to try

The upper reaches of the stream, they pressed

Behind Forsyth, their leader, pricking west

With Beecher there beside him in the van.

They might have disobeyed a lesser man;

For what availed another wild goose chase,

Foredoomed to end some God-forsaken place

With twilight dying on the prairie rim?

But Fame had blown a trumpet over him;

And men recalled that Shenandoah ride

With Sheridan, the stemming of the tide

Of rabble armies wrecked at Cedar Creek,

When thirty thousand hearts, no longer weak,

Were made one victor’s heart.
And so the band

Pushed westward up the lonely river land

Four saddle days from Wallace. Then at last

They came to where another band had passed

With shoeless ponies, following the sun.

Some miles the new trail ran as lean creeks run

In droughty weather; then began to grow.

Here other hoofs had swelled it, there, travaux;

And more and more the circumjacent plains

Had fed the trail, as when torrential rains

Make prodigal the gullies and the sloughs,

And prairie streams, late shrunken to an ooze,

Appal stout swimmers. Scarcity of game

(But yesterday both plentiful and tame)

And recent pony-droppings told a tale

Of close pursuit. All day they kept the trail

And slept upon it in their boots that night

And saddled when the first gray wash of light

===Chinese===

有觉得冒险欠了账的野小子，
有被危险的诱饵重新变年轻的留须汉子——
都是那场大战的漂流与残骸，
抛在喧闹的边地。两人一排，
胜者与败者，灰与蓝，
并肩骑过堪萨斯平原，
心随着鞍皮的咕哝和马刺的
叮当歌唱。吃草的野牛
像奶牛一样，懒得抬一抬
蓬乱的头看骑队经过。
像开花弹炸开，一丛丛蓝节草
向四周爆响，掷出松鸡、鹌鹑
和鸻鸟。黄昏时野母鸡沿路扑翅；
羚羊和鹿，好奇多于恐惧，
小跑着走开又停下看过够。

过了短鼻溪和比弗河，仍在那儿小跑，
他们紧追一条遇岔谷就变细的踪迹。
马匹喝下秋瘦的共和河，蹚了过去；
终于，变细的踪迹在沙丘
迎着长风吐烟的地方消失了。
众人困惑、抱怨，不想去试
这条河的上游，却还是紧随
他们的领队福赛斯向西疾进，
比彻就在他身旁走在前头。
换个声望差些的人他们也许抗命；
因为再来一场追风捕影、
注定在上帝抛弃的某处、
在草原边缘的垂死暮色里收场的奔逐，
又有什么用？
但名声已在他头顶吹响过号角；
人们记得他随谢里登在谢南多亚
的那次驰奔，记得他在雪松溪
拦住溃兵狂潮的那一手——
那一天，三万颗不再软弱的心
被铸成一颗胜者的心。

于是这一小队
沿孤独的河谷向西推进，
离华莱士四个马程。终于，
他们来到另一支人马经过的地方——
不挂掌的小马，追着太阳走。
几英里内新踪迹像旱天
瘦细的溪流；随后开始变宽。
这里另一些蹄印把它加宽，那边是雪橇拖痕；
四周的草原越来越多地
给踪迹汇入支流，像暴雨
把冲沟和洼沼灌得慷慨，
草原的河流，前一阵还细成一线泥浆，
吓坏善泳的人。猎物稀少
（昨天还又多又不怕人）
和新鲜的马粪，讲出一个
紧追不舍的故事。他们循迹走了一天，
当晚和靴而眠，
当第一抹灰白的天光刚上山顶就备好了鞍

===Original===

Was on the hill tops. Past the North Fork’s mouth

It led, and, crossing over to the south,

Struck up the valley of the Rickaree﻿—

So broad by now that twenty, knee to knee,

Might ride thereon, nor would a single calk

Bite living sod.
Proceeding at a walk,

The troopers followed, awed by what they dared.

It seemed the low hills stood aloof, nor cared,

Disowning them; that all the gullies mocked

The jingling gear of Folly where it walked

The road to Folly’s end. The low day changed

To evening. Did the prairie stare estranged,

The knowing sun make haste to be away?

They saw the fingers of the failing day

Grow longer, groping for the homeward trail.

They saw the sun put on a bloody veil

And disappear. A flock of crows hurrahed.
Dismounting in the eerie valley, awed

With purple twilight and the evening star,

They camped beside the stream. A gravel bar

Here split the shank-deep Rickaree in two

And made a little island. Tall grass grew

Among its scattered alders, and there stood

A solitary sapling cottonwood

Within the lower angle of the sand.
No jesting cheered the saddle-weary band

That night; no fires were kindled to invoke

Tales grim with cannon flare and battle smoke

Remembered, and the glint of slant steel rolled

Up roaring steeps. They ate short rations cold

And thought about tomorrow and were dumb.
A hint of morning had begun to come;

So faint as yet that half the stars at least

Discredited the gossip of the east.

The grazing horses, blowing at the frost,

Were shadows, and the ghostly sentries tossed

Their arms about them, drowsy in the chill.
Was something moving yonder on the hill

To westward? It was there﻿—it wasn’t there.

Perhaps some wolfish reveller, aware

Of dawn, was making home. ’Twas there again!

And now the bubble world of snoring men

Was shattered, and a dizzy wind, that hurled

Among the swooning ruins of the world

Disintegrating dreams, became a shout:

“Turn out! Turn out! The Indians! Turn out!”

Hearts pounding with the momentary funk

Of cold blood spurred to frenzy, reeling drunk

With sleep, men stumbled up and saw the hill

Where shadows of a dream were blowing still﻿—
