Once more beneath the valor of his hand

The execrated troopers, blotted out,

Became a dust. Then, troubled with a doubt,

He ventured: “Uncle, will they find us here﻿—

The soldiers?” ’Twas a buzzing in the ear

Of Red Hawk where he brooded on his cast.

“The wind is coming up,” he said at last;

“The sky grows dusty.” “Then the fish won’t bits,”

Said Running Wolf. “There may be rain tonight”

Said Red Hawk, falling silent. Bravely then

The youngster wrought himself a world of men

Where nothing waited on a wind of whim,

But everything, obedient to him,

Fell justly. All the white men in the world

Were huddled there, and round about them swirled

More warriors than a grownup might surmise.

The pony-thunder and the battle-cries,

The whine of arrows eager for their marks

Drowned out the music of the meadowlarks,

The rising gale that teased the cottonwoods

To set them grumbling in their whitened hoods,

The chatter of a little waterfall.

These pebbles﻿—see!﻿—were Crazy Horse and Gall;

Here Crow King raged, and Black Moon battled there!

This yellow pebble﻿—look!﻿—was Yellow Hair;

This drab one with a little splotch of red,

The Gray Fox, Crook! Ho ho! And both were dead;

And white men fell about them every place﻿—

The leafage of the autumn of a race﻿—

Till all were down. And when their doom was sealed,

The little victor danced across the field

Amid the soundless singing of a throng.

The brief joy died, for there was something wrong

About this battle. Mournfully came back

That other picture of a dawn attack﻿—

The giant horses rearing in the fogs

Of their own breath; the yelping of the dogs;

The screaming rabble swarming up the rise;

The tangled terror in his mother’s eyes;

The flaming lodges and the bloody snow.

Provokingly oblivious of woe,

The two still eyed the waters and were dumb.
“But will they find us, Uncle? Will they come?”
Now Red Hawk grunted, heaving at his line,

And, wrought of flying spray and morning-shine,

A spiral rainbow flashed along the brook.

“*Hey hey!*” said Red Hawk, staring at his hook,

“He got my bait! Run yonder to the bluff

And catch some hoppers, Hohay. Get enough

===Chinese===

六月末的清晨，在油腻草滩上，
两个苏族战士去钓鱼；
两人沉默的寂静里，
一个孩子在沙上摆画着战役。
在他手威风的指挥下，遭诅咒的
骑兵又一次被抹掉，
化成一撮尘土。随后他起了疑，
壮着胆子问：“叔叔，他们会找到
这儿来吗——那些兵？”
这话在红鹰耳边嗡了一下，
他正盯着自己的钓线出神。
“起风了，”他末了说；
“天发尘了。”“那鱼就不咬钩了，”
奔狼说。“今夜也许有雨，”
红鹰说着，静了下来。于是那孩子
勇敢地给自己造了一个
人的世界，那里没有什么
要看任性的风色，
一切都听他的，
倒得其所。全世界的白人
都被圈在那里，四周旋着
比大人能猜出的还多的战士。
小马雷霆和战吼、
急着找靶的箭的啸叫，
淹掉了草地鹨的歌、
逗得三角叶杨在发白的
叶冠里噸哝的阵风、
一道小瀑布的絮语。
这些卵石——看！——是疯马和加尔；
这里乌鸦王在怒，黑月在那里战！
这颗黄卵石——瞧！——是黄毛；
这颗带一点红斑的灰的，
是灰狐克鲁克！嚯嚯！两个都死了；
白人在他们四周到处倒——
一个种族的秋天落叶——
直到全倒了。他们的劫数一封，
小小的胜者就在全场跳舞，
穿行在人群无声的歌唱中。

这点快意死了，因为这场战役
有点不对。哀哀地回来了
另一幅画，黎明突袭的画——
巨马在自己呵气的雾里
人立；狗在吠；
尖叫的人群涌上坡地；
他母亲眼里缠乱的恐怖；
燃烧的帐篷和血染的雪。
两个大人可恼地对灾祸
浑然不觉，只盯着水，不作声。
“可他们会找到我们吗，叔叔？会来吗？”
红鹰拽着钓线，哼了一声，
飞溅的水沫和晨光织成
一道螺旋的虹在小溪上闪过。
“*嘿-嘿*！”红鹰盯着鱼钩说，
“饵叫它叼了！跑到那边崖上
捉些蚂蚱来，霍海。捉够了，

===Original===

And you shall see how fish are caught today!”
Half-heartedly the youngster stole away

Across a brawling riffle, climbed the steep

And gazed across the panoramic sweep

Of rolling prairie, tawny in the drouth,

To where the Big Horns loomed along the south,

No more than ghosts of mountains in the dust.

Up here the hot wind, booming gust on gust,

Made any nook a pleasant place to dream.

You could not see the fishers by the stream;

And you were grown so tall that, looking down

Across the trees, you saw most all the town

Strung far along the valley. First you saw

The Cheyennes yonder opposite the draw

That yawned upon the ford﻿—a goodly sight!

So many and so mighty in a fight

And always faithful brothers to the Sioux!

Trees hid the Brulé village, but you knew

’Twas half a bow-shot long from end to end.

Then Ogalalas filled a river bend,

And next the Minneconjoux did the same.

A little farther south the Sans Arc came,

And they were neighbors to the Hunkpapas.﻿—

The blackened smoke-vents, flapping in the flaws,

Were like a startled crow flock taking wing.﻿—

Some Ogalalas played at toss-the-ring

And many idlers crowded round to see.﻿—

The grazing ponies wandered lazily

Along the flat and up the rolling west.
Now, guiltily remembering his quest,

He trotted farther up the naked hill,

Dropped down a gully where the wind was still﻿—

And came upon a hopping army there!

They swarmed, they raged﻿—but Hohay didn’t care;

For suddenly it seemed the recent climb

Had been a scramble up the height of time

And Hohay’s name was terror in the ears

Of evil peoples. Seizing weeds for spears,

He charged the soldiers with a dreadful shout.

The snapping of their rifles all about

Might daunt a lesser hero. Never mind;

His medicine made all their bullets blind,

And ’twas a merry slaughter. Then at last

The shining glory of the vision passed,

And hoppers were but hoppers as before,

And he, a very little boy once more,

Stood dwarfed and lonely on a windy rise.

The sun was nearly up the dusty skies.

’Twas white with heat and had a funny stare﻿—

All face! The wind had blown away its hair.

It looked afraid; as though the sun should fear!
Now, squinting downward through the flying blear,

He scanned the town. And suddenly the old

Remembered dawn of terror struck him cold.

Like startled ants that leave a stricken mound

===Chinese===

就让你看看今天鱼是怎么钓的！”
孩子没精打采地溜过
一道哗哗的浅滩，爬上陡坡，
眺望起伏草原的全景——
旱得焦黄——直到南边
大角山脉影影绰绰，
只是尘里几缕山的魂。
这上头热风一阵接一阵轰着，
哪个角落都是做梦的好地方。
你已经看不见溪边的钓鱼人；
而且你长得这么高了，隔着
树冠俯视，几乎看得见全城
沿河谷远远排开。先看见的是
冲着渡口打呵欠的
那道深谷对面的夏延营——好气派！
人这么多，打起仗来这么强，
对苏族永远是把忠心的兄弟！
树挡住了布鲁勒村，可你知道
它从这头到那头足有半箭之地。
然后奥加拉拉占了一道河湾，
米尼康朱接着又占一道。
再往南一点是桑萨克，
他们挨着洪克帕帕。——
熏黑的出烟口在阵风里拍打，
像一群惊起的乌鸦振翅。——
几个奥加拉拉人在玩套环，
许多闲人围着看。——
吃草的小马懒洋洋
沿着平川、向着起伏的西边游荡。
这时他愧然想起自己的差事，
小跑着上了秃山的更上头，
下到一条无风的冲沟——
撞见一支蹦跳的大军！
它们蜂拥、它们狂跳——霍海不在乎；
因为忽然间，仿佛方才那一爬
是攀上了时间的高处，
霍海的名字在邪恶之族的
耳朵里就是恐怖。他拔草当矛，
一声可怕的呐喊冲向那些兵。
四下步枪噼啪，
也许能吓退小一号的英雄。没关系；
他的巫术叫所有子弹全瞎了眼，
这是一场痛快的屠杀。终于
幻象闪亮的光荣退去了，
蚂蚱又只是蚂蚱，
他呢，又成了很小很小的男孩，
缩在多风的坡上，孤零零。
太阳快爬上布尘的天空了。
它热得发白，瞪着一副怪眼神——
全是脸！风把它的头发吹没了。
它像是害怕；太阳也会怕似的！
他眎眼透过飞沙往下
扫视城镇。突然，记忆里
那个恐怖的黎明把他激得冰凉。

===Original===

In silence that is felt as panic sound

By one who sees, the squaws and children poured

Along the valley northward past the ford;

And men were chasing ponies every place,

While many others ran, as in a race,

To southward.
Hohay, taking to his heels,

Made homeward like a cottontail that feels

A kiote pant and whimper at his tail.

He reached the bluff rim, scrambled to the vale

And crossed the stream. The fishermen were gone.

A hubbub in the village led him on

Pell-mell among the snatching underwood,

Till, checked as by a wall of sound, he stood

Apant and dripping in the howling town.
A bent old man there hobbled up and down

Upon a staff and sang a cackling song

Of how his heart was young again and strong;

But no one heeded. Women ran with guns

And bows and war clubs, screaming for their sons

And husbands. Men were mounting in a whirl

Of manes and tales to vanish in a swirl

Of scattered sand; and ever louder blew

The singing wind of warriors riding through

To battle. Hohay watched them, mouth agape,

Until he felt a hand upon his nape

That shoved him north, and someone shouted “*Run!*”

He scampered.
Meanwhile, nearer to the sun,

A rifle shot beyond the village end,

Came Reno’s troopers pouring round a bend,

Their carbines ready at their saddle bows.

A bugle yammered and a big dust rose

And horses nickered as the fours swung wide

In battle order; and the captains cried,

And with a running thunder of hurrahs

The long line stormed upon the Hunkpapas

Strung thin across the open flat. They fled

Like feeble ghosts of men already dead

Beneath the iron feet that followed there;

For now they deemed the far-famed Yellow Hair,

The Wolf of Washita, with all his pack

Potential in the dust cloud at his back,

Bore down upon them.
Flame along a slough

Before a howling wind, the terror grew

As momently increased the flying mass,

For all the others running up were grass

Before that flame; till men became aware

Of how another voice was booming there,

Outsoaring Panic’s, smashing through the brawl

Of hoofs and wind and rifles.
It was Gall.

A night wind blowing when the stars are dim,

His big black gelding panted under him;

And scarce he seemed a man of mortal race,

His naked body and his massive face

Serene as hewn from time-forgotten rock,

Despite the horse’s rearing to the shock

Of surging men. Boy-hearted warriors took

沿着河谷涌过渡口涌向北方；
到处有人追马，

奔向南边。
霍海撒腿就跑，
家去的一路像只觉出郊狼
在尾后喘气呜咽的棉尾兔。
他奔到崖沿，手脚并用下到谷底，
蹚过小溪。钓鱼的人不见了。
村里一片喧腾引着他
在乱抓的树丛里横冲直撞，
直到像被一堵声音的墙挡住，
他喘着、滴着水站在嚎叫的镇子里。
一个伛偻的老人拄着杖
在那里走来走去，哑着嗓子唱：
他的心又年轻又壮了；
没有人理。女人们提着枪、
弓和战棒奔走，尖声喊着
儿子和丈夫。男人们在鬃毛
和传说的旋涡里上马，卷进
飞沙的旋涡里没影；战士们

直到一只手按上他的后脖颈，
把他往北一搡，有人喊“*跑！*”
他窜了。

与此同时，朝太阳那边，
村尾传来一声枪响，
雷诺的骑兵绕过一道河湾涌来，
卡宾枪顶在鞍头待放。

战斗队形时马匹嘶鸣；军官们在喊，

像已经死了的人的虚弱的鬼，
被后面的铁蹄追赶；
因为他们认定，来者正是
大名鼎鼎的黄毛，
瓦希塔之狼，他的整个狼群
都揣在他背后的尘云里，
正朝他们压来。
恐怖像吼风前头
沿着洼沼烧起的一道火线，
随着奔逃的人越聚越多而越长，
因为一切迎面跑上来的
都是那火线前的草；直到人们
发觉那里轰响起另一个声音，
高过恐慌，撞开蹄声、
风声和枪声的喧闹。
那是加尔。
像星辰黯淡时吹着的一阵夜风，
他胯下的大黑马喘着气；
他简直不像凡人的族类，
赤裸的身子、厚重的脸
静得像从被时间遗忘的岩石上
凿下，任马在人潮的
冲涌前人立。孩子心的战士们

===Original===

New courage from the father in his look

And listened in a sudden lull of sound.

“The foe is there!” he shouted. “Turn around!

Die here today!” And everywhere he rode

A suck of men grew after him and flowed

To foeward.
Now it seemed the routed fear

Had joined the halted troops. They ceased to cheer.

Dismounting with their right upon the trees

Along the river, and the Rickarees

Upon their left, they flung a blazing dam

Across the valley. Like a river jam

The eager rabble deepened on the front,

For other hundreds, howling to the hunt,

Were dashing up with ponies. Then they say

A sound was heard as when a jam gives way

Before a heaped up freshet of the Spring,

And ponies in a torrent smote the wing

Where, mounted yet, the little Ree band stood.
Now those, remembering where life was good,

Regretting that they ever chose to roam

So far from kindly faces, started home

Without farewells; and round the crumpled flank

The Sioux came thronging, bending back the rank

Upon the pivot of the farther troop,

Till, crowded in a brushy river loup,

The soldiers fought bewildered and forlorn.

Behind them from across the Little Horn

The long range rifles on the bluff rim spat

A hornet swarm among them; and the flat

Before them swam with ponies on the run﻿—

A vertigo of shadows; for the sun

Went moony in the dust and disappeared.

Inverted faces of a nightmare leered

Beneath the necks of ponies hurtling past;

And every surge of horsemen seemed the last,

So well their daring fed upon their rage.
It might have been a moment or an age

The troopers gripped that slipping edge of life,

When some along the left saw Bloody Knife,

By Reno, straighten from his fighting squat,

And heard him scream, and saw the wound he got

Spew brains between the fingers clutching there.

Then like a drowning man with hands in air

He sank. And some who fought nearby have said

The Major’s face, all spattered with the red

Of that snuffed life, went chalky, and his shout

Scarce reached the nearer troopers round about:

“*Back to the bluffs!*” But when a few arose

To do his will, they say he raged at those:

“*Get down! Get down!*” Then once again he cried:

“*Get to the bluffs!*”﻿—And was the first to ride.
Now some along the right, who had not heard,

But saw the mounting, passed a shouted word

That groped, a whisper, through the roaring smoke:

从他眼中的父辈气概里得了
新的勇气，在骤然的一阵寂静里听。
“仇敌在那儿！”他喊。“转身！
今天就在这儿死！”他骑到哪儿，
哪儿的男人们就被吸在他身后，
涌向前去。
溃逃的恐惧仿佛
这时追上了停住的骑兵。欢呼停了。
他们右手依托沿河的
树林下马，左边是
里斯人，朝河谷横抛出
一道喷火的坝。急切的人群
在正面越挤越厚，像河道里的堵塞，
因为另外的几百骑嚎着
奔猎而来。随后，据说
响起一种声音，像春天壅高的
洪峰冲垮堵塞，
小马的激流砸在还骑在
马上的那一小队里斯人把着的翼上。
这些人想起了活着好的地方，
后悔不该跑这么远、
离开那么多和善的脸，没道别
就动身回家；苏族绕着
垮掉的侧翼蜂拥而来，把
另一队当轴心往外卷，
直到士兵们挤在一条
灌木的河曲里，昏乱孤绝地打。
他们背后，小角河对岸
断崖边远程步枪
朝他们吐出黄蜂群；前面的平川
奔马如梭，晃成一团影子的
晕眩；因为太阳
在尘里变成月色，不见了。
噩梦倒悬的脸在掠过的
马颈下面狞笑；
每一浪骑潮都像是最后一浪，
他们的胆气靠怒气养得这么足。
也许只是一瞬、也许是一个世代，
骑兵们攥着滑脱的生命之缘，
这时左翼有人看见血刀——
就在雷诺身边——从战斗的蹲伏里
直起身，听见他惨叫，
看见他挨的那处伤
把脑浆喷在摀着的手指间。
随后他像溺水的人一样
举着手沉了下去。近处
作战的人说，少校那张
溅满那条熄灭的生命之红的脸
刷地惨白，他的喊声
勉强够到身边的骑兵：
“*退到崖上去！*”可当几个人
起身照办时，据说他又冲他们咆哮：
“*趴下！趴下！*”随后又一次喊：
“*上崖去！*”——自己第一个上马。
右翼有些人没听见，
只看见有人上马，传开一声喊，
那喊声像耳语，在轰响的硝烟里摸索：

===Original===

“We’re going to charge!” And where it fell, it broke

The ragged line. Men scrambled to the rear

Where now the plunging horses shrieked with fear

And fought their holding “fours”﻿—nor all in vain,

For whole quadrigae, fastened bit to rein,

Ramped down that stormy twilight of the Sioux.

The nearest empty saddle seat would do

For any lucky finder. Rout or charge﻿—

What matter? All along the river marge

The man storm raged, and all the darkened vale

Was tumult. To retreat was to assail,

Assault was flight. The craven and the bold

Seemed one that moment where the loud dust rolled,

Death-strewing, up along the Little Horn.
About the loup a mockery of morn

Broke in upon the gloaming of the noon,

And horseless troopers, starting from the swoon

Of battle, saw, and knew themselves alone

And heard the wounded wailing and the moan

Of dying men around them. Even these,

Forlorn among the bullet-bitten trees,

Were scarce less lucky than the fleeing ranks

With crowding furies snapping at their flanks,

Death in the rear and frantic hope ahead.

’Twas like a bison hunt, the Sioux have said,

When few bulls battle and the fat cows run

Less fleet than slaughter. Hidden from the sun,

How many a boy, struck motherless, belied

The whiskered cheek; what heroism died,

Fronting the wild white glory!
Funk or fight,

Lost in the noon’s anomaly of night,

The troopers struggled, groping for a ford.

But more and more the pressure of the horde

Bore leftward, till the steep-banked river spread

Before them, and the bluffs that loomed ahead

Were like the domes of heaven to the damned.

A shrinking moment, and the flood was jammed

With men and horses thrashing belly deep;

And down upon them, jostled to the leap,

The rear cascaded. Many-noted pain

Sang medley in the roaring rifle rain

That swept the jetting water, gust on gust.

And many a Sioux, gone wild with slaughter lust,

Plunged after. Madmen grappled in the flood,

And tumbling in the current, streaked with blood,

Drank deep together and were satisfied.
Now scrambling out upon the further side,

The hunted troopers blundered at a steep

More suited to the flight of mountain sheep

Than horses; for a narrow pony trail,

That clambered up a gully from the vale,

Immediately clogged with brutes and men.

Spent horses skittered back to strive again,

Red-flanked and broken-hearted. Many bore

===Chinese===

“我们要冲锋了！”这话落在哪儿，
哪儿残破的战线就断。
人们往后面爬，后面惊跳的
马匹恐惧地嘶叫，
挣着它们“四骑”的拴系——也不全白挣，
因为整组整组的四马，衔铁连着缰绳，
从苏族风暴的晦冥里
倒栽着冲下坡去。
谁运气好抢到，最近的空鞍
就是谁的。溃逃还是冲锋——
有什么要紧？沿着河边，
人的风暴狂刮，发暗的整个河谷
一片鼎沸。退即是攻，
攻即是逃。在尘嚣滚滚、
一路播死的小角河上游，
懦夫和勇士那一刻
浑然是一个。
正午的暝色里，河曲四周
透进一缕嘲弄的晨光，
丢了马的骑兵从战斗的
昏厥里惊起，看见，知道只剩自己，
听见四周伤者的哀号和
垂死者的呻吟。就连这些人，
弹痕累累的树间这些孤绝者，
也不比那些侧翼被紧咬的
狂怒所挤、后有死、前有疯望的
奔逃行伍更不济。
苏族人后来说，那像一场猎牛：
公牛少少几头在斗，肥母牛
跑不过屠宰。背弃了太阳，
多少没了母亲的男孩，
顶着大人的胡须倒下；多少英雄气概
在直面那狂野的白色荣光时
死掉！是怯是战，
迷失在正午的夜的异象里，
骑兵们挣扎着摸索一个渡口。
可人群的压力越逼越左，
直到陡岸的河横在
他们面前，前面耸起的断崖
对被罚下地狱的人就像
天国的穹顶。一缩神的工夫，
洪流里就塞满了没腹
扑腾的人和马；后面的
推搪着、纵身叠下来，泻成瀑布。
千声万调的疼痛
在扫过喷涌河水的枪雨里，
一阵接一阵，唱成杂曲。
许多杀红了眼的苏族人
跟着扎进水里。疯子在洪流里扭打，
在流水里翻滚，带着血道子，
一同痛饮，喝了个够。
这时爬上对岸，
被猎的骑兵们撞上一道陡坡，
那更宜于大角羊逃命，
而不是马；一条窄窄的马道
从谷底顺冲沟爬上去，
立刻塞满了牲口和人。
力竭的马滑下来再试，
两胁通红，心都碎了。许多马驮着

===Original===

Their riders where no horse had gone before,

Nor ever shall go. Bullets raked the slope,

And from the valley to the heights of hope

The air was dirty with the arrow-snow.

The heights of hope? Alas, that stair of woe,

Strewn with the bleeding offal of the rout,

Led only to an eminence of doubt,

A more appalling vision of their plight;

For in the rear and on the left and right

The nearer bluffs were filling with the Sioux,

And still along the flat beneath them blew

The dust of thousands yelping for the kill.
They say that good men broke upon the hill

And wept as children weep. And there were some

Who stared about them empty eyed and dumb,

As though it didn’t matter. Others hurled

Profane irrelevancies at the world

Or raved about the jamming of their guns.

And yet there lacked not level-headed ones,

Unruffled shepherds of the flock, who strove

For order in the milling of the drove

With words to soothe or cheer, or sting with scorn.
Now up the valley of the Little Horn

Wild news came crying from the lower town

Of other soldiers yonder riding down

Upon the guardless village from the east;

And every tongue that sped the news increased

The meaning of it. Victory forsook

Big hearts that withered. Lo, the Gray Fox, Crook,

Returning for revenge﻿—and not alone!

How many camps along the Yellowstone

Were emptied on the valley there below?

The whipped were but a sprinkle of the foe,

And now the torrent was about to burst!

With everything to know, they knew the worst,

And saw the clearer in that no one saw.

Then broke a flying area of awe

Across the rabble like a patch of sun

Upon the troubled corn when gray clouds run

And in the midst a glowing rift is blown.

Pressed back before the plunging white-faced roan

Of Crazy Horse, men brightened. How they knew

That lean, swift fighting-spirit of the Sioux,

The wizard eyes, the haggard face and thin,

Transfigured by a burning from within

Despite the sweat-streaked paint and battle grime!

Old men would ponder in the wane of time

That lifting vision and alluring cry:

“*There never was a better day to die!*

*Come on, Dakotas! Cowards to the rear!*”
Some hundreds yonder held the net of fear

Round Reno’s hill; but in the cloud that spread

Along the valley where the fleet roan led

Were thousands.
Now the feeble and the young,

The mothers and the maidens, terror-flung

===Chinese===

它们的骑手，去到马从未去过、
也永不会去的地方。子弹耙着坡，
从谷底到希望的高处，
空气被箭雪弄脏。
希望的高处？唉，那道祸患的阶梯，
撒满溃乱者的血污，
只通向一处疑惧的高地，
一副更骇人的绝境图景；
因为背后、左右，
近处的断崖正被苏族填满，
他们脚下的平川上仍旧滚着
千百张嘴喊着杀的尘土。
据说好好的男人在山上垮了，
哭得像孩子。也有人
瞪着空茫的眼四下看，哑着，
仿佛一切都无所谓了。还有人
朝着世界乱骂不相干的脏话，
或是咒着枪又卡壳了。
可头脑冷静的也不缺，
不动声色的羊倌，在乱转的
羊群里，用安抚或打气的话、
或讥刺的冷语维持着秩序。

这时，从小角河谷下游的镇子，
狂野的哭喊传来消息：
又一支军队正从东边
朝不设防的村子压下来；
传话的每一张嘴都给消息
再加一层分量。胜利抛下了
一颗颗枯萎的大心。看，灰狐克鲁克
回来寻仇了——还不是一个！
黄石河沿岸多少营帐
倾泻到了下面那道河谷里？
刚打垮的不过是仇敌的一撮，
洪流眼看就要决口！
什么都无从知道，他们却知道了最坏的，
并且因为谁也看不见而看得更清。

随后，一片飞掠的敬畏
破开人群，像乌云奔涌的
愁惨玉米地上一方日光，
正中豁开一道发亮的裂缝。
在疯马那匹人立的白脸菊花青
面前退开的人们，眼睛亮了。他们多认得
苏族那精瘦迅疾的战斗之魂啊，
那双巫眼，那张清瘭瘦削的脸，
尽管汗冲的战画和征尘满身，
却被体内的一场燃烧变了容！
老人们会在时间的余晖里
久久回想那升起的幻象和诱人的呼喊：
“*再没有比今天更好的死日了！*
*来吧，达科他人！懦夫退后！*”
那边几百人围着雷诺的山
拉紧恐惧之网；而在那朵
沿着河谷、随疾驰的菊花青
铺开的云里，是几千人。
这时，老弱和孩童，
母亲和少女，被恐怖抛掷着

===Original===

Beyond the lower village to the west,

Had seen the soldiers loom along a crest

Beyond the town, and, heading down a swale

By fours, with guidons streaming in the gale,

Approach the ford. ’Twas Custer with the grays,

A sorrel troop and thrice as many bays﻿—

Two hundred and a handful at the most;

But ’twas the bannered onset of a host

To those who saw and fled. Nor could they know

The numbers and the valiance of the foe

Down river where the bulls of war were loud;

For even then that thunder and the cloud

Came northward. Were they beaten? Had they won?

What devastation, darkening the sun,

Was tearing down the valley? On it roared

And darkled; deepened at the lower ford

And veered cyclonic up the yawning draw

To eastward. Now the breathless people saw

The dusty ponies darting from the van

And swarming up the left. The guns began,

A running splutter. Yonder to the south

The big dust boiling at a coulee’s mouth

Was pouring ponies up around the right.

Grown dimmer in the falling battle-night,

The stormy guidons of the troopers tossed,

Retreating upward, lessened and were lost

Amid a whirling cloud that topped the hill.

And steadily the valley spouted still

The double stream of warriors.
Then a shout

Enringed the battle, and the scene went out

In rumbling dust﻿—as though a mine were lit

Beneath the summit and the belch of it

Gloomed bellowing. A windy gloaming spread

Across the ridges flicked with errant lead

And wayward arrows groping for a mark.

And horses, hurtled from the central dark,

With empty saddles charged upon the day.
Meanwhile on Reno’s hill four miles away

Men heartened to a rousing cheer had seen

The bays and blacks and sorrels of Benteen,

Hoof-heavy with their unavailing quest

Among the valleys to the south and west,

Toil upward. Unmolested by the foe,

The pack mules, trumpeting “We told you so,”

Trudged in a little later. By the cheers

It might have been reunion after years;

And was in truth; for there were graying locks,

That night, to mock the pedantry of clocks,

Untroubled by the ages life can pack

Between the ticks.
The fire had fallen slack

Upon the watching summits round about

And in a maze of wonderment and doubt

Men scanned the north that darkled as with war.

“What was it that the Major waited for?

He’d best be doing something pretty quick

Or there’d be Custer with a pointed stick

===Chinese===

逃到下游村子更西边的地方，
他们看见士兵在镇子那头
一道山脊上耸现，四人一排
顺着洼地下来，队旗在烈风里
翻飞，逼近渡口。那是卡斯特
带着灰马队、一支栗马队
和三倍于此的枣红马——
至多两百挂零；可在看见而逃的人
眼里，那就是旌旗蔽日的大军。
他们也无从知道下游
战牛吼声震天的地方
仇敌的人数和勇武；
因为就在那时，那片雷声和尘云
正压向北方。他们败了吗？赢了吗？
是什么遮天蔽日的东西
正沿河谷撕下来？它吼着、
暗着，在下渡口涨大，
旋风般折上东边张口的深谷。
屏息的人们看见
蒙尘的小马从前锋里穿出，
涌向左侧。枪声起了，
一阵跑动的噼啪。南边那边，
一道冲沟口沸腾的大尘
正往右侧灌小马。
在落下的战斗之夜里越变越暗，
骑兵们暴风雨般的队旗
且退且上，缩小、消失
在山顶一团旋卷的云里。
而河谷仍在稳定地喷着
战士的双重洪流。
随后一声呐喊
环住战场，整个场景
在滚动的尘里熄灭——仿佛山顶下
点着了一座矿，它喷出的
轰响黑黑地炸开。一阵刮风的昏暝
铺过弹子乱飞、
歪箭摸索着找靶的山脊。
而从中央黑暗里掷出的马匹，
驮着空鞍，朝着白昼冲锋。

与此同时，四英里外雷诺的山上，
人们望见引发一阵欢呼的景象：
本廷的枣红马、黑马和栗色马，
在南边和西边的河谷里
一无所获、蹄音沉重地
爬上山来。没受仇敌滋扰，
驮骡们“我们早说过”地号叫着，
稍后也蹒跚赶到。听那欢呼，
简直像阔别多年的重逢；
也确实是重逢；因为那晚
有几绺头发白了，嘲弄着
时钟的迂腐，不在乎生命
能在滴答之间塞进的岁月。
四周瞭望山顶上的火力
稀了下去，人们在惊奇
和疑虑的迷宫里
瞭望那像战争一样暗下来的北方。
“少校到底在等什么？
他最好快点动手，
不然卡斯特就要拿着尖头棍
来找他了！”几个胆大的这样咕哝。

===Original===

To look for him!” So growled a bolder few.

But many thought of little else to do

Than just to dodge the leaden wasp that kills

Sent over by the snipers on the hills

In fitful swarms.
Now like a bellowed word

The miles made inarticulate, they heard

A sound of volley-firing. *There! and there!*

Hoarse with a yet incredible despair

That incoherent cry of kin to kin

Grew big above the distant battle din﻿—

The sequent breakers of a moaning sea.

And twice the murmuring veil of mystery

Was rent and mended. Then the tearing drawl

Was heard no more where Fury, striding tall,

Made one in dust the heavens and the earth.

“He’s pitching into them for all he’s worth,”

Some ventured;﻿—“was there nothing else to do

But hug that hill?”
Then suddenly there grew

A voice of wrath, and many lying near,

Who heard it, looked﻿—and it was Captain Wier

By Reno yonder; and the place went still:

“Then, Major, if you won’t, by God I will,

And there’ll be more to say if we get back!”

They saw him fling a leg across his black

And take the northward steep with face set grim;

And all the black horse troop rode after him

Across the gulch to vanish on a rise.
Two miles away from where the smudgy skies

Of afternoon anticipated night,

They halted on a space-commanding height

And, squinting through the dusty air ahead,

Were puzzled. For the silence of the dead

Had fallen yonder﻿—only now and then

A few shots crackled. Groups of mounted men﻿—

Not troopers﻿—by the rifting dust revealed,

Were scattered motionless about the field,

As wearily contented with a work

Well done at last.
Then suddenly the murk

Began to boil and murmur, like a storm

Before the wind comes. Ponies in a swarm

Were spreading out across the ridgy land

Against the blacks.
By now the whole command

Was coming up, and not a whit too soon;

For once again the sun became a moon

Amid the dust of thousands bearing down.
Now farther back upon a bleak bluff crown

The troop of Godfrey waited for the fight,

Not doubting that their comrades held the right,

When orders, riding with an urgent heel,

Arrived with more of prudence to reveal

Than pluck: *Withdraw at once!* A startled stare

Made plain how all the flanking hills were bare

And not a sign of Reno in the rear!

Just then the fleeing troops of French and Wier

Came roaring down across a ridge in front

===Chinese===

几个胆大的这样咕哝。
可许多人想的招数也不过是
躲开那些杀人的铅黄蜂——
山上的狙击手一阵一阵
放过来的。这时，像一声
被几英里吼哑了的话，
他们听见一阵排枪的响。*在那儿！在那儿！*
亲属对亲属那声嘶哑的、
还不敢相信的绝望喊叫，
在远处的战喧上越长越大——
呻吟的大海一道接一道
涌来的浪。神秘那层低语的纱
两次撕开又两次补上。随后那
拖长的爆裂再也听不见了，在狂怒
跨着大步、把天地和进
尘土的地方。“他在拼了老命
往里砸呢，”有人试着说；“难道
除了抱住这座山就没别的
可干了？”突然一声怒喝长起，
许多趴在近处的人
听见，抬头看——是韦尔上尉，
就在那边雷诺身旁；全场静了：
“那么，少校，你不去，老天在上，我去，
要是咱们还能回去，有得好算账！”
他们看见他一腿翻上他的黑马，
铁青着脸冲向北面的陡坡；
整个黑马队跟着他
跨过冲沟，在一道山梁上没影。
离那片午后提前入夜的
昏黑天空两英里，
他们在一处俯瞰四野的高地
停下，眎眼透过前面的尘雾，
糊涂了。因为死者般的寂静
已经落在了那边——只是偶尔
几声枪响。一小群一小群骑马的人——
不是骑兵——被裂开的尘雾
显出来，散布在战场上不动，
像干完一件终于干好了的活、
倦倦地心满意足。
突然，那黑暗开始
沸腾、低语，像大风
到来之前的风暴。成群的小马
在丘峦间朝黑马队
四面铺开。这时整个部队
都上来了，来得一点也不早；
因为几千人的尘压过来，
太阳又一次成了月亮。
这时更后面的、一座荒凉崖顶上，
戈弗雷的一队人等着接战，
毫不怀疑战友们守着右翼，
这时一道马鞭催急的命令
到了，露出的审慎
多于胆气：*立即撤退！*
一声惊愕的环顾
看清了侧翼所有的山都空了，
背后也没有雷诺的影子！
恰在这时，法伦奇和韦尔
溃下来的队伍吼着
从前面的山脊上冲过

===Original===

And, close upon their heels, the howling hunt

Made dimmer yet the summit of the slope.

And Godfrey, seeing very little hope

If all should flee those thousands, overjoyed

With some great coup, dismounted and deployed

To fight on foot, and sent the horses back.

And so he dared the brunt of the attack,

Retreating slowly like a wounded bear

With yelping dogs before him everywhere

Regardful of the eager might at bay.

And so the whole command got back that day

Of big despairs; and men remember still.
Then all the ridges circling Reno’s hill

Were crowded. In among the flattened men,

Now desperately fighting one to ten,

Hell hornets snarled and feathered furies crooned

A death song; and the sun was like a wound

Wherewith the day bled dizzy. Yet from all

The muddled nightmare of it, men recall

Deeds brighter for the years: how Captain French,

Like any stodgy tailor on his bench,

Sat cross-legged at the giddy edge of life

Serenely picking with a pocket knife

The shell-jammed guns and loading them anew;

How, seemingly enamoured of the view,

Deliberate, Johnsonian of mien,

His briar drawing freely, strolled Benteen

Along his fighting line; how Wallace, Wier

And Godfrey yonder, fearing only fear,

Walked round among the troopers, cheering them.

And some remember Happy Jack of M,

The way his gusty laughter served to melt

The frost of terror, though the joy he felt

Seemed less to mark a hero than a fool.

And once, they say, an ammunition mule

Broke loose and bolted, braying, as he went,

Defiance and a traitorous intent

To quit the Whites forever. Then they tell

How Sergeant Hanley with an Irish yell

Took horse and followed, jealous for the pack;

And all the line roared after him, “Come back!

Come back, you fool!” But Hanley went ahead.

At times you hardly saw him for the lead

That whipped the dust up. Blindly resolute,

The traitor with the Irish in pursuit

Struck up along a hostile ridge that burned

And smoked and bellowed. Presently he turned

And panted home, an image of remorse;

And Hanley, leaping from his winded horse,

Lay down and went to work among the rest.
The wounded day bled ashen in the west;

The firing dwindled in the dusk and ceased;

The frightened stars came peeking from the east

To see what anguish moaned. The wind went down﻿—

A lull of death. But yonder in the town

===Chinese===

紧跟着脚跟，嚎叫的围猎
把坡顶搅得更暗。
戈弗雷眼看要是全队
都从那几千人面前逃跑、
白丢一次大胜，就没什么指望了，
便下马展开
步战队形，把马匹送了回去。
就这样，他硬顶着攻击的锋头，
像一头受伤的熊缓缓退却，
四面八方是狂吠的狗，
顾念着困兽拼死的巨力。
就这样，全队在那大绝望的
一天里退了回去；人们至今记得。
随后，环绕雷诺之山的
所有山脊都挤满了。在贴地
的人堆里——此刻正以一敌十
拼命——地狱的黄蜂嚎叫，
插羽的复仇神哼着死亡之歌；
太阳像一道伤口，白昼
流着血晕眩。可从这一切
混乱的噩梦里，人们记起的
是越久越亮的事迹：法伦奇上尉
怎样像个守摊的裁缝坐在
他那长凳上，盘腿坐在
生命的晕眩边缘，安详地用小刀
剔开卡壳的枪、重新装弹；
本廷怎样像看风景看入了迷，
从容不迫、一脸约翰逊式的
威严，叼着畅吸的石楠木烟斗
沿他的战线遛达；华莱士、韦尔
和那边的戈弗雷，只怕“怕”字，
怎样在士兵中间走动打气。
有人还记得 M 连的快乐杰克，
他那一阵一阵的大笑
怎样化开了恐怖的霜，虽然
他那份快乐看着
与其说像英雄，不如说像傻子。
还有一次，据说一头驮弹药的骡子
挣脱了，边跑边叫，
叫的是反了、老子从此
不伺候白人了。然后人们讲
汉利中士怎样爱尔兰地一声喊，
上马追去，一心要夺回驮子；
全线的人冲他背后吼：“回来！
回来，你这蠢货！”可汉利只管往前。
好一阵子铅雨抽起的尘土里
你几乎看不见他。那叛徒
瞎着心眼跑，爱尔兰人在后面追，
一头撞上一道着火、
冒烟、轰鸣的敌岭。不一会儿它折回来，
喘着回家，一副悔过的样子；
汉利从跑乏的马上跳下，
躺倒，照旧干活去了。
受伤的白昼在西天流着灰血；
枪声在暮色里稀落、停息；
受惊的星星从东边探头，
看是什么在哀号。风息了——
死亡般的一阵平息。可那边的镇子里

===Original===

All night the war drums flouted that despair

Upon the hill, and dancers in the glare

Of fires that towered filled the painted dark

With demon exultation, till the lark

Of doom should warble. Heavy-lidded eyes

Saw often in the sage along a rise

The loom of troops. If any shouted “Look!”

And pointed, all the others cheered for Crook

Or Terry coming; and the bugles cried

To mocking echoes. When the sick hope died,

They fell to sullen labor, scraping up

The arid earth with plate and drinking cup

Against the dreaded breaking of the day.

And here and there among the toilers lay

The winners of an endless right to shirk;

While many panted at a harder work,

The wage whereof is nothing left to buy.
It seemed that all were men about to die,

Forlornly busy there among the dead﻿—

Each man his sexton. Petulant with dread,

They talked of Custer, grumbling at a name

Already shaping on the lips of Fame

To be a deathless bugle-singing soon.

For no one guessed what now the tardy moon

Was poring over with a face of fright

Out yonder: naked bodies gleaming white

The whole way to the summit of the steep

Where Silence, brooding on a tumbled heap

Of men and horses, listened for a sound.﻿ ﻿…

A wounded troop horse sniffed the bloody ground

And ghosts of horses nickered when he neighed.
Now scarcely had the prairie owls, afraid

Of morning, ceased, or waiting hushes heard

A timid, unauthoritative bird

Complain how late the meadowlarks awoke,

When suddenly the dreaded fury broke

About the sleepless troopers, digging still.

It raked the shallow trenches on the hill;

It beat upon the little hollow where

The mules and horses, tethered in a square

About the wounded, roared and plunged amain,

Tight-tailed against no pasture-loving rain;

And many fell and floundered. What of night

From such a morning? For the hostile light

Increased the fury, and the battle grew.
That day it seemed the very sun was Sioux.

The heat, the frenzy and the powder gas

Wreaked torture. Men were chewing roots of grass

For comfort ere the day had well begun.

Bare to the grim mid-malice of the sun,

The wounded raved for water. Far below,

Cool with the melting of the mountain snow,

The river gleamed; and, queasy with the smell

Of bodies bloating in a stew of hell,

Men croaked about it. Better to be killed

Half way to yonder joy than perish grilled

===Chinese===

战鼓整夜嘲弄着山上
那片绝望，跳舞的人在冲天的
火光里，把涂彩的黑暗
填满魔鬼的狂喜，直等到
末日云雀开口。沉重的眼皮
常在山脊的鼠尾草里
看见军队的影。谁喊一声“看！”
一指，大家就欢呼克鲁克
或特里来了；军号
对着嘲弄的回声吹。当病态的
希望死了，他们闷头苦干起来，
用盘子和水杯刨着
干土，防备那个怕人的破晓。
苦干的人中间，这里那里
躺着一些赢得了永远
免工权利的人；许多人喘着
干更难的活，那活计的工钱
是再没什么可买。
那一圈人都像将死的人，
凄凄惶惶地在死人中间忙——
人人自任司阍。恐惧使他们
暴躁，他们谈论卡斯特，
抱怨着那个已经登上
名声之唇、就要化作
不朽号音的名字。
因为没人猜到，迟迟不肯上来的月亮
此刻正满脸惊惶地
俯读着那边：赤裸的尸身
泛着白，一路白到陡坡的
顶上，寂静蹲在一堆
翻倒的人和马上，等着一个声音。……
一匹受伤的军马嗅着血地，
它一嘶，马的鬼魂们应着嘶鸣。
草原的鸮鸟怕早晨，刚一住口，
或等待的寂静刚听见一只
怯生生的、说话不算数的鸟
抱怨草地鹨醒得太迟，
突然，那可怕的狂怒
就在还在掘壕的不眠骑兵中
炸开。它耙过山上浅浅的壕；
它捶打那圈牲口的小洼地——
骡马围着伤员拴成方阵，
咆哮着猛拽，夹紧尾巴
顶着这场绝不爱草的雨；
倒下、翻滚的不在少数。
这样的早晨，还算什么夜？
因为敌意的天光
助长着狂怒，战斗升级了。
那天，连太阳都像是苏族的。
暑热、疯狂和硝烟
施展着酷刑。天还没大亮，
人们就嚼起草根来讨一点慰藉。
裸露在太阳恶毒的正午恶意下，
伤员们谵妄着要水。远在山下，
凉着高山的融雪，
河水闪着光；人们嗅着
尸体在地狱的炖锅里发胀的
气味，沙哑地念叨着它。
半路死在那份喜悦里，
也好过夹在那张
土地与灼空的烤架上烤死！

===Original===

Between that grid of earth and burning air!
So nineteen troopers volunteered to dare

A grisly race. The twentieth who ran,

Invisible and fleeter than a man,

With hoofs of peril flicked the dusty sod

Where pluckily the sprinting water squad

Made streamward. Giddy with a wound he got,

A trooper tumbled, and his cooking pot

Pursued the others with a bounding roll.

A second runner crumpled near the goal.

And when the sprawling winners drank, they say

The bullets whipped the water into spray

About their heads; for yonder in the brush

The Sioux kept watch, but dared not make a rush

Because of marksmen stationed on the bluff.

And when the greedy drinkers had enough,

With brimming kettles and the filled canteens

They toiled along the tortuous ravines

And panted up a height that wasn’t Fame’s.

Men still recall the water; but the names

Enrich that silence where the millions go.
The shadows had begun to overflow

Their stagnant puddles on the nightward side,

When presently the roar of battle died

On all the circling summits there. Perplexed

With what the wily foe might purpose next,

The troopers lay and waited. Still the swoon

Of silence held the stifling afternoon,

Save for a low monotony of pain,

The keening of the gnats about the slain

That festered. Nothing happened. Shadows crept

A little farther nightward. Many slept,

Dead to the sergeant’s monitory shake;

And some, for very weariness awake,

Got up and dared to stretch a leg at last,

When from the summits broke a rifle blast

That banished sleep and drove the strollers in.
Abruptly as it started, ceased the din

And all the hills seemed empty as before.
And, breath by breath, the weary waiting wore

The hours out. Every minute, loath to pass,

Forewarned the next of some assault in mass

Preparing in the hush. A careless head

Above a horse’s carcass drew the lead

Of lurking marksmen. What would be the end?

The prayed-for dark itself might prove no friend

For all its pity.
Now the early slant

Of evening made the thirsty horses pant

And raise a running whimper of despair,

When, seemingly ignited by the glare,

The very prairie smouldered. Spire by spire,

Until the whole fat valley was afire,

Smoke towered in the windless air and grew

Where late the league long village of the Sioux

Lay hidden from the watchers on the hill;

And like the shadow of a monster ill

===Chinese===

夹在那张土地与灼空的烤架上！
于是十九名骑兵志愿去跑
一场可怖的赛跑。跑的人里
第二十个，看不见、比人还快，
用危险的四蹄撩着尘土，
追着拼命奔向河边的
取水小队。一个骑兵
挨了一枪，晕晕地栽倒，
他的行军锅一路蹦跳着
追赶前面的伙伴。
第二个跑的人在终点前折了。
趴下喝水的人喝的时候，据说
子弹把他们头顶的河水
抽成一片飞沫；因为那边的
灌木里有苏族守望，却不敢扑上来，
因为崖上有神枪手。
贪喝的人喝够了，
提着满壶满罐，
沿九曲八弯的冲沟挣扎，
喘上一座不属于名声的高地。
人们还记得那水；而那几个名字
让千百万人去的那片沉默更富。
阴影开始漫出
它们朝夜那一面的死水洼，
这时四周环立山顶上的
战吼忽然死了。揣着
狡黠的仇敌下一步会怎样的
疑虑，士兵们趴着等。
窒息的午后仍是
昏厥般的寂静，
只有一阵低低的、单调的痛，
蚊蚋围着发臭的
死者哀鸣。什么也没发生。
影子又向夜里爬了一点。许多人睡了，
中士推也推不醒；
有些人困得反倒醒着，
起身壮着胆子伸伸腿，
山顶上突然炸开一排枪，
把睡意赶跑、把遛达的人赶回来。
来得突然，停得也突然，
群山又空得跟先前一样。
一呼一吸地，疲惫的等待
磨着时辰。每一分钟都不肯走，
警告下一分钟：寂静里
正在准备一次总攻。
马尸上一颗大意的头，
招来潜伏射手的枪子。
结局会是什么样？
连祈求来的黑夜，
尽管存着怜悯，也未必是朋友。
这时，傍晚最初的斜光
让干渴的马喘起来，
发出一阵连绵的绝望呜咽，
就在这时，仿佛被那强光点着，
草原本身冒烟了。一股烟柱
接一股烟柱，直到整条肥沃的
河谷都烧起来，烟
在无风的空气里高高长大，
在不久前苏族那一英里长、
瞞过山上瞭望者的
村子所在的地方；
像一个病怪物的影子，

===Original===

Untimely gloaming fell across the height.

Yet nothing but the failing of the light

Upon the distant summits came to pass.

The muffled murmur of the burning grass

Was all the reeking valley had of sound;

And when the troopers dared to walk around,

No spluttering of rifles drove them back.
The shadows in the draws were getting black

When someone lifted up a joyous cry

That set the whole band staring where the sky,

To southward of the smoke, remembered day.

And there they saw, already miles away﻿—

A pictographic scrawl upon the glow﻿—

The tangled slant and clutter of travaux

By crowding hundreds; ponies that pursued,

A crawling, milling, tossing multitude,

A somber river brawling out of banks;

And glooms of horsemen flowing on the flanks﻿—

The whole Sioux village fleeing with the light

To where the Big Horn Mountains glimmered white

And low along the south!
The horses neighed

To swell the happy noise their masters made.

The pack mules sang the only song they knew.

And summits, late familiar with the Sioux,

Proclaimed a new allegiance, cheer on cheer.

For who could doubt that news of Terry near

Had driven off the foe?

===Chinese===

不合时宜的暮色落过高地。
然而除了远山山顶上
光的衰微，什么也没发生。
燃草喑哑的低语
就是那条冒烟河谷仅有的声音；
士兵们壮着胆子四下走动，
也没有枪声把他们赶回去。
冲沟里的影子黑透了，
有人发出一声欢喜的大喊，
全队人顺着烟南边
还记得白昼的天空望去。
他们看见，已在几英里外——
辉光上一笔象形的涂鸦——
成百的人挤着、雪橇的
斜乱堆簇；后面追着的马群，
一条蠕蠕、乱转、颠簸的大众，
一条漫出河岸的暗色大河；
两翼流动着骑手的暗影——
整个苏族村子携着光逃向
大角山脉泛白、低伏
在南天的地方！
军马嘶鸣，
给主人发出的欢呼添声。
驮骡唱起它们唯一会唱的歌。
不久前还同苏族相熟的山顶，
一遍欢呼接一遍欢呼，
宣布改换了效忠。
谁还能怀疑，是特里逼近的消息
把仇敌赶跑了呢？

## XIII The Twilight / 十三 暮色

## XIII The Twilight /

## XIII The Twilight / 
===Original===

Moon wore by,

And in the rainless waning of July

Ten thousand hearts were troubled where the creeks,

Young from the ancient winter of the peaks,

Romped in the mountain meadows green as May.

The very children lost the heart to play,

Awed by the shadow of an unseen thing,

As covies, when the shadow of a wing

Forebodes a pounce of terror from the skies.

They saw it in the bravest father’s eyes﻿—

That shadow﻿—in the gentlest mother’s face;

Unwitting how there fell upon a race

The twilight of irreparable wrong.

The drums had fallen silent with the song,

And valiant tales, late eager to be told,

Were one with all things glorious and old

And dear and gone forever from the Sioux.
For now the hunted prairie people knew

How powerful the Gray Fox camp had grown

On Goose Creek; how along the Yellowstone

The mounted soldiers and the walking ones﻿—

A multitude﻿—had got them wagon guns,

Of which the voice was thunder and the stroke,

Far off, a second thunder and a smoke

That bit and tore. A little while, and then

Those open jaws, toothed terribly with men,

Would move together, closing to the bite.

What hope was left in anything but flight?

And whither? O the world was narrow now!

South, east, the rat-like nibbling of the plow

Had left them but a little way to go.

The mountains of the never melting snow

Walled up the west. Beyond the northern haze,

There lay a land of unfamiliar ways,

Dark tongues and alien eyes.
As waters keep

Their wonted channels, yearning for the deep,

The homeless rabble took the ancient road.

From bluff to bluff the Rosebud valley flowed

Their miles of ponies; and the pine-clad heights

Were sky-devouring torches in the nights

Behind them, and a rolling gloom by day;

And prairies, kindled all along the way,

Bloomed balefully and blackened. Noon was dark,

Night starless, and the fleeing meadowlark

Forgot the morning. Where the Bluestone runs

Their dust bore east; and seldom did the suns

Behold them going for the seed they strewed

To crop the rearward prairie solitude

With black starvation even for the crow.

Creeks, stricken as with fever, ceased to flow

And languished in a steaming ashen mire.

But more than grass was given to the fire﻿—

O memories no spring could render young!

And so it was that, marching down the Tongue,

The Gray Fox, seeking for the hostile bands,

===Chinese===

月亮磨损着过去，
在七月无雨的衰减里，
一万颗心在山溪边不安——
那些从群峰古老冬天里
年轻出来的溪水，
在绿如五月的高山草甸里嬉闹。
连孩子们都失了玩耍的心，
被一件未见之物的影子慑住，
像鹌鹑被一片翅膀的影子
吓住了，它预告天上
一扑的恐怖。
他们在最勇的父亲眼里看见了
那影子——在最温柔的母亲脸上；
不知道一个种族身上正落下
无可挽回之冤的暮色。
鼓声随着歌声哑了，
英勇的故事，不久前还争着要讲，
如今同一切光荣、古老、
亲爱、永远离开苏族的东西
归成了一样。因为被猎的
草原之民如今知道
灰狐在鹅溪的营地
长到了多大；知道黄石河沿线
骑马的兵和步行的兵——
一大群——弄来了车载炮，
那炮的声音是雷，打击
在远处是第二声雷、一口
咬撕的烟。不一会儿，
那两张张开的、长满了人牙的
大口就要合拢来咬。
除了逃还有什么指望？
往哪儿逃？哦，世界如今窄了！
南边、东边，犁像耗子啮嗌，
只给他们剩下一小截路了。
永不化的雪山
把西边砌死。北边的雾霭外，
躺着一片风俗陌生的土地，
黑舌头和外族的眼。
像水守着旧河道、
渴望着深处，无家的人群
走上了古老的路。
从崖到崖，玫瑰蕾河谷里
淌着他们几英里的马群；
身后，披松的山峰
夜里是吞天的火炬，
白天是滚动的阴霾；
一路点着的草原
凶恶地开花、变黑。正午是暗的，
夜是无星的，逃亡的草地鹨
忘了早晨。在蓝石河流过的地方，
他们的尘土向东；
他们一路播下的种子，太阳
也难得看见，那是拿黑色饥馑
去刈后方的草原荒寂，
连乌鸦都不放过。
溪河像害了热病，断流了，
瘫在一滩冒汽的灰泥里。
可交给火的远不止是草——
哦，那些任何春天都无法催年轻的记忆！
就这样，灰狐顺着汤河下行，
寻找敌对的部落，

===Original===

Saw nothing but the desolated lands

Black to the sky; and when a dreary week

Had brought him to the mouth of Bluestone Creek,

Lo, Terry with another empty tale!
Broad as a road to ruin ran the trail

Of driven pony herds, a livid scar

Upon a vast cadaver, winding far

To eastward as the tallest hill might look.

And thither pressed the horse and foot of Crook,

Their pack mules, lighter for a greater speed,

With scant provisions for a fortnight’s need

Upon their saddles.
Burning August waned

About the toiling regiments. It rained﻿—

A sodden, chill monotony of rains﻿—

As though the elements had cursed the plains,

And now that flame had stricken, water struck.

The scarecrow horses struggled with the suck

Of gumbo flats and heartbreak hills of clay;

And many a bone-bag fell beside the way

Too weak to rise, for still the draws were few

That were not blackened. Crows and buzzards knew

How little eager claws and whetted beaks

Availed them where so many hollow cheeks

Had bulged about a brief and cookless feast.
Still wearily the main trail lengthened east

By hungry days and fireless bivouacs;

And more and more diverging pony tracks,

To north and south, and tangent lodge pole trails

Revealed the hunted scattering as quails

Before a dreaded hunter. Eastward still

They staggered, nourished by a doggèd will,

Past where a little river apes in mud

And name the genius of a titan flood

That drinks it. Crumbling pinnacles of awe

Looked down upon them; domes of wonder saw

The draggled column slowly making head

Against the muck; the drooping horses, led,

Well loaded with their saddles; empty packs,

Become a cruel burden on the backs

Of plodding mules with noses to the ground.

Along the deeps of Davis Creek they wound,

To where the Camel’s Hump and Rosebud Butte

Behold the Heart’s head.
 Here the long pursuit,

It seemed, had come to nothing after all.

The multitude of Crazy Horse and Gall

Had vanished in that God-forsaken place

And matched their fagged pursuers for a race

With something grimmer than a human foe.

Four marches east across the dim plateau

Fort Lincoln lured them. Twice as many days

Beyond the dripping low September haze,

Due south across the yet uncharted lands,

Lay Deadwood, unprotected from the bands

Of prowling hostiles. ’Twas enough for Crook.

Half-heartedly the ragged column took

===Chinese===

只看见黑到天边的
荒废之地；等一个沉闷的星期
把他带到蓝石溪口，
看哪，又是特里和一个空空的故事！
被驱赶的马群踏出的踪迹
宽得像一条通向毁灭的路，
一大道青紫的疤，
在一具巨大尸体上，蜿蜒着
向东去，最高的山也望不到头。
克鲁克的骑兵步兵
朝那里压去，驮骡为求快
减了载，鞍上只有
够半个月的口粮。

燃烧的八月围着
苦熬的团队衰减。下雨了——
一场湿透、阴冷的连绵的雨——
仿佛元素们诅咒过这片平原，
火焰打完了，轮到水来打。
稻草人似的马在胶泥平地
和令人心碎的黏土山里
同吸陷搏斗；一袋袋骨头
倒卧路旁，无力再起，
因为没烧黑的冲沟已经不多。
乌鸦和秃鹫领教了
急爪和快喙在这里多么没用：
那么多瘪脸颊才在一场
无火的短宴上鼓起来。
主径仍旧疲疲地向东延展，
伴着挨饿的白昼和无火的野营；
岔向南北的小马踪迹
和切线的帐篷杆道越来越多，
显出被猎的人像鹌鹑
在可畏的猎手面前四散。
他们仍旧向东
蹒跚，靠一股狠劲养着，
过了一条在泥里效颦、
却顶着那吞下它的巨洪之名的小河。
崩裂的敬畏之巅俯看他们；
奇观的穹顶看着
拖泥带水的纵队缓慢地
顶着头前进；牵着的垂头马，
驮着满满一副鞍；空的驮包
成了累赘，压在一步一陷、
鼻子拱地的骡子背上。
他们沿戴维斯溪的深处
绕行，到骆驼峰和玫瑰蕾山
俯望“心”河源头的地方。
到这里，漫长的追击
似乎终究落了空。
疯马和加尔的大队
在那片上帝抛弃的地方
消失了，同累垮的追兵
赛起了跑，对手是比人
更冷酷的东西。往东四个行军日，
林肯堡招引着他们。两倍的日子
之外，隔着九月低垂滴水的雾，
正南横过那些尚未测绘的土地，
躺着德德伍德，没有防备
游荡敌队的侵扰。这理由对克鲁克
足够了。褴褛的纵队没精打采地

===Original===

The way of duty.
 And the foe appeared!

Where, like a god-built stadium, the tiered

Age-carven Slim Buttes watch the Rabbit’s Lip

Go groping for the ocean, in the drip

And ooze of sodden skies the battle raged;

And presences, millennially aged

In primal silence, shouted at the sight.

Until the rifles gashed the front of night

With sanguinary wounds, they fought it out;

And darkness was the end of it, and doubt

And drizzle. Unrejoicing victors knew

What enemy, more mighty than the Sioux,

Would follow with no lagging human feet;

And early morning saw them in retreat

Before that foe. Above their buried slain

A thousand horses trampled in the rain

That none might know the consecrated ground

To violate it.
Up and up they wound

Among the foggy summits, till the van

Was checked with awe. Inimical to Man,

Below them spread a featureless immense,

More credibly a dream of impotence

Than any earthly country to be crossed﻿—

A gloomy flat, illimitably lost

In gauzes of the downpour.
Thither strove

The gaunt battalions. And the chill rain drove

Unceasingly. Through league on league of mire

Men straggled into camps without a fire

To wolf their slaughtered horses in the red;

And all the wallow of the way they fled

Was strewn with crowbaits dying in the bogs.

About them in the forest of the fogs

Lurked Crazy Horse, a cougar mad for blood;

And scarce the rearguard-battles in the mud

Aroused the sullen plodders to the fore.

The Deer’s Ears loomed and vanished in the pour;

The Haystack Buttes stole off along the right;

And men grew old between a night and night

Before their feeble toil availed to raise

The Black Hills, set against the evil days

About a paradise of food and rest.
Now Crazy Horse’s people, turning west,

Retraced the trail of ruin, sick for home.

Where myriads of the bison used to roam

And fatten in the golden autumn drowse,

A few rejected bulls and barren cows

Grew yet a little leaner. Every place

The good old earth, with ashes on her face,

Was like a childless mother in despair;

Though still she kept with jealous, loving care

Some little hoard of all her youth had known

Against the dear returning of her own;

But where the starving herd of ponies passed,

The little shielded hollows, lately grassed,

Were stricken barren even as with fire.

And so they reached the place of their desire,

===Chinese===

走上了尽职之路。
而仇敌出现了！
在层层的、岁月凿刻的瘦崖
像一座神造的竞技场、
看守住兔子唇河摸向大海的地方，
在湿透的天空的滴沥和
泥泞里，战斗狂烧；
那些在太初的寂静里
老过千年的存在，看见这景象
也喊出声来。
直到步枪把夜的前脸
割出一道道血淋淋的口子，
他们才打完；
黑暗就是它的结局，还有疑惧
和冻雨。不得意的胜者知道，
还有一个比苏族更强的仇敌
会不拖人脚地跟上来；
清晨看见他们在那个
仇敌面前撤退。在他们埋好的
阵亡者上面，一千匹马
在雨里践踏，好让没人认出
那片圣土、再去亵渎它。
他们绕着雾蒙蒙的山顶
盘旋而上，直到前队
被敬畏拦住。与人为敌的
一片无貌的无垠在脚下铺开，
与其说是可以横渡的人间国土，
不如说更像一场无能为力的梦——
一片阴郁的平川，无边无际地
迷失在大雨的纱幕里。
憔悴的营朝那里
挣扎。寒雨不停地
抽打。穿过一里又一里的泥沼，
人们散着走进没有火的营地，
就着血色生吞宰杀的战马；
他们逃一路，一路的泥泞里
都撒着在沼泽里等死的鸦食。
雾的森林里，疯马在
他们四周潜行，一头渴血的
美洲狮；后卫在烂泥里的
苦战也难得把
闷头赶路的人唤到前面来。
鹿耳峰在大雨里隐现；
干草垛山从右边悄悄溜走；
人在一夜和一夜之间老去，
他们微弱的辛劳才勉强
把黑山抬到眼前——它迎着恶日，
环抱一个饮食安眠的乐园。
这时疯马的人民转向西，
重履着毁灭的旧迹，想家想病了。
在万千野牛曾经游荡、
在金色的秋困里养肥的地方，
几头被剔剩的老公牛和不孕的母牛
又瘦了一点。每一处，
善良的老大地蒙着灰，
像一个绝望的无子母亲；
可她仍怀着嫉妒的爱护，
偷偷存着一点她青春
相识的东西，等亲爱的儿女
归来；可饥饿的马群
经过的地方，那些新近
还长着草的隐蔽小洼地，
像遭了火一样绝了草。
就这样，他们到了心愿之地，

===Original===

The deep-carved valley where the Powder flows.

Here surely there was peace.
But when the snows

Came booming where the huddled village stood

And ponies, lean with gnawing cottonwood,

Were slain to fill the kettles, Dull Knife came,

The great Cheyenne. The same﻿—O not the same

As he who fought beside the Greasy Grass

And slew his fill of enemies! Alas,

The beggar in his eyes! And very old

He seemed, for hunger and the pinch of cold

Were on him; and the rabble at his back﻿—

Despairing hundreds﻿—lacked not any lack

That flesh may know and live. The feeble wail

Of babies put an edge upon the tale

That Dull Knife told.
“There was a fight,” he said.

“I set my winter village at the head

Of Willow Creek. The mountains there are tall.

A canyon stood about me for a wall;

And it was good to hear my people sing,

For there was none that wanted anything

That makes men happy. We were all asleep.

The cold was sharp; the snow was very deep.

What enemy could find us? We awoke.

A thunder and a shouting and a smoke

Were there among us, and a swarm of foes﻿—

Pawnees, Shoshones and Arapahoes,

And soldiers, many soldiers. It was night

About us, and we fought them in the light

Of burning lodges till the town was lost

And all our plenty. Bitter was the frost

And most of us were naked from the bed.

Now many of our little ones are dead

Of cold and hunger. Shall the others die?”
There was a light in Crazy Horse’s eye

Like moony ice. The other spoke again.

“As brothers have Dakota and Cheyenne

Made war together. Help us. You have seen

We can not live until the grass is green,

My brother!”
Then the other face grew stone;

The hard lips moved: “A man must feed his own,”

Said Crazy Horse, and turned upon his heel.

But now the flint of him had found the steel

In Dull Knife, and the flare was bad to see.

“Tashunka Witko, dare to look at me

That you may not forget me. We shall meet.

The soldiers yonder have enough to eat,

And I will come, no beggar, with the grass!”
And silently the people saw him pass

Along the valley where the snow lay blue,

The plodding, silent, ragamuffin crew

Behind him. So the evil days began.
Now Crazy Horse, they say, was like a man

Who, having seen a ghost, must look and look

And brood upon the empty way it took

To nowhere; and he scarcely ate at all;

And there was that about him like a wall

===Chinese===

波德河流过的深凿的河谷。
这里总该有安宁了。
可当大雪轰隆隆压到
挤挤的村子、啃三角叶杨
啃瘦了的小马被杀去下锅时，
钝刀来了，那位伟大的夏延人。
还是那个人——哦，已不是
油腻草滩边并肩作战、
仇人杀了个够的那个人了！唉，
他眼里的那副乞相！他显得
很老，饥饿和严寒的掐拧
都在他身上；他身后那群
绝望的几百人——凡是血肉
能缺而又活着的，没有一样不缺。
婴孩微弱的啼哭
给钝刀讲的故事开了刃。
“打了一仗，”他说。
“我把冬村扎在柳溪上游。
那里的山高。一道峡谷
替我当墙；听我的人民唱歌
真好，因为没有谁缺任何
让人快活的东西。我们都睡了。
天冷得尖；雪深得很。
什么仇敌找得到我们？我们醒了。
一声雷、一阵呐喊、一团烟
就在我们中间，还有一大群仇敌——
波尼人、肖肖尼人和阿拉帕霍人，
还有兵，很多兵。我们四下
是黑夜，我们就在燃烧的
帐篷的光里同他们打，
直打到村子丢了，
我们的一切富足都丢了。
霜咬得狠，
我们大多数人是光着身子
从被窝里逃出来的。
如今我们的许多小家伙
已经死于寒冷和饥饿。
其余的也该死吗？”
疯马的眼里有一种光，
像月色的冰。对方又说。
“达科他和夏延像兄弟
一向一起作战。帮帮我们。
你已经看见了，草绿之前
我们活不下去，我的兄弟！”
随后那张脸变成了石头；
硬嘴唇动了动：“人得喂饱自己人，”
疯马说，转身便走。
可他这块燧石撞上了
钝刀这块钢，爆出的火不好看。
“塔顺卡·维特科，敢看着我，
叫你忘不了我。我们会再见的。
那边的兵有的是吃的，
等草绿时我会来，不是来讨饭！”
人们默然看他沿积雪
发蓝的河谷走过去，
那群拖沓、沉默的破衣烂衫的人
跟在后面。恶日就这样开始了。
据说疯马那时像一个
见过鬼的人，得看了又看，
对着那条通向乌有的空路
出神；他几乎什么都不吃了；
他身上有一种东西像一堵墙，

===Original===

To shut men out. He seemed no longer young.
Bleak January found them on the Tongue

In search of better forage for the herd﻿—

A failing quest. And hither came the word

Of many walking soldiers coming down

With wagon guns upon the starving town

That might not flee; for whither could they go

With ponies pawing feebly in the snow

To grow the leaner? Mighty in despair,

They waited on a lofty summit there

Above the valley.
Raw gray dawn revealed

A scaly serpent crawling up a field

Of white beneath them. Leisurely it neared,

Resolving into men of frosty beard

With sloping rifles swinging to the beat

And melancholy fifing of their feet

Upon the frost; and shrill the wagon tires

Sang rearward. Now the soldiers lighted fires

And had their breakfast hot, as who should say:

“What hurry? It is early in the day

And there is time for what we came to do.”

With wistful eyes the rabble of the Sioux

Beheld the eating; knew that they defied

In vain their own misgivings when they cried:

“Eat plenty! You will never eat again!”

It was not so; for those were devil men

Who needed nothing and were hard to kill.
The wagon-guns barked sharply at the hill

To bite the summit, always shooting twice;

And scrambling upward through the snow and ice

Came doggedly, without a sign of fear,

The infantry of Miles. They didn’t cheer,

They didn’t hurry, and they didn’t stop,

For all the rifles roaring at the top,

Until the gun-butt met the battle-ax.

Still fighting with their children at their backs

The Sioux gave slowly. Wind came on to blow,

A hurrying northwester, blind with snow,

And in the wild white dusk of it they fled.
But when they reached the Little Powder’s head,

So much of all their little had been lost,

So well had wrought their hunger and the frost,

One might have thought ’twas Dull Knife coming there.

The country had a cold, disowning stare;

The burned-off valleys could not feed their own.
The moon was like a frozen bubble, blown

Along the rim of February nights,

When Spotted Tail, the lover of the Whites,

Came there with mighty words. His cheeks were full,

His belly round. He spoke of Sitting Bull

And Gall defeated, driven far away

Across the line; of Red Cloud getting gray

Before his time﻿—a cougar in a cage,

Self-eaten by a silent, toothless rage

That only made the watching sentry smile.

===Chinese===

把人挡在外面。他显得不再年轻。
荒凉的一月找到他们时，
他们正在汤河上给马群
找好一点的草料——一场
注定落空的寻找。这时传来
消息：许多步行的兵带着
车载炮下来了，冲着这座
逃无可逃的饥饿之城；
还能往哪儿去呢，让小马
在雪里刨着、越刨越瘦？
绝望使他们强大，
他们在河谷上方一座
高高的山顶上等着。
铅灰色的原始黎明
显出一条鳞甲的蛇，
在下面白色的原野上爬。
它不慌不忙地近了，
化成一队霜须的人，
肩枪随着脚步踏霜那
忧郁的笛声摆动；
车轮在后面尖声唱。
士兵们生起火，
吃着热腾腾的早饭，像在说：
“急什么？天还早，
我们来干的事有的是工夫。”
苏族的人群眼巴巴
望着他们吃；知道他们喊
“多吃点！你们再也吃不着了！”
不过是白白压住
自己的疑虑。
事实不然；那些是魔鬼一样的人，
什么都不需要，又难得杀死。
车载炮朝山上一口口
尖声吠，咬着山顶，
总是连放两响；
踩着冰雪往上爬的，
顽强、没有一丝惧色的，
是迈尔斯的步兵。他们不欢呼，
不赶急，也不停步，
任凭山顶上枪吼成一片，
直到枪托碰上了战斧。
背着孩子仍在战斗的
苏族缓缓退让。风刮起来了，
一阵疾行的西北风，夹雪
盲眼，他们就在那野白
的暮色里逃了。
可等他们到小波德河源头时，
他们那一点点家当丢得那样多，
饥饿和严寒干得那样出色，
人家会当是钝刀到了那里。
那片土地有一种冷冷的、
不认账的凝视；
烧过的河谷养不活自己人。
月亮像一个冻住的泡，
漂在二月夜天的边上，
这时斑尾——那个爱白人的人——
带着威风的话来了。他腮帮子
饱满，肚子滚圆。他讲起
坐牛和加尔如何吃了败仗，
被远远赶过了
界线；讲起红云如何
未老先衰——笼里的一头美洲狮，
被无声的、无牙的怒
慢慢咬着自己，
只叫看守的哨兵看了发笑。

===Original===

And still the story saddened. All the while

The scattered Sioux were coming in to save

Their children with the food the soldiers gave

And laying down their guns and making peace.

He told how Dull Knife’s fury did not cease

But grew upon the soldier food he ate;

And how his people fattened, nursing hate

For Crazy Horse. And many more than these

But waited for the grass﻿—the Loup Pawnees,

The Utes, the Winnebagoes and the Crows,

Shoshones, Bannocks and Arapahoes,

With very many more Dakotas too!
“Now what could Crazy Horse’s people do

Against them all?” said Spotted Tail, the Wise.

And with the ancient puzzle in his eyes

That only death may riddle; gazing long

Now first upon the fat one in the wrong

And now upon the starving in the right,

The other found an answer: “I could fight!

And I could fight till all of us were dead.

But now I have no powder left,” he said;

“I can not fight. Tell Gray Fox what you saw;

That I am only waiting for a thaw

To bring my people in.”

===Chinese===

故事还在往伤心里走。
这一向，四散的苏族
正不断投进来，用士兵发的
粮食救自己的孩子，
放下枪，讲和。
他讲钝刀的怒火如何
没熄，反倒就着
士兵的口粮越烧越旺；
讲他的人民如何养肥了，
胸口焐着对疯马的恨。
还有比这些更多的人
只等草绿——卢普的波尼人、
尤特人、温内巴戈人和乌鸦人、
肖肖尼人、班诺克人和阿拉帕霍人，
外加许许多多的达科他人！
“你说，疯马的人
拿什么抵挡他们全体？”
智者斑尾说。
另一位眼睛里带着那道
只有死才能解的古老的谜，
久久地看，先看
错了理的胖子，
又看占着理的饿汉，
找到了回答：“我能打！
我能打到我们全部死光。
可如今我火药都没了，”他说；
“我打不了。把你看见的告诉灰狐；
说我只是在等一场解冻，
把我的人民带进来。”

## XIV The Death of Crazy Horse / 十四 疯马之死
===Original===

And now ’twas done.

Spring found the waiting fort at Robinson

A half-moon ere the Little Powder knew;

And, doubting still what Crazy Horse might do

When tempted by the herald geese a-wing

To join the green rebellion of the spring,

The whole frontier was troubled. April came,

And once again his undefeated name

Rode every wind. Ingeniously the West

Wrought verities from what the East had guessed

Of what the North knew. Eagerly deceived,

The waiting South progressively believed

The wilder story. April wore away;

Fleet couriers, arriving day by day

With but the farthing mintage of the fact,

Bought credit slowly in that no one lacked

The easy gold of marvelous surmise.

For, gazing northward where the secret skies

Were moody with a coming long deferred,

Whoever spoke of Crazy Horse, still heard

Ten thousand hoofs.
But yonder, with the crow

And kiote to applaud his pomp of woe,

The last great Sioux rode down to his defeat.

And now his people huddled in the sleet

Where Dog Creek and the Little Powder met.

With faces ever sharper for the whet

Of hunger, silent in the driving rains,

They straggled out across the blackened plains

Where Inyan Kara, mystically old,

Drew back a cloudy curtain to behold,

Serene with Time’s indifference to men.

And now they tarried on the North Cheyenne

To graze their feeble ponies, for the news

Of April there had wakened in the sloughs

A glimmering of pity long denied.

Nor would their trail across the bare divide

Grow dimmer with the summer, for the bleach

Of dwindled herds﻿—so hard it was to reach

The South Cheyenne. O sad it was to hear

How all the pent-up music of the year

Surged northward there the way it used to do!

In vain the catbird scolded at the Sioux;

The timid pewee queried them in vain;

Nor might they harken to the whooping crane

Nor heed the high geese calling them to come.

Unwelcome waifs of winter, drab and dumb,

Where ecstasy of sap and thrill of wing

Made shift to flaunt some color or to sing

The birth of joy, they toiled a weary way.

And giddy April sobered into May

Before they topped the summit looking down

Upon the valley of the soldier’s town

At Robinson.
Then eerily began

Among the lean-jowled warriors in the van

The chant of peace, a supplicating wail

That spread along the clutter of the trail

Until the last bent straggler sang alone;

===Chinese===

事就这样成了。
春天找到罗宾逊堡里
等待的人，比小波德河
知道得早半个月；
边境全线仍然不安，
怕疯马受着传信雁阵的
诱惑，去加入春天的
绿色叛乱。四月来了，
他那不败的名字又一次
乘风而行。西部别出心裁，
把东部对北部
所知的猜测铸成了真理。
急着受骗的、等待的南部
一步步信了更野的版本。
四月磨完了；
轻快的信使一天天到来，
带着事实的四分之一便士
铸币，慢慢地买到信任，
因为谁都不缺揣测的
廉价黄金。因为，朝北边
那片因迟迟不来的事变
而阴沉着脸的秘天望去，
谁一说起疯马，就听见
万只蹄音。
可是在那边，有乌鸦和
郊狼为他哀荣的排场喝彩，
最后一位伟大的苏族人
骑向他的失败。
这时他的人民在冻雨里
挤作一团，在狗溪与
小波德河相会的地方。
面孔被饥饿的磨石
越磨越尖，在斜打的冷雨里
沉默，他们零零落落地
穿过烧黑的平原，
在那里，神秘地古老的
英岩峰掠开一幅云的
帷幕来观看，带着时间
对人的漠然，安详。
随后他们停在北夏延河上
放养虚弱的小马，因为
那里的四月消息在洼沼里
唤醒了久欠的一点怜悯。
他们翻过光秃分水岭的踪迹
也不会随夏天淡下去，
因为那是衰减下去的马群
漂白的路——到南夏延河
竟是那样难。哦，听见
一年里憋住的全部乐音
还是像从前一样涌向北方，
多么伤心！猫声鸟骂苏族人，
白骂；怯生生的绿霸鹟
问他们，白问；
他们也听不得鸣鹤，
也不理会高天雁阵唤他们来。
冬天不受欢迎的流落儿，
灰扑扑、闷声不响，
在树的汁液的狂喜、翅膀的
战栗都凑合着炫一点颜色、
唱一支欢乐诞生之歌的地方，
他们赶着疲乏的路。
晕眩的四月清醒成五月，
他们才登上山顶，俯望
罗宾逊军镇所在的河谷。
随后，诡异地，在前队
瘦腮的战士们中间，开始了
和平的吟唱，一声哀求的
哭喊沿着乱糟糟的队伍
传开，直到最后一个
佝偻的掉队者独自在唱；

===Original===

And camp dogs, hunger-bitten to the bone,

Accused the heavens with a doleful sound;

But, silent still, with noses to the ground,

The laden ponies toiled to cheat the crows,

And famine, like a wag, had made of those

A grisly jest.
 So Crazy Horse came in

With twice a thousand beggars.
 And the din

Died out, though here and there a dog still howled,

For now the mighty one, whom Fate had fouled,

Dismounted, faced the silent double row

Of soldiers haughty with the glint and glow

Of steel and brass. A little while he stood

As though bewildered in a haunted wood

Of men and rifles all astare with eyes.

They saw a giant shrunken to the size

Of any sergeant. Now he met the glare

Of Dull Knife and his warriors waiting there

With fingers itching at the trigger-guard.

How many comrade faces, strangely hard,

Were turned upon him! Ruefully he smiled,

The doubtful supplication of a child

Caught guilty; loosed the bonnet from his head

And cast it down. “I come for peace,” he said;

“Now let my people eat.” And that was all.
The summer ripened. Presages of fall

Now wanted nothing but the goose’s flight.

The goldenrods had made their torches bright

Against the ghostly imminence of frost.

And one, long brooding on a birthright lost,

Remembered and remembered. O the time

When all the prairie world was white with rime

Of mornings, and the lodge smoke towered straight

To meet the sunlight, coming over late

For happy hunting! O the days, the days

When winds kept silence in the far blue haze

To hear the deep-grassed valleys running full

With fading cows, and thunders of the bull

Across the hills! Nights given to the feast

When big round moons came smiling up the east

To listen to the drums, the dancing feet,

The voices of the women, high and sweet

Above the men’s!
And Crazy Horse was sad.

There wasn’t any food the white man had

Could find his gnawing hunger and assuage.

Some saw a blood-mad panther in a cage,

And some the sulking of a foolish pride,

For there were those who watched him narrow-eyed

The whole day long and listened for a word,

To shuttle in the warp of what they heard

A woof of darker meaning.
Then one day

A flying tale of battles far away

And deeds to make men wonder stirred the land:

How Nez Perce Joseph led his little band,

With Howard’s eager squadrons in pursuit,

Across the mountains of the Bitter Root

===Chinese===

营狗饿得只剩一把骨头，
哀声控告着天；
可驮重的小马仍旧沉默，
鼻子拱着地，苦干着去瞞乌鸦——
饥馑像个促狭鬼，
把那些马开成一个
惨烈的玩笑。
疯马就这样带着
两千个乞丐进来了。
喧声落了，
虽然这里那里还有狗在吠，
因为此刻，那个被命运
作践的伟人下了马，
面对两排沉默的士兵，
钢与铜的光芒使
他们倨傲。他站了一小会儿，
仿佛在一座闹鬼的
人和步枪的林子里，
四面全是瞪着的眼睛。
他们看见一个巨人，
缩成了一个中士的个头。
这时他迎上钝刀
和他等在那儿的战士们的
怒视，他们的手指
在护圈上发痒。多少张
同志的脸，出奇地硬，
都转向他！他苦笑了一下，
像孩子被抓了现行时
那种没底的讨好；
解下头上的战冠，
掷在地上。“我是为和平来的，”他说；
“现在让我的人民吃饭吧。”
就这一句。

夏天熟了。秋的先兆
如今只差雁阵一飞。
秋麒麟已把火把燃亮，
迎着霜的幽灵般的逼近。
有一个人，久久咀嚼着
失去的与生俱来的权利，
回忆了又回忆。哦那样的时光：
清晨整片草原世界
白茫茫一片霜，
帐篷的烟笔直上升，
迎着娜娜来迟、好去
快活围猎的阳光！哦那些
日子，那些日子：风在远处
蓝色的雾霭里屏息，
听深草的河谷涨满
渐渐远去的母牛，
公牛的雷滚过群山！
交给欢宴的夜：滚圆的大月
笑吟吟爬上东天，
听鼓、听跳舞的脚、
听女人们高亢甜润的
盖过男人的声音！
疯马忧伤。
白人有的任何吃食
都找不到他啃心的饥饿、
安慰不了。有人看见
笼中一头血疯的豹，
有人看见蠢蠢的赌气，
因为有些人整天
眎着眼盯他，等着一个字，
好往他们听见的东西的
经线里，梭进更黑的纬线。
随后有一天，
一个远方战事的飞传
和叫人惊叹的功业
撼动了这片土地：
内兹珀斯的约瑟夫怎样
率他小小的一队，
身后是霍华德穷追的骑兵，
越过了苦根山，

===Original===

To Big Hole Basin and the daylong fight;

And how his women, fleeing in the night,

Brought off the ponies and the children too.

O many a heart beat fast among the Sioux

To hear the way he fled and fought and fled

Past Bannack, down across the Beaverhead

To Henry’s Lake, relentlessly pursued;

Now swallowed by the dreadful solitude

Where still the Mighty Spirit shapes the dream

With primal fires and prodigies of steam,

As when the fallow night was newly sown;

Now reappearing down the Yellowstone,

Undaunted yet and ever making less

That thousand miles of alien wilderness

Between a people’s freedom and their need!
O there was virtue in the tale to feed

The withered heart and make it big again!

Not yet, not yet the ancient breed of men

Had vanished from the aging earth! They say

There came a change on Crazy Horse the day

The Ogalala village buzzed the news.

So much to win and only life to lose;

The bison making southward with the fall,

And Joseph fighting up the way to Gall

And Sitting Bull!
Who knows the dream he had?

Much talk there was of how his heart was bad

And any day some meditated deed

Might start an irresistible stampede

Among the Sioux﻿—a human prairie-fire!

So back and forth along the talking wire

Fear chattered. Yonder, far away as morn,

The mighty heard﻿—and heard the Little Horn

Still roaring with the wind of Custer’s doom.

And there were troopers moving in the gloom

Of midnight to the chaining of the beast;

But when the white light broke along the east,

There wasn’t any Ogalala town

And Crazy Horse had vanished!
Up and down

The dusty autumn panic horsemen spurred

Till all the border shuddered at the word

Of how that terror threatened every trail.
They found him in the camp of Spotted Tail,

A lonely figure with a face of care.

“I am afraid of what might happen there”

He said. “So many listen what I say

And look and look. I will not run away.

I want my people here. You have my guns.”

But half a world away the mighty ones

Had spoken words like bullets in the dark

That wreak the rage of blindness on a mark

They can not know.
Then spoke the one who led

The soldiers: “Not a hair upon your head

Shall suffer any harm if you will go

To Robinson for just a day or so

And have a parley with the soldier chief.”

He spoke believing and he won belief,

So Crazy Horse went riding down the west;

===Chinese===

直到大洞盆地那场
整日的血战；他的女人们
怎样趁夜奔逃，把小马
和孩子也一并带了出来。
哦，苏族里多少颗心
听得怦怦急跳：听他
怎样打了逃、逃了打，
过班纳克，跨过比弗黑德
到亨利湖，被无情地追；
一会儿被那片可怕的荒寂
吞没——大神灵仍在那里
用太初的火和蒸汽的奇观
造着梦，像休耕的夜
新播下种的时候；一会儿
又在黄石河边现身，
仍不屈服，一步一步
缩短那一千英里
隔在一个民族的自由
和他们的需要之间的
异乡荒野！
哦，这故事里有养料，
喂得活枯萎的心，
叫它重新壮大！
还没有，还没有呢，
古老的族类还没有
从衰老的地球上绝迹！
据说，奥加拉拉村子
传遍这消息的那天，
疯马身上起了一个变化。
赢的那么多，输的只有命；
野牛正随秋南下，
约瑟夫正沿着去加尔
和坐牛的路打上来！
谁知道他做的什么梦？
说他心怀不善的话
很多：说不定哪天，
一件蓄谋的事就会在
苏族里点起一场
挡不住的奔溃——人的野火！
于是恐惧顺着电报线
来来回回饶舌。那边，
远得像早晨的地方，
大人物们听着——听见小角河
还在卡斯特劫数的风里咆哮。
半夜里有军队在幽暗里
移动，去锁那头兽；
可当白光破开东天，
奥加拉拉的村子已经空了，
疯马不见了！
尘土的秋天里，惊惶的骑兵
来回疾驰，直到整条
边境都为“那恐怖威胁着
每一条路”的传言发抖。
他们在斑尾的营里找到他，
一个孤影，一脸忧思。
“我怕那边会出事，”他说。
“听我说话、盯着我看的
人太多。我不会跑。
我要我的人民在你们这儿。
我的枪都在你们手里。”
可是在半个世界之外，
大人物们已经说出了
像黑暗里的子弹一样的话，
把盲目的怒火发泄到
他们无从知道的靶子上。
这时带兵的那位开了口：
“你头上一根头发
也不会受损伤，只要你
去罗宾逊待上那么一两天，
同军队的长官谈一谈。”
他说得恳切，也赢得了信任，
于是疯马骑马向西下行；

===Original===

And neither he nor any trooper guessed

What doom now made a rutted wagon road

The highway to a happier abode

Where all the dead are splendidly alive

And summer lingers and the bison thrive

Forever.
If the better hope be true,

There was a gate of glory yawning through

The sunset when the little cavalcade

Approached the fort.
The populous parade,

The straining hush that somehow wasn’t peace,

The bristling troops, the Indian police

Drawn up as for a battle! What was wrong?

What made them hustle Crazy Horse along

Among the gleaming bayonets and eyes?

There swept a look of quizzical surprise

Across his face. He struggled with the guard.

Their grips were steel; their eyes were cold and hard﻿—

Like bayonets.
There was a door flung wide.

The soldier chief would talk with him inside

And all be well at last!
The stifling, dim

Interior poured terror over him.

He blinked about﻿—and saw the iron bars.
O nevermore to neighbor with the stars

Or know the simple goodness of the sun!

Did some swift vision of a doom begun

Reveal the monstrous purpose of a lie﻿—

The desert island and the alien sky,

The long and lonely ebbing of a life?

The glimmer of a whipped-out butcher knife

Dismayed the shrinking squad, and once again

Men saw a face that many better men

Had died to see! Brown arms that once were kind,

A comrade’s arms, whipped round him from behind,

Went crimson with a gash and dropped aside.

“Don’t touch me! I am Crazy Horse!” he cried,

And, leaping doorward, charged upon the world

To meet the end. A frightened soldier hurled

His weight behind a jabbing belly-thrust,

And Crazy Horse plunged headlong in the dust,

A writhing heap. The momentary din

Of struggle ceased. The people, closing in,

Went ominously silent for a space,

And one could hear men breathing round the place

Where lay the mighty. Now he strove to rise,

The wide blind stare of anguish in his eyes,

And someone shouted “*Kill that devil quick!*”
A throaty murmur and a running click

Of gun-locks woke among the crowding Sioux,

And many a soldier whitened. Well they knew

What pent-up hate the moment might release

To drop upon the bungled farce of peace

A bloody curtain.
One began to talk;

His tongue was drunken and his face was chalk;

But when a half-breed shouted what he spoke

The crowd believed, so few had seen the stroke,

===Chinese===

他、还有任何一个骑兵，
都没猜到，此刻的厄运
正把一条车辙纵横的大车道
变成通往一个更幸福的
居所的大道：
那里的死者都活得
光辉灿烂，夏天永驻，
野牛永远繁盛。
如果更好的那个希望属实，
那么当这一小队人马
接近堡垒时，正有一道
荣耀之门在落日里敞开。
人挤人的操场，
那种不知怎么不像安宁的
绷紧的寂静，毛刺刺的
军队，还有印第安警察，
摆开了如临大战的阵势！
哪儿不对？是什么
推搡着疯马，在闪亮的
刺刀和眼睛中间疾走？
一丝讥诮的惊讶
扫过他的脸。他同卫兵
挣扎。卫兵的钳握是钢；
他们的眼睛又冷又硬——
像刺刀。有一扇门敞开着。
军队长官要在里面
同他谈，一切终将
好起来！窒息、昏暗的
室内向他倾泻恐怖。
他眨眼四顾——看见了铁栏。
哦，从此再不能与星星
为邻，再不能领受
太阳单纯的善意！
是不是一场刚开头的厄运
的迅疾幻象，向他揭穿了
一个谎言的可怕用心——
荒岛和异邦的天，
一生漫长而孤独的退潮？
一柄霍然抽出的屠刀
寒光一闪，惊退了缩着的
那一小队人，人们又一次
看见一张多少更好的人
拼死才见得着的脸！
一双曾经是善良的褐色臂膀、
一双同志的臂膀从背后
把他死死箍住，随即
一道刀口染红了它，垂落。
“别碰我！我是疯马！”他喊，
一跃向门，朝着世界
冲锋，去迎那个结局。
一个吓慌的士兵把全身
重量压进一记刺入腹部的
直刺，疯马一头栽进
尘土，成了一堆扭动。
挣扎的短促喧声停了。
围拢的人们一时
不祥地静默，听得见
那位伟人躺卧之地四周
人们的呼吸。这时他
挣扎着想坐起，眼里
是痛苦睁大的失神的凝视，
有人喊“*快杀了那个魔鬼！*”
一声喉音的低吼和一片
枪机连响在围挤的
苏族里醒来，许多士兵
脸都白了。他们太知道
那一刻会放出多少
憋久的恨，给这场
演砸的和平闹剧
落下一道血的幕。
有一个人开始讲话；
他的舌头醉着，脸是粉笔色；
可当一个混血儿喊出
他讲的话时，人群信了，
因为看见那一刀的人太少，

===Original===

Nor was there any bleeding of the wound.

It seemed the chief had falleasick and swooned;

Perhaps a little rest would make him strong!

And silently they watched him borne along,

A sagging bundle, dear and mighty yet,

Though from the sharp face, beaded with the sweat

Of agony, already peered the ghost.
They laid him in an office of the post,

And soldiers, forming in a hollow square,

Held back the people. Silence deepened there.

A little while it seemed the man was dead,

He lay so still. The west no longer bled;

Among the crowd the dusk began to creep.

Then suddenly, as startled out of sleep

By some old dream-remembered night alarm,

He strove to shout, half rose upon an arm

And glared about him in the lamp-lit place.
The flare across the ashes of his face

Went out. He spoke; and, leaning where he lay,

Men strained to gather what he strove to say,

So hard the panting labor of his words.

“I had my village and my pony herds

On Powder where the land was all my own.

I only wanted to be let alone.

I did not want to fight. The Gray Fox sent

His soldiers. We were poorer when they went;

Our babies died, for many lodges burned

And it was cold. We hoped again and turned

Our faces westward. It was just the same

Out yonder on the Rosebud. Gray Fox came.

The dust his soldiers made was high and long.

I fought him and I whipped him. Was it wrong

To drive him back? That country was my own.

I only wanted to be let alone.

I did not want to see my people die.

They say I murdered Long Hair and they lie.

His soldiers came to kill us and they died.”
He choked and shivered, staring hungry-eyed

As though to make the most of little light.

Then like a child that feels the clutching night

And cries the wilder, deeming it in vain,

He raised a voice made lyrical with pain

And terror of a thing about to be.

“*I want to see you, Father! Come to me!*

*I want to see you, Mother!*” O’er and o’er

His cry assailed the darkness at the door;

And from the gloom beyond the hollow square

Of soldiers, quavered voices of despair:

“*We can not come! They will not let us come!*”
But when at length the lyric voice was dumb

And Crazy Horse was nothing but a name,

There was a little withered woman came

Behind a bent old man. Their eyes were dim.

They sat beside the boy and fondled him,

Remembering the little names he knew

===Chinese===

伤口也没有流一滴血。
酋长像是病倒晕过去了；
歇一歇也许就会好些！
人们默默看他被抬走，
一捆松垂的形体，依旧
亲爱而伟大，只是那
蒙着痛汗的尖瘦脸上，
鬼魂已经探出头来。
他们把他放在哨站的
一间办公室里，士兵
结成一个空心的方阵，
拦住人群。那里的寂静
越来越深。有一会儿
那人像是死了，
他躺得那样静。西天
不再流血了；人群里
暮色开始爬动。
随后突然，像被旧梦里
记着的夜警惊出睡眠，
他挣扎着喊，用一条胳膊
半撑起身，在灯亮的
屋子里瞪视四周。
他脸上灰烬间的一道
火光熄了。他说话了；
人们俯向他躺着的地方，
竭力收拢他拼命想说的话，
他的字喘得那样苦。
“我在波德河上有我的村子、
我的马群，那片土地
全是我自己的。
我只想让人家别来打扰。
我不想打仗。灰狐派来
他的兵。他们走后我们更穷了；
我们的婴儿死了，因为
好多帐篷烧了，天又冷。
我们又抱了指望，把脸
转向西边。玫瑰蕾河那边
一模一样。灰狐来了。
他的兵扬起的尘土又高又长。
我打了他，我打败了他。
把他赶回去难道错了吗？
那片乡野是我自己的。
我只想让人家别来打扰。
我不想看着我的人民死。
他们说我杀了长毛，
他们撒谎。他的兵来杀我们，
他们就死了。”

===Original===

Before the great dream took him and he grew

To be so mighty. And the woman pressed

A hand that men had feared against her breast

And swayed and sang a little sleepy song.

Out yonder in the village all night long

There was a sound of mourning in the dark.

And when the morning heard the meadowlark,

The last great Sioux rode silently away.

Before the pony-drag on which he lay

An old man tottered. Bowed above the bier,

A little wrinkled woman kept the rear

With not a sound and nothing in her eyes.
Who knows the crumbling summit where he lies

Alone among the badlands? Kiotes prowl

About it, and the voices of the owl

Assume the daylong sorrow of the crows,

These many grasses and these many snows.

===Chinese===

他哽住，打颤，饥饿般
瞪着眼，像要把这点微光
用尽。随后，像一个
摸到黑夜攫住的孩子，
觉得哭也无益、反而
哭得更野，他扬起一副
被疼痛和一件将至之事的
恐怖谱成歌的嗓音。
“*我要见你，父亲！到我这儿来！*
*我要见你，母亲！*”一遍
又一遍，他的呼喊扑打
门上的黑暗；空心方阵
之外的幽暗里，颤着
绝望的嗓音回答：
“*我们来不了！他们不让我们来！*”
可当那歌喉终于喑哑、
疯马只剩一个名字时，
来了一个小小的干瘪
妇人，跟着一个伛偻的
老人。他们的眼睛都花了。
他们坐在孩子身边，
摩挲着他，念着大梦
攫住他、把他养成
这般伟大之前，他还
懂的那些小名。妇人把
一只人人畏惧过的手
按在胸前，摇着，哼起
一支小小的催眠曲。
那边村子里，整夜
黑暗里都是哀声。
当早晨听见草地鹨歌唱，
最后一位伟大的苏族人
默默地上路了。
他躺着的马拖前头，
一个老人踉跄走着。
灵床的上方，一个
皱纹满脸的小妇人
殿后，没有一点声音，
眼里没有一点东西。
谁知道他孤零零躺在
劣地里哪一座崩毁的
山顶？郊狼在四周
逡巡，鸮的声音接过
乌鸦整日的哀伤，
一年又一年，一雪又一雪。

## The Song of Three Friends / 三友之歌
===Original===



===Chinese===

三友之歌

### To Hilda / 给希尔达

### To Hilda /

### To Hilda /

### To Hilda /

### To Hilda /

### To Hilda /

### To Hilda /

### To Hilda /

### To Hilda /
